\title{\textbf{GRUT}\\[0.3em]
\Large Geometric Relational Unified Topology Theory\\[0.7em]
\large Derivations from the seed $h=r$, the $C_3$ triangle and the force axiom\\[0.2em]
\normalsize Version 1.23.2}
\author{Jose Mancilla}
\date{18 October 2026}

\begin{document}
\maketitle

\begin{abstract}
\noindent
GRUT derives gravity, horizon thermodynamics, cosmology and the lepton sector from the speed of light and three geometric primitives:
the seed $h=r$ (the null cone), three equivalent generators (the equilateral $C_3$ triangle, total deficit $\pi$, $\pi/3$ each), and the
force axiom $FG=c^4$, whose quarter $c^4/4G$ is the force carried by one cone. Every equation is derived, every constant is calibrated once
in a ledger, and every result carries a status tag, so that what the theory adds is visible line by line.

The axiom turns length into mass, $\MH=Fr/2c^2$: the horizon is the Schwarzschild radius of its own contents, which is flatness. From it
follow $G=c^4/F$, $\hbar=2\pi Fr^2/Nc$, the entropy $S=N/2$, the temperature $k_BT=\hbar c/2\pi r$ and the exact exchange law $\EH=k_BT\,S$.
Every horizon quantity $X\propto r^k$ flows at $\dot X/X=k(1+q)H$, and the horizon's energy grows at $3(1+\weff)\,c^5/4G$: four, three,
two, one and zero times the maximum power through radiation, matter, strings ($q=0$), walls ($\Omega_m=\tfrac13$, $q=-\tfrac12$) and vacuum.
The present is a resonance: the horizon's $C_3$ mode reaches the final expansion rate when $\Omega_\Lambda=27/4\pi^2=0.684$ (Planck
$0.685\pm0.007$). With the derived baryon ratio and the derived neutrino masses, a Boltzmann fit to the acoustic angle gives
$H_0=67.35$, $\omega_b=0.02248$, $\omega_c=0.12022$ and $\Omega_m=0.316$, within $1\sigma$ of Planck's densities. Dark energy is the
Planck density shared over the final horizon's cells, $\rho_\Lambda=\tfrac34\rho_P/N_\infty=1.13\times10^{-123}\rho_P$.

The horizon's generating arcs spin at $\omega=H_\infty$ with rims at light speed. The spin fixes $\Lambda=3\omega^2/c^2$, adds $8\pi\lP^2$ of
area per quantum to any horizon, predicts the CMB's birefringence, $\beta=(2\alpha/\pi)\,\mathrm{artanh}(3\sqrt3/2\pi)=0.314^\circ$ (measured
$0.277^\circ\pm0.057^\circ$), and the galactic acceleration scale, $a_0=(2/3\pi)\,c^2\sqrt{\Lambda/3}=(\sqrt3/\pi^2)\,cH_0=1.148\times10^{-10}$
m\,s$^{-2}$. Across 153 SPARC rotation curves the threshold law fits with tail orders $1.62\le n\le1.91$, the predicted scale $1.0\sigma$ from
the likelihood value. The extra gravity is carried by the universe's rotation, a phase held at its rate that dilutes as cold dust; a system
holds it only where its escape speed exceeds the motion of the field around it, so the Solar System and wide binaries stay Newtonian.

The dark sector is one geometry: dark energy is spacetime unexcited, what is called dark matter is the same spacetime excited, and the
whole has $w=-1+\Theta$. What lensing infers as dark matter is interpreted by GRUT as a dilation $\mathcal S=1/\cos_n\theta$, $\tan\theta=a_0/g$: one at the
centre, growing toward the edge and varying with capture and relaxation. In the Bullet Cluster, lensing-inferred mass peaks are offset from the
X-ray-bright gas and lie closer to the galaxies; this is consistent with a collisionless gravitating component under standard lensing/GR
interpretations, but does not by itself identify that component as GRUT's phase sector or establish a cosmological reset. On the unbound web the horizon dilates clocks and rulers together from the onset of
acceleration, above $68$ Mpc, so matter feels it and light does not: $S_8$, cosmic shear and CMB lensing keep the background's values,
the late Sachs--Wolfe signal falls, and the bulk flows Cosmicflows-4 measures (below $0.03\%$ in $\Lambda$CDM) lie at the $78$th--$90$th
percentiles. The framework has one expansion rate, $67.35$; the dilation does not move it, and the supernova Hubble diagram rules out a
local void, so its prediction is that distance ladders calibrated alike converge on $67.3$ (the 2026 network lies $6.1\sigma$ away, the JWST
red-giant ladder $1.5\sigma$).

The present is also fixed in Planck cells by the electric coupling, $r=8\pi\lP\,e^{1/\alpha+3\pi\alpha/2}$: with no cosmological input it
gives $H_0=67.377$, $0.4\sigma$ from the CMB fit, and $\Lambda\lP^2=(81/256\pi^4)\,e^{-2/\alpha-3\pi\alpha}$. The four interactions are then four
features of one shape---the cone, the generators' phase, the conjugate pair and the triangle---written as one action with one gauge group,
$(SU(3)\times SU(2)\times U(1))/\mathbb Z_6$, whose content fixes the running ($b_3=-7$). The horizon and the nucleon are the transmutations
of the electric and the strong coupling from one Planck cell, gravity's weakness at the nucleon is $e^{-2/3\alpha}$, and there is no
unification scale and so no gauge-mediated proton decay.

In generation space the seed cone is Koide's relation, with the lepton cone at the axiom's vacuum: $m_\tau=1776.969$ MeV,
$\sum m_\nu=59.34$ meV (normal) or $102.0$ meV (inverted), $\sin^2\theta_{12}=0.3180$. The fine-structure constant follows from
$S^1(2\pi)\times S^3(1)$, $1/\alpha=4\pi^3+\pi^2+\pi-\tfrac{\alpha}{24}(1+e^{-2\pi})=137.035\,999\,152$, $1.2\sigma$ from CODATA 2022. The
primordial tilt measures the reset carrier's mass, with negligible running and zero tensor tilt; the 21-cm onset of cosmic dawn is set by
the CMB's twist, $42.7^{+5.6}_{-6.0}$ MHz; and the Schwarzschild radii of everything inside the horizon add up to it, $\Omega_{\rm tot}=\chi/2=1$.
Auxoviscausality identifies the phase-flow integral of proper-volume expansion as retained geometric strain, with its local Ricci-load/shear response fixed by the existing Raychaudhuri/Einstein system. At the conformal junction, the raw strain diverges rather than matching continuously; the finite conformal-volume remainder matches under the stated smooth-metric and common-flow-tube assumptions. This does not preserve the entire prior FLRW expansion integral as a separate state. Every prediction is listed with its dependencies and falsification condition (Appendices~\ref{app:register} and~\ref{app:deps}).
\end{abstract}

\section*{What this work contributes}
This framework is original work. Its contributions are:
\begin{enumerate}\itemsep2pt
\item The four aspects of the geometry kept distinct: reality, the spin; actuality, the swept sphere; occurrence, the
crossings; representation, the boundary. The present is the occurrence at which actuality falls into step with reality
(the opening principle).
\item The two-C/S geometry of the horizon and its $C_3$ structure: one closed shape that organizes the cosmos, galaxies
and the nucleon (Sections~\ref{sec:rotation} and~\ref{sec:closure}).
\item The spinning-arc principle, $\omega R=c$: the arcs that sweep each sphere spin at a fixed rate with rims at light speed;
one spin sets the final horizon, the cosmological constant and the present, is measured by the CMB's birefringence, and
gives each particle its Compton radius (\S36.9). For the final horizon it is derived: its thermal geometry is the spinning
arcs, with the observer on the rim; the charge of the spin is the horizon's angular momentum and its entropy over $2\pi$; and
the quantum of the spin is the area step $8\pi\lP^2$ of every horizon (\S36.10--36.12).
\item The threefold count as a beat: the fold, turning twice for each turn of the arc, beats against the conjugate arc three
times per turn and keeps exactly $\mathbb Z_3$, as the conjugate pair keeps $\mathbb Z_2$ (\S1.11).
\item The birefringence angle in closed form, $\beta=(2\alpha/\pi)\,\mathrm{artanh}(3\sqrt3/2\pi)$, and the acceleration scale from
the cosmological constant, $a_0=(2/3\pi)\,c^2\sqrt{\Lambda/3}$ (\S36.13).
\item The quantum of the universe's rotation as the quantum of the horizon's spin, $\Omega_0=jH_\infty$ with $j=1$ measured (\S36.14).
\item The dark ratio from the resonance, $2.580$, in place of an identification of densities with powers (\S25.11).
\item Koide's relation as the vacuum of the axiom's potential, seen from the generators under the seed's right angle (\S42.8).
\item First starlight counted in cycles: the 21-cm onset fixed by the CMB's birefringence,
$\nu_\star/\nu_{21}=[\sqrt{\Omega_m/\Omega_\Lambda}\,\sinh(\pi\beta/2)]^{2/3}$, the fine-structure constant, the clock and the Hubble constant
cancelling (\S48.8).
\item The dark sector as one geometry: dark energy as spacetime unexcited and the cosmic share as spacetime excited---the spin's quanta
held by the horizon and carried by the rotation---with the equation of state $w=-1+\Theta$ of the whole, and what lensing infers as dark matter
as the dilation $\mathcal S=1/\cos_n\theta$, $\tan\theta=a_0/g$; the dilation field as the index of the optical metric, the switch as the capture
fraction, and the middle scales as geometric means of the outer ones; the background unmoved by the dilation, and the web dilated as a
clock is slowed---from the onset of acceleration, above $68$ Mpc, on clocks and not on light---carrying the measured bulk flows with $S_8$
unchanged (\S53).
\item The closure law in $3{+}1$ dimensions: energy as defect on the cosmological horizon, $\sum r_s=(\chi/2)r$ and
$\Omega_{\rm tot}=\chi/2$, so that flatness is topology (\S50.1).
\item The scale ladder as the horizon's symmetric cuts, ending at one Planck cell whose energy is the framework's energy
per bit (\S50.2).
\item The MOND acceleration from the horizon's $C_3$ eigenvalue, $a_0=(\sqrt3/\pi^2)\,cH_0$ (\S22).
\item Dark matter as the internal rotation of the horizon's phase: one rotation that sets $a_0$, carries the cosmic cold
share and needs no particle (\S23).
\item The birefringence--acceleration relation $\beta=\tfrac92\alpha\,a_0t_0/c$: galaxy rotation curves predict the rotation of
the CMB's polarization (\S49.13).
\item The derived baryon ratio $\Omega_b/\Omega_c=0.18697$, which passes the full CMB spectrum (\S25.9).
\item The footprint balance, which places the two-thirds point with its coefficient fixed and reduces the
Eddington--Weinberg coincidence to the framework's scale data (\S50.8).
\item The nucleon as the horizon's closed $C_3$ at the strong scale, with integer charge from the
$\mathbb Z_2\times\mathbb Z_3$ closure (\S50.10).
\item The electroweak-volume reading of $\alpha$, the lepton cone with $m_\tau$, and the closure of $N_c=3$ by $C_3$, each
labelled derived or identified in the text.
\item Auxoviscausality: logarithmic proper-volume strain follows the phase-flow integral and its Ricci-load/shear response follows from Raychaudhuri/Einstein. At the conformal junction the raw strain is not continuous; the regularized conformal-volume remainder matches under explicit geometric regularity assumptions, without adding a field or claiming microscopic irreversibility.
\end{enumerate}
Earlier results are cited where this work meets them. Each citation marks a point of agreement with established
physics, not a source of the framework.

\newpage
\section*{The opening principle: reality, actuality, occurrence and representation}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{The opening principle: reality, actuality, occurrence and representation}
The framework distinguishes four aspects of one geometry and keeps them apart throughout.

\textbf{Reality} is the spin: the C-arcs that generate the horizon and their fixed rate $\omega$, set once and unchanged,
since nothing acts on them (\S36.9). What is real does not change.

\textbf{Actuality} is the swept sphere: what the spin has made actual so far, the horizon at this moment, its radius and
its cells. Reality is the rate; actuality is how much of the sphere the rate has swept. Actuality grows toward the final
horizon $R_\infty=c/\omega$, where the arcs' rims reach light speed.

\textbf{Occurrence} is a crossing. Something happens only where lines or rates cross: the arcs crossing at the three $C_3$
nodes, which are the defects; the wobble falling into step with the spin, which is the present (\S36.7); the reset, where
one aeon's future meets the next one's past; a horizon, where time and radius exchange; and each activation of the
threshold law (\S22). Between crossings the motion continues and nothing occurs.

\textbf{Representation} is the boundary. Every energy inside the horizon appears on it as a defect, its share of the
horizon's cells (\S50.2), so the sphere carries an image of everything it contains: the universe represents itself on its
own surface. Measurements read that image; the CMB's birefringence reads the spin (\S36.9). The framework's equations, and
the picture of the arcs itself, are representations of the same reality.

Physics has often merged these. The block universe of relativity treats reality as if it were all actuality, as though
the whole sphere were already swept; quantum theory blurs representation, the wavefunction, with reality. Kept apart,
they define the present. The wobble belongs to actuality, its frequency falling as the swept sphere grows; the spin
belongs to reality, and is fixed. \emph{The present is the occurrence at which actuality falls into step with reality.}

\section*{0\quad Method, anchors and calibration}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{0\quad Method, anchors and calibration}

\subsection*{0.1\quad Equations are derived; values are calibrated}
The form of each law is derived and its constants are measured once: each observation
calibrates one constant (the bootstrap of \S2.7), and every number downstream is a prediction.

\begin{theorem}[Nothing dimensionful follows from $c$ alone]\label{thm:c-alone}
\sD\ Any quantity computed from $c$ and dimensionless numbers has dimension
$(\mathrm{L}\,\mathrm{T}^{-1})^k$, so no length, time or mass follows from $c$ alone;
three dimensionful anchors are conventions and carry no predictive content.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Dimensions multiply, transcendental functions need dimensionless arguments and summed terms
share one dimension, so anything built from $c$ is $c^k$ times a pure number, of dimension
$\mathrm L^k\mathrm T^{-k}$: a length needs $k=1$ and $k=0$, a time $k=0$ and $k=-1$, and no
$k$ gives mass. L, T and M thus need three independent anchors, whose values a change of units
rescales at will; only their dimensionless combinations are predictive.
\end{proof}

\subsection*{0.2\quad Anchors and dimensional closure}
Four anchors carry the dimensions: $c$ converts time to length (the seed $h=r$); $r$, the
seed's scale, flows with the expansion ($r_0=c/H_0$ today); $F$, the force of the axiom
$FG=c^4$, converts length to mass; $\lP$, the horizon's cell size, converts area to count. Hence
\begin{equation}
[\text{length}]=r,\quad [\text{time}]=\frac rc,\quad [\text{mass}]=\frac{Fr}{c^2},\quad
[\text{energy}]=Fr,\quad [\text{action}]=\frac{Fr^2}{c},\quad [\text{count}]=\frac{r^2}{\lP^2}.
\label{eq:units}
\end{equation}
$c$, $r$, $F$ fix the units; $\lP$ enters only through the pure number $N=2\pi r^2/\lP^2$,
the horizon cell count (\S7), so $\hbar=(2\pi/N)\,Fr^2/c$ and $G=c^4/F$. Every quantity not
calibrated in \S0.3 is a geometric constant---built from $\pi$, $\sqrt2$, $\sqrt3$, the
deficits and the $C_3$ eigenvalues---times a monomial in~\eqref{eq:units}, possibly times a
power of $N$.

\subsection*{0.4\quad The three primitives and the identifications}
\begin{postulate}[Seed]\label{post:seed}
Light emitted at an event reaches, after a time $t$, every point at distance $ct$, in every
direction and for every inertial observer: with height measured as light-travel time,
$h\equiv ct=r$, i.e.\ $ds^2=0$ (\S1.10).
\end{postulate}
\begin{postulate}[Generators]\label{post:c3}
Three equivalent generators generate the structure; their cyclic symmetry $C_3$ prefers
none, and its minimal realization is the equilateral triangle (\S1.1--1.4).
\end{postulate}
\begin{postulate}[Force]\label{post:force}
$FG=c^4$, with $F$ the force of the whole horizon sphere; one light cone carries $F/4$ (\S8).
\end{postulate}
Identifications, which read a geometric quantity as a physical one, carry the risk:
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}llp{11.7cm}@{}}
\toprule
I1 & \S8.2 & the factor $4$ of the maximum force is the four-cone closure $V_s=4V_c$ \\

\section{The Geometry}\label{sec:geometry}

\noindent\textbf{Geometric origin.} The cosmic horizon is generated by two conjugate C-shaped arcs spinning about
perpendicular axes and crossing as they sweep the sphere. Their threefold wobble, the beat of the fold against the
counter-turning conjugate arc (\S1.11), creates three conical defects at equal azimuthal separations, each of deficit
$\pi/3$, closing into the $C_3$ cycle (the equal-frequency figure first proposed has no nodes and is withdrawn, \S52.5).
The spin is physical: its rims move at $c$, and its fixed rate, derived from the final horizon's thermal geometry, sets the
final horizon, the cosmological constant and the present (\S36.9--36.10).

\subsection*{1.1--1.4\quad Point, conjugate pair, third point, triangle}
A point separates into the conjugate pair $p_\pm$; a third point off their line spans a plane, the smallest structure
with an area. No vertex is preferred (as for the generators, \S4.2), so the generating triangle is equilateral, with
angles $\pi/3$ and rotation group $C_3$.

\subsection*{1.5\quad The recursion}
Each vertex generates its own pair and third point: after $d$ levels there are $N_d=\sum_{j=0}^{d}3^j=(3^{d+1}-1)/2=1$,
$4$, $13$, $40$, $121,\dots$ units, the same triangle at every scale.

\subsection*{1.6\quad The surface}
A triangle rotated about its altitude sweeps a right circular cone, apex at the upper vertex. The right isosceles
triangle gives $h=r$ (apex $90^\circ$), the light cone (\S1.10); the equilateral one gives $h=\sqrt3\,r$ (apex $60^\circ$),
the defect cone (\S4.4).

\subsection*{1.7\quad The defect}
Unrolled, the lateral surface is a sector of radius the slant height $l$ and arc $2\pi r$. The missing angle is the
deficit,
\begin{equation}
\delta=2\pi\Big(1-\frac rl\Big),
\label{eq:deficit}
\end{equation}
the rotation of a vector parallel-transported once around the apex: the cone's curvature, concentrated there.

\subsection*{1.8--1.9\quad The fold and the 3-sphere}
The solid cone is a 3-ball. The conjugate pair's two balls, glued along their boundary, close into the 3-sphere; a single
ball folded onto itself, opposite boundary points identified, is the rotation group (the rotation by $\psi\le\pi$ about
$\hat n$ is the point $\psi\hat n$, and those by $\pi$ about $\pm\hat n$ coincide):
\begin{equation}
B^3_+\cup_{S^2}B^3_-\;\cong\;S^3\;\cong\;SU(2),\qquad
B^3\big/(x\sim-x\ \text{on}\ \partial B^3)\;\cong\;\mathbb{RP}^3\;\cong\;SO(3).
\label{eq:S3}
\end{equation}
The map $S^3\to\mathbb{RP}^3$ is two-to-one: the pair double-covers the fold. This is the geometry of spin: a $2\pi$
rotation closes in $SO(3)$ but not in $SU(2)$, so a spin-$\tfrac12$ state needs $4\pi$. $S^3$ is finite (volume
$2\pi^2R^3$), with no centre and no boundary.

\subsection*{1.10\quad The seed}
With height measured in light-travel time, $h\equiv ct$, light emitted at the apex event reaches every point at distance
$ct$, in every direction and for every inertial observer: the seed is the null condition,
\begin{equation}
r=ct=h\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad ds^2=-c^2dt^2+dx^2+dy^2+dz^2=0 .
\label{eq:null}
\end{equation}
The seed and the constancy of $c$ are one statement: $c$ converts the height, a time, into a length. In an expanding
universe the seed holds exactly in conformal time $\eta=\int dt/a$: with $ds^2=a^2(-c^2d\eta^2+d\chi^2)$, light obeys
$\chi=c\eta$ (\S26.2).

\subsection*{1.11\quad The threefold beat}
\sD\ Per turn of the arc $p_+$, the conjugate $p_-$ turns once in the opposite sense and the fold twice, since the pair
double-covers it (\S1.8): phases $e^{i\theta}$, $e^{-i\theta}$, $e^{2i\theta}$. Two superposed motions of counts $n_1$,
$n_2$ stay in step under $\theta\to\theta+\psi$ only if $e^{i(n_1-n_2)\psi}=1$, so they keep $\mathbb Z_{|n_1-n_2|}$:
\begin{equation}
e^{i\theta}+e^{-i\theta}\ (\text{pair}):\ \mathbb Z_2,\ \text{nodes }\pi\text{ apart};\qquad
e^{2i\theta}+e^{-i\theta}\ (\text{fold against arc}):\ \mathbb Z_3,\ \text{nodes }\tfrac{2\pi}3\text{ apart},
\label{eq:beat}
\end{equation}
the latter the three-petalled rose $2\cos(3\theta/2)\,e^{i\theta/2}$. For the complex scalar $\Phi=\phi\,e^{i\theta}$ of
the action,
\begin{equation}
|\Phi^2+\Phi^*|^2=\phi^4+\phi^2+2\phi^3\cos3\theta ,
\label{eq:beat-intensity}
\end{equation}
whose interference term $2\,\mathrm{Re}\,\Phi^3$ is the $\mathbb Z_3$ harmonic leading the phase potential of \S45.1; the
pair alone, $|\Phi+\Phi^*|^2=2\phi^2(1+\cos2\theta)$, gives the $\mathbb Z_2$ harmonic, the elementary cut of the deficit
$\pi$ (\S50.2) and the $\pi$ crossing of the coil's lobes (\S44.5). The two beats are the $\mathbb Z_2$ and $\mathbb Z_3$
of the horizon's closure (\S50.10), together $\mathbb Z_6$.

\sI\ The threefold wobble is the second beat, realizing Postulate~\ref{post:c3} without replacing it: that its three nodes
are the horizon's three defects is the reading of the generators as phases (\S15.3).

\section{The Method}\label{sec:method}

\subsection*{2.1--2.7\quad Equation, algebra, bootstrap}
An equation relates an object's invariant to $r$ and $\pi$; calculus enters when a quantity changes with scale
($d\EH/dr=F_{\rm geom}$, $dN/dr=4\pi r/\lP^2$), and the algebra is that of the axiom (\S8) and the cell count (\S7). An
observation calibrates one anchor (\S0.3), which predicts the next quantity, checked against a different observation; no
calibrating value counts as a test, and every value downstream of the calibrations is one.

\subsection*{2.8\quad Dynamic coupling}
\sD\ Every horizon quantity is a geometric constant times a power of $r$, $X\propto r^k$: $k=1$ for $r$, $\MH$, $\EH$;
$k=2$ for $N$, $\SGH$, $L_{\rm H}$; $k=-1$ for $\TGH$, $a_c$, $a_0$, $H$. With $r=c/H$, the deceleration parameter
$q=-\ddot aa/\dot a^2$ and $\dot H=-(1+q)H^2$, one master rate drives them all:
\begin{equation}
\dot r=c\,(1+q),\qquad \frac{\dot X}{X}=k\,(1+q)\,H .
\label{eq:master}
\end{equation}
The anchors $c$, $F$, $\lP$ do not flow, so $G=c^4/F$ and $\hbar$ are constant, as lunar laser ranging requires
($|\dot G/G|\lesssim10^{-13}\,\mathrm{yr^{-1}}$ \cite{Hofmann2018}). The horizon energy $\EH=Fr/2$ grows at
\begin{equation}
\dot E_{\rm H}=\tfrac12F\dot r=2(1+q)\,P_{\max},\qquad P_{\max}=F_{\rm cone}\,c=\frac{c^5}{4G},
\label{eq:power}
\end{equation}
today $0.95\,\Pmax$ ($q_0=-0.526$, \S25.11). As $1+q=\tfrac32(1+\weff)$, with $\weff$ the total equation of state,
$\dot\EH=\Pmax$ exactly at $\weff=-\tfrac23$, $\Omega_m=\tfrac13$: the maximum-power epoch (\S26.5). The cell count grows,
$\dot N=2(1+q)HN\ge0$ for $q\ge-1$ (\S26.6).

\section{The Seed and the Cone}\label{sec:cone}

\subsection*{3.1--3.11\quad Measures of the seed cone}
For $h=r$: apex angle $90^\circ$, slant $l=\sqrt2\,r$, base area $A_b=\pi r^2$, total area $A_c=(1+\sqrt2)\pi r^2$, volume
$V_c=\tfrac13\pi r^3$, apex solid angle $2\pi(1-h/l)=(2-\sqrt2)\pi=1.8403$ sr. Against the sphere of the same radius,
$V_s/V_c=A_s/A_b=4$.

\subsection*{3.12\quad Why four cones fill the sphere}
\sD\ At height $z\in[0,r]$ above the apex, the slices of the cylinder and hemisphere of radius $r$ and of the seed cone
obey (cylinder $-$ cone $=$ hemisphere)
\begin{equation}
\pi r^2-\pi z^2=\pi(r^2-z^2) .
\label{eq:cavalieri}
\end{equation}
A cone of height $h$ has the slice $\pi z^2r^2/h^2$, so the identity holds at every $z$ only for $h=r$. Integrated slice by
slice (Cavalieri), it gives Archimedes' theorem,
\begin{equation}
V_c:V_{\rm hemi}:V_{\rm cyl}=1:2:3,\qquad V_s=2V_{\rm hemi}=4V_c ,
\label{eq:archimedes}
\end{equation}
and $h=r$ is the unique cone for which it holds slice by slice: the seed closes the sphere by volume.

\section{The Defect and the Fold}\label{sec:defect}

\subsection*{4.1--4.4\quad Three equal generators and the value of the deficit}
\sD\ The three vertices generate three defects $\delta_j$, equal because no vertex is preferred. They meet at the apex as
three equilateral faces of angle $\pi/3$, so the total angle there is $\pi$ and
\begin{equation}
\dtot=3\delta_j=2\pi-\pi=\pi,\qquad \delta_j=\frac{\pi}{3}:
\label{eq:dtot}
\end{equation}
each deficit is the triangle's interior angle, the total its angle sum. Three results confirm the value. (i) By
\eqref{eq:deficit}, $\delta=\pi$ requires $l=2r$, i.e.\ $h=\sqrt3\,r$, the cone of the equilateral triangle
(\S1.6); it unrolls to a half-disk and is intrinsically the three-face apex. (ii) Four such
apexes, the vertices of the regular tetrahedron, close a sphere: by Descartes' theorem (polyhedral Gauss--Bonnet),
\begin{equation}
4\,\dtot=4\pi=\oint_{S^2}K\,dA .
\label{eq:descartes}
\end{equation}
The light cone closes the sphere by volume (\S3.12), the $C_3$ cone by curvature: both closures take four cones.

(iii) \sD\ (I2) With height as light-travel time, the $h=\sqrt3\,r$ cone is a front moving at $c/\sqrt3$, the sound speed
of radiation, whose stress is traceless:
\begin{equation}
T^\mu{}_\mu=-\rho c^2+3p=0\;\Rightarrow\;p=\tfrac13\rho c^2\;\Rightarrow\;c_s=\frac{c}{\sqrt3}.
\label{eq:soundcone}
\end{equation}
The epochs the cone describes, the radiation era and the conformally invariant reset boundary, are traceless, so the
defect cone is the early universe's sound cone, imprinted as the CMB's acoustic scale (\S39.1). The seed cone, of deficit
$(2-\sqrt2)\pi$ and front speed $c$, is causal, not defect-carrying.

\subsection*{4.5--4.6\quad The fold; the 3-sphere and its radius}
The pair closes into $S^3\cong SU(2)$, the fold into $\mathbb{RP}^3\cong SO(3)$~\eqref{eq:S3}. An $S^3$
spatial section of radius $R$ enters the Friedmann equation as $H_0^2=\tfrac{8\pi G}{3}\rho-c^2/R^2$, so
\begin{equation}
\Omega_{\rm tot}=1+\Big(\frac rR\Big)^2 .
\label{eq:S3radius}
\end{equation}
\sP\ The horizon condition makes the section flat, $R\to\infty$ (\S12.2, \S50.7); a closed section survives only as a
correction beyond it, bounded by $\Omega_K=0.0007\pm0.0019$ \cite{Planck2018} to $R>18\,r$ ($95\%$) and tested by surveys
reaching $|\Omega_K|\sim10^{-3}$. An $S^3$ of radius $r$ ($\Omega_{\rm tot}=2$) is the group manifold $SU(2)$ of \S1.8,
not space.

\section{The Cycle \texorpdfstring{$C_3$}{C3}}\label{sec:C3}
\sD\ The generators define the cycle $1\to2\to3\to1$ on the base circle, with arcs $\ell=2\pi r/3$. The discrete Laplacian
$(\Delta f)_n=\ell^{-2}(2f_n-f_{n+1}-f_{n-1})$ has on $f_n=e^{2\pi imn/3}$ the eigenvalues
\begin{equation}
\lambda_m=\frac{2}{\ell^2}\Big[1-\cos\frac{2\pi m}{3}\Big]:\qquad
\lambda_0=0,\qquad \lambda_1=\frac{3}{\ell^2}=\frac{27}{4\pi^2r^2}.
\label{eq:lambda}
\end{equation}
On $n$ nodes $\lambda_1=\frac{n^2}{2\pi^2r^2}\big[1-\cos\frac{2\pi}{n}\big]\to1/r^2$, the continuum value; $C_3$, the
coarsest closed cycle, has $\lambda_1r^2=27/4\pi^2=0.6839$, the present $\Omega_\Lambda$ at the resonance (\S25.11). Light on
the cycle has $\omega_1=c\sqrt{\lambda_1}=3\sqrt3\,c/2\pi r$ and period $\tau_1=2\pi/\omega_1$.

\textbf{Definition.} The critical acceleration brings a body from rest to $c$ in one period (the centripetal $c\,\omega_1$
is $2\pi$ larger):
\begin{equation}
a_c=\frac{c}{\tau_1}=\frac{3\sqrt3}{4\pi^2}\,\frac{c^2}{r}=k_3\,cH_0,\qquad k_3=0.131620 ,
\label{eq:ac}
\end{equation}
($\kappa$ is the surface gravity, \S8.2); at $H_0=67.35$, $a_c=8.612\times10^{-11}\ \mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}=\tfrac34a_0$
(\S22).

\section{Probability and the Born Rule}\label{sec:born}

\subsection*{6.1--6.3\quad Effective solid angle and $P=\tfrac12$}
\sD\ A line defect through the observer removes its deficit from the azimuth:
\begin{equation}
\Omega_{\rm eff}=\int_0^\pi\sin\theta\,d\theta\int_0^{2\pi-\dtot}d\phi=4\pi-2\dtot=2\pi,\qquad
P=\frac{\Omega_{\rm eff}}{4\pi}=\frac12 .
\label{eq:Omegaeff}
\end{equation}
The seed gives it independently: $P=\cos^245^\circ$, with $45^\circ$ its half-apex angle, is the probability that a system
prepared along one axis passes a test at $45^\circ$ (Malus's law).

\subsection*{6.4--6.5\quad The Born rule; conjugate points}
\sS\ The conjugate pair with equal weights, $|\psi\rangle=(|{+}\rangle+e^{i\varphi}|{-}\rangle)/\sqrt2$, has amplitudes
$\cos45^\circ$ and probabilities $P_\pm=\tfrac12$, $P_++P_-=1$. The general rule is Gleason's theorem
\cite{Gleason1957}: in a Hilbert space of dimension three or more, every probability assignment additive over orthogonal
outcomes is $P=\mathrm{Tr}(\rho\,\Pi)$ ($\rho$ the density operator, $\Pi$ the outcome's projector).

\sI\ The three generators supply exactly that minimal dimension, three.

\section{Effective Area and Cell Count}\label{sec:cells}
\sD\ The effective horizon area is $r^2\Omega_{\rm eff}=2\pi r^2$; in cells of the Planck area $\lP^2=\hbar G/c^3$ the
horizon carries
\begin{equation}
N=\frac{2\pi r^2}{\lP^2}=\frac{2\pi c^5}{\hbar GH_0^2}=4.538\times10^{122}\quad(H_0=67.35).
\label{eq:N}
\end{equation}
The same number measures the vacuum: against the Planck density $c^5/\hbar G^2$,
\begin{equation}
\frac{\rho_c}{\rho_{\rm P}}=\frac{3H_0^2}{8\pi G}\cdot\frac{\hbar G^2}{c^5}=\frac{3}{4N}=1.65\times10^{-123},
\label{eq:ccproblem}
\end{equation}
so the famous ratio is a count of horizon cells: the holographic reading of the vacuum-energy problem \cite{CKN1999}, with
its coefficient, $3/4$, fixed exactly.

\section{The Axiom}\label{sec:axiom}

\subsection*{8.1\quad The force axiom}
\begin{equation}
F\,G=c^4,\qquad F=\frac{c^4}{G}=1.2103\times10^{44}\ \mathrm N ,
\label{eq:axiom}
\end{equation}
the force of the whole universe. It is the Planck force: two Planck masses one cell apart attract with
$Gm_{\rm P}^2/\lP^2=c^4/G$.

\subsection*{8.2\quad The factor of four: one cone}
\sS\ A mass $M$ has horizon radius $r_s=2GM/c^2$ and surface gravity $\kappa=c^4/4GM$; the force on it at its own horizon,
$F_{\rm cone}=M\kappa=Mc^2/2r_s=c^4/4G=F/4=3.0256\times10^{43}$ N, is the same for every $M$: the maximum force of a
horizon \cite{Gibbons2002,Schiller2005}.

\sD\ (I1) The four is the closure of \S3.12: a uniformly filled sphere gives each of its four cones a quarter of its mass,
$M_{\rm cone}=\rho\,\pi r^3/3=\MH/4$, so $F=4F_{\rm cone}\Leftrightarrow V_s=4V_c$. General relativity supplies the value
$c^4/4G$; the axiom supplies its geometric reading and the normalization of $F$.

\subsection*{8.3\quad The electric axiom}
\sD\ Two elementary charges repel with $F_e=\alpha\hbar c/d^2$ and two Planck masses attract with
$F_g=Gm_{\rm P}^2/d^2=\hbar c/d^2$, so $F_e/F_g=\alpha$ at every separation $d$; at $d=\lP$, where $F_g=F$,
\begin{equation}
F_e\,G=\alpha\,c^4,\qquad F_e=\alpha F .
\label{eq:axiomalpha}
\end{equation}

\subsection*{8.4\quad Geometric force}
\sD\ With $\MH=Fr/2c^2$ (\S12.2) and $\EH=\MH c^2=Fr/2$, $F_{\rm geom}\equiv d\EH/dr=F/2=c^4/2G$ and
$\EH/2r=F/4=F_{\rm cone}$: the horizon energy over the horizon diameter is the one-cone force.

\section{The Zero Equation}\label{sec:zeros}

\subsection*{9.1--9.2\quad Smooth count and zero equation}
\sS\ The zeros of $\zeta(s)$ with $0<\mathrm{Im}\,s\le T$ number $N(T)=\vartheta(T)/\pi+1+S(T)$, with
$\vartheta(T)=\arg\Gamma(\tfrac14+\tfrac{iT}2)-\tfrac T2\log\pi=\tfrac T2\log\tfrac{T}{2\pi}-\tfrac T2-\tfrac\pi8+O(1/T)$
and $S(T)=O(\log T)$, so $N(T)/N_{\rm smooth}(T)\to1$ with
\begin{equation}
N_{\rm smooth}(T)=\frac{T}{2\pi}\log\frac{T}{2\pi e}+\frac78 ,
\end{equation}
the $\tfrac78$ being the pole at $s=1$ less the Gamma factor's phase $\tfrac18$ ($N(1000)=649$,
$N_{\rm smooth}(1000)=648.6$).

\subsection*{9.3--9.4\quad Cone connection and offset}
The cone has the same form: $A_c/V_c=3(1+\sqrt2)/r\to0$ is its smooth part, and the deficit the constant that keeps the
surface from closing flat. The leading count $\tfrac{T}{2\pi}(\log\tfrac{T}{2\pi}-1)$ is the phase-space area of the
Hamiltonian $xp$, which generates scale transformations \cite{BerryKeating1999}; in GRUT the same generator, $r\partial_r$,
has the horizon quantities of \S2.8 as eigenfunctions, $r\partial_rX=kX$. A scale operator whose spectrum is the zeros would
complete the link (the Hilbert--P\'olya problem); this is an analogy, not yet a derivation.

\section{Derivation of \texorpdfstring{$r$}{r}}\label{sec:r}

\subsection*{10.1\quad From $H_0$}
\sC\ $r=c/H_0=1.3735\times10^{26}$ m $=14.52$ Gly at $H_0=67.35$, set through the budget by the CMB acoustic angle
(\S0.3, \S16).

\subsection*{10.2\quad From $G$: the horizon condition}
\sD\ The horizon is the Schwarzschild radius of the mass it contains,
\begin{equation}
r=\frac{2G\MH}{c^2}=\frac{2c^2\MH}{F},
\label{eq:horizon}
\end{equation}
a condition equivalent to flatness (\S12.2).

\subsection*{10.3\quad The radius partition}
\sD\ Being linear in mass, the Schwarzschild radius divides among the contents by density fraction: with
$M_i=\Omega_i\MH$,
\begin{equation}
r=\sum_i r_i,\qquad r_i=\frac{2GM_i}{c^2}=\Omega_i\,r ,
\label{eq:partition}
\end{equation}
$r_m=0.316\,r$ and $r_\Lambda=0.684\,r$ at the resonance budget. The observable horizon, the distance light has travelled
in the space frame, is the conformal radius $c\eta_0=3.18\,r=46.1$ Gly (\S26.3).

\section{Derivation of \texorpdfstring{$N$}{N}}\label{sec:N}

\subsection*{11.1--11.3\quad From the cell count and from the action}
\sD\ Besides~\eqref{eq:N}: the horizon energy $\MH c^2$ acting for the light-crossing time $r/c$ is the action $\MH cr$, in
units of $\hbar$
\begin{equation}
Q=\frac{\MH cr}{\hbar}=\frac{Fr^2}{2\hbar c}=\frac{c^3r^2}{2\hbar G}=\frac{N}{4\pi}
\label{eq:Q}
\end{equation}
quanta, the $4\pi$ being the full sphere's. The two routes agree, $2\pi r^2c^3/\hbar G=4\pi\MH cr/\hbar$, exactly when
$\MH=c^2r/2G$: the horizon condition~\eqref{eq:horizon}.

\section{Derivation of \texorpdfstring{$G$}{G}}\label{sec:G}

\subsection*{12.1\quad From the axiom}
$G=c^4/F=6.6743\times10^{-11}\ \mathrm{m^3\,kg^{-1}\,s^{-2}}$, constant because $F$ is (\S2.8).

\subsection*{12.2\quad From the horizon mass}
\sD\ The horizon mass
\begin{equation}
\MH=\frac{4\pi}{3}r^3\rho_c=\frac{c^2r}{2G}=\frac{Fr}{2c^2}
\label{eq:MH}
\end{equation}
satisfies $2G\MH/c^2=r$; conversely, $r_s(M)=r$ for $M=\tfrac{4\pi}{3}r^3\rho$ gives $\tfrac{8\pi G}{3}\rho=c^2/r^2=H^2$,
the flat Friedmann equation. The horizon condition and flatness are one statement,
$2G\MH/c^2=r\iff\rho=\rho_c\iff\Omega_{\rm tot}=1$. Mass is linear in $r$; the power $r^2$ belongs to the information
currency, $N\propto r^2$ (\S24).

\section{Derivation of \texorpdfstring{$\hbar$}{hbar}}\label{sec:hbar}
\sD\ From the cell count, the axiom and~\eqref{eq:MH},
\begin{equation}
\hbar=\frac{2\pi r^2c^3}{GN}=\frac{2\pi Fr^2}{Nc}=\frac{4\pi\MH cr}{N},
\end{equation}
constant as $r$ flows since $N\propto r^2$. In the units~\eqref{eq:units} it is $2\pi/N$ units of action: the horizon
action shared among the cells.

\section{Derivation of \texorpdfstring{$F$}{F}}\label{sec:F}
\sD\ From \S8: the universe carries $F=c^4/G=1.2103\times10^{44}$ N and each of its four cones
$F/4=3.0256\times10^{43}$ N, $F_{\rm geom}=F/2$, and the maximum power is $\Pmax=F_{\rm cone}c=c^5/4G=9.071\times10^{51}$ W.

\section{The Fine-Structure Constant \texorpdfstring{$\alpha$}{alpha}}\label{sec:alpha}

\subsection*{15.1--15.2\quad From the force ratio; value}
\sC\ By~\eqref{eq:axiomalpha}, $\alpha=F_e/F=F_eG/c^4$, calibrated at $1/137.035\,999\,177(21)$ (CODATA 2022). This is the
zero-momentum value, from which the running to $1/128.9$ at the $Z$ mass (on shell) follows (\S31.5).

\subsection*{15.3\quad Geometric origin}
\sI\ The generators' phases $1$, $e^{2\pi i/3}$, $e^{4\pi i/3}$ form $\mathbb Z_3$, in the Standard Model the centre of
colour $SU(3)$, whose tie to hypercharge puts quark charges in thirds: the gauge group is
$[SU(3)\times SU(2)\times U(1)]/\mathbb Z_6$ \cite{BaezHuerta2010}. Closure gives the singlet rule: with $\pi/3$ per
generator and closure at multiples of $\pi$, one generator ($\pi/3$) or two ($2\pi/3$) do not close, a generator with its
conjugate ($0$) or three generators ($\pi$) do---the meson and baryon combinations; the two that fail are never seen free.
This is a correspondence with triality, not a proof of confinement.

\sD\ The number of colours follows without it: Witten's global anomaly needs an even number of $SU(2)$ doublets per
generation, $N_c+1$, so $N_c$ is odd \cite{Witten1982}, and asymptotic freedom with six flavours needs $11N_c>12$; the
smallest allowed $N_c$ is $3$, the order of $C_3$ (\S5). Anomaly cancellation then derives $Q_u=\tfrac23$,
$Q_d=-\tfrac13$, $Q_e=-1$, $Q_\nu=0$ (\S31.4).

\sI\ The value of $\alpha$ is the conjecture of \S43A/C, solved self-consistently,
\[
\frac1\alpha=4\pi^3+\pi^2+\pi-\frac{\alpha}{24}\big(1+e^{-2\pi}\big)=137.035\,999\,151\,7 ,
\]
$1.2\sigma$ below CODATA 2022; no other prediction rests on it.

\section{Derivation of \texorpdfstring{$H_0$}{H0}}\label{sec:H0}
\sP\ $H_0=c/r$. With the resonance budget (\S25.11) the CMB acoustic angle fixes
$H_0=67.35\pm0.06\ \mathrm{km\,s^{-1}\,Mpc^{-1}}$, at which every $H_0$-dependent number in this paper is quoted ($r$,
$\MH$, $\EH\propto H_0^{-1}$; $N$, $L_{\rm H}\propto H_0^{-2}$; $\TGH$, $a_0\propto H_0$).

\subsection*{16.1\quad $H_0$ against the 2025--26 measurements}
\sM\ Pulls of $67.35$; after the slash, with the local-rate scatter below added to the three direct ladder measurements:
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lcc@{}}
\toprule
measurement & value & pull \\
\midrule
Local Distance Network \cite{H0DN2026} & $73.50\pm0.81$ & $-7.6$ / $-6.1$ \\
SH0ES \cite{Riess2022} & $73.04\pm1.04$ & $-5.5$ / $-4.8$ \\
CCHP TRGB \cite{CCHP2025} & $70.39\pm1.94$ & $-1.6$ / $-1.5$ \\
TDCOSMO lensing \cite{H0DN2026} & $71.6^{+3.9}_{-3.3}$ & $-1.3$ \\
DESI DR2 BAO + BBN, flat $\Lambda$CDM \cite{H0DN2026} & $68.51\pm0.58$ & $-2.0$ \\
CMB, $\Lambda$CDM \cite{H0DN2026}; Planck 2018 \cite{Planck2018} & $67.24\pm0.35$; $67.36\pm0.54$ & $+0.3$; $-0.1$ \\
DESI Ly$\alpha$ + BBN, CMB-independent \cite{DESI2026lya} & $66.5\pm1.3$ & $+0.6$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The ladder lies $9\%$ above $67.35$; $99.5\%$ of the gap is the CMB calibration GRUT shares with $\Lambda$CDM
(Appendix~\ref{app:variance}).

\sD\ GRUT has one expansion rate, the background's $67.35$. The dilation leaves the background and its sound horizon
unmoved and changes the local rate only through the local density contrast (\S53.9). It shifts clocks and rulers only by
the gravitational potential, under a part in $10^5$, so no dilation of clocks can carry the ladder's $9\%$. Nor can a local
void: the supernova Hubble diagram shows no change of $H_0$ across $0.023<z<0.15$ to $0.60\%$ (\S53.9).

\sH\ On the unbound web the dilated flows widen the scatter of the local rate to $0.88\%$ on $300$ Mpc ($0.41\%$ in
$\Lambda$CDM, \S53.9), which leaves the network $6.1\sigma$ from $67.35$ ($7.6\sigma$ bare), SH0ES $4.8\sigma$ and CCHP
$1.5\sigma$.

\sP\ Every distance ladder calibrated alike converges on $67.3$ within that scatter; the CMB-independent Lyman-$\alpha$
value already lies $0.6\sigma$ from it.

\section{Energy}\label{sec:E}
% =====================================================================
Values in \S\S17--21 are at $H_0=67.35$; $r$, $\MH$, $\EH$ scale as $H_0^{-1}$ and $N$, $\SGH$, $L_{\rm H}$ as $H_0^{-2}$.

\subsection*{17.1\quad From mass}
$\EH=\MH c^2=Fr/2=c^5/2GH_0=8.312\times10^{69}$ J.

\subsection*{17.2\quad From force}
$\EH=\int_0^rF_{\rm geom}\,dr'=\tfrac12Fr$, with $F_{\rm geom}=F/2$ (\S8.4).

\subsection*{17.3\quad From the axiom}
\sD\ The factor $\tfrac12$ is the horizon condition $2G\MH/c^2=r$ (\S10.2) and the active fraction $p=\tfrac12$ of the force
(\S6): $E=Frp=Fr/2=\EH$, and one cone carries $E_{\rm cone}=F_{\rm cone}\,r\,p=Fr/8=\EH/4=2.078\times10^{69}$ J, in the
volume ratio $V_s=4V_c$.

% =====================================================================
\section{Mass}\label{sec:M}
% =====================================================================
$\MH=\EH/c^2=Fr/2c^2=c^3/2GH_0=9.248\times10^{52}$ kg, and one cone carries $M_{\rm cone}=\MH/4=2.312\times10^{52}$ kg;
in the units~\eqref{eq:units}, $\MH=\tfrac12$ and $M_{\rm cone}=\tfrac18$.

\subsection*{18.1\quad Mass as deficit, and the closure mass}
\sS\ A straight defect carrying mass $M$ along a length $L$ has deficit angle $\delta=8\pi GM/c^2L$ \cite{Vilenkin1981}: that
of a point mass $M$ in $2{+}1$ gravity with the reduced coupling $G_3=G/L$, $\delta=8\pi G_3M/c^2$ \cite{DJtH1984}.

\sD\ (I3) The horizon's cone carries the $C_3$ deficit $\pi$ (second axiom) and the mass $M_{\rm cone}=c^2r/8G$ (I1), so
$\pi=8\pi GM_{\rm cone}/c^2L$ gives $L=r$: the reduction length is the horizon radius, and
\begin{equation}
\delta(M)=\frac{8\pi GM}{c^2r}=4\pi\,\frac{M}{\MH}.
\label{eq:defectmass}
\end{equation}

\begin{theorem}[Closure mass]\label{thm:closure}
\sD\ A spatially closed section of the reduced geometry carrying point masses $M_i$ requires $\sum_i\delta(M_i)=4\pi$, that is
$\sum_iM_i=\MH$: the horizon mass is the closure mass. The regular tetrahedron realizes it with four masses $M_{\rm cone}$, each
carrying the deficit $\dtot=\pi$ of one $C_3$ apex.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
A closed surface flat except at conical points has total curvature $2\pi\chi=4\pi$, the sum of its deficits (Gauss--Bonnet;
Descartes,~\eqref{eq:descartes}); use~\eqref{eq:defectmass}. A vertex of the regular tetrahedron has defect
$2\pi-3\cdot\tfrac\pi3=\pi$ (\S4.4).
\end{proof}
The horizon condition (flatness, \S12.2), the curvature closure $4\dtot=4\pi$ (\S4.4) and the four-cone partition
$\MH=4M_{\rm cone}$, which I1 reads as $V_s=4V_c$ (\S3.12), are therefore one statement.

% =====================================================================
\section{Momentum}\label{sec:p}
% =====================================================================
The four-momentum $p^\mu=(E/c,\vec p)$ has $p^\mu p_\mu=-E^2/c^2+|\vec p|^2=-m^2c^2$. For $m=0$, $E=|\vec p|\,c$: the cone
$h=r$ with height $E/c$ and radius $|\vec p|$, so the seed in momentum space is the dispersion relation of light. A massive
particle lives on the hyperboloid inside the cone,
\begin{equation}
E=\sqrt{p^2c^2+m^2c^4}=mc^2+\frac{p^2}{2m}-\frac{p^4}{8m^3c^2}+\cdots .
\label{eq:dispersion}
\end{equation}
The horizon's momentum scale is $\MH c=Fr/2c=2.773\times10^{61}\ \mathrm{kg\,m\,s^{-1}}$.

% =====================================================================
\section{Information}\label{sec:I}
% =====================================================================
\begin{equation}
I=\frac N2=\frac{\pi r^2}{\lP^2}=\frac{4\pi r^2}{4\lP^2}=\SGH=2.269\times10^{122}\ \text{nats}\ (3.275\times10^{122}\ \text{bits}).
\label{eq:SGH}
\end{equation}
\sS\ This is the Bekenstein--Hawking entropy of the full horizon sphere \cite{Bekenstein1973,Hawking1975,GibbonsHawking1977}:
the full area is twice the effective area, $A=2A_{\rm eff}=2N\lP^2$, so $A/4\lP^2=N/2$.

% =====================================================================
\section{Angular Momentum}\label{sec:L}
% =====================================================================
\sD\ The horizon's mass carried around its radius at light speed,
\begin{equation}
L_{\rm H}=\MH\,c\,r=\frac{Fr^2}{2c}=\frac{\hbar N}{4\pi}=3.808\times10^{87}\ \mathrm{J\,s},
\end{equation}
is the horizon action of \S11 ($L_{\rm H}/\hbar=Q$) and the Noether charge of the horizon's spin (\S36.11):
$\SGH=2\pi L_{\rm H}/\hbar$ and $\EH=(c/r)\,L_{\rm H}=k_B\TGH\SGH$.

% =====================================================================
\section{Critical Acceleration and \texorpdfstring{$a_0$}{a0}}\label{sec:a0}
% =====================================================================
Rotating galaxies obey the radial acceleration relation (RAR): the observed acceleration $g$ is one function of the baryons'
Newtonian acceleration $g_{\rm bar}$, Newtonian at high acceleration and $g\to\sqrt{a_0\,g_{\rm bar}}$ below
$a_0\approx1.2\times10^{-10}\ \mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$ \cite{Milgrom1983,McGaugh2016}, a scale of order $cH_0/6$. GRUT derives the
coefficient from the $C_3$ spectrum (\S5) and the function from a threshold response on the cycle; the one modelling input,
the exposure law, does not affect the scale (Theorem~\ref{thm:universal}).

\subsection*{22.1\quad Exposure and survival}
\sD\ (I4) A mode on the horizon cycle is activated by a local acceleration $a$ as a Poisson process of rate $\beta x$ per unit
affine parameter $s$, linear in the load $x\equiv a/a_c$ ($a_c=c/\tau_1=k_3cH_0$, \S5; derived in \S22.10), with $\beta$ the
rate constant of the relaxation operator $d/ds+\beta$ of the eikonal flow. The exposure is one memoryless stage per arc: on a
cycle of $n$ arcs it is Erlang, $p_n(s)=(n\beta)^ns^{n-1}e^{-n\beta s}/(n-1)!$, with mean $1/\beta$.

\begin{lemma}[Survival]\label{lem:survival}
\sD\ A mode survives unactivated with probability $\int_0^\infty p_n(s)\,e^{-\beta xs}ds=(1+x/n)^{-n}$, independent of
$\beta$; its active fraction is
\begin{equation}
f_n(x)=1-\Big(1+\frac xn\Big)^{-n}.
\label{eq:Sn}
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
For $n=1$ the survival $a_c/(a+a_c)$ is the TRSD threshold distribution; a fixed exposure gives $e^{-x}$. The index $n$ (a
Gamma exposure for non-integer $n$) is the order of the Newtonian tail: a coordinate on shapes, constrained by data
(\S22.6--22.7), not a count.

\subsection*{22.2\quad Activation and response}
\sI\ The response along a direction is the load times the active fraction, $j_n(x)=x\,f_n(x)$.

\subsection*{22.3\quad Isotropic average}
\sD\ The radial field averages the response over directions: with $u=\cos\theta$ the projected load is $xu$, its radial
projection carries a second factor $u$, and isotropy weights $u$ uniformly on $[0,1]$; the factor $3$ makes a linear response
return itself:
\begin{equation}
\sigma_n(x)=3\int_0^1 j_n(xu)\,u\,du,\qquad
\mu_n(x)\equiv\frac{\sigma_n(x)}{x}=3\int_0^1u^2\,f_n(xu)\,du .
\label{eq:mun}
\end{equation}
With $w=1+xu/n$ the integrals close (checked against quadrature to $10^{-12}$):
\begin{align}
\mu_1(x)&=1-\frac{3}{2x}+\frac{3}{x^2}-\frac{3\ln(1+x)}{x^3},\label{eq:mu1}\\
\mu_2(x)&=1-\frac{24}{x^3}\Big[\frac x2+\frac{x/2}{1+x/2}-2\ln\Big(1+\frac x2\Big)\Big],\label{eq:mu2}\\
\mu_3(x)&=1-\frac{81}{x^3}\Big[\ln\!\Big(1+\frac x3\Big)+\frac{2}{1+x/3}
-\frac{1}{2(1+x/3)^2}-\frac32\Big],\label{eq:mu3}\\
\mu_\infty(x)&=1-\frac{3}{x^3}\Big[2-e^{-x}\big(x^2+2x+2\big)\Big],\label{eq:muinf}
\end{align}
and at small load $\mu_n=\tfrac34x-\tfrac{3(n+1)}{10n}x^2+\tfrac{(n+1)(n+2)}{12n^2}x^3-\cdots$.

\begin{theorem}[Universality of $\tfrac34$]\label{thm:universal}
\sD\ If (a) activation is a Poisson process whose hazard is linear in the load, (b) the exposure has a finite mean, used as
the unit, (c) the response is the load times the active fraction, and (d) directions are averaged as in~\eqref{eq:mun}, then
$\mu(x)=\tfrac34x+O(x^2)$ for every exposure distribution.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The survival $1-f$ is the Laplace transform of the exposure density in units of its mean, so $f(y)=y+O(y^2)$ and
$\mu(x)=3\int_0^1u^2(xu)\,du+O(x^2)=\tfrac34x+O(x^2)$.
\end{proof}
Outside the class (a)--(d) the slope changes, and (a) is itself required by data:
\begin{proposition}[Linear hazard]\label{prop:hazard}
\sD\ A hazard growing as $x^p$ gives $\mu\simeq\frac{3}{3+p}x^p$ and, in the deep regime, $g\propto g_{\rm bar}^{1/(1+p)}$:
rotation curves are asymptotically flat, with $V^4\propto M$, only for $p=1$, which the baryonic Tully--Fisher relation
\cite{Lelli2016btfr} therefore fixes. If the exposure mean diverges, so does $f'(0)$, and $\mu$ is not linear at small $x$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proposition}[Transition and tail]\label{prop:decouple}
\sD\ For real $n\neq1,2,3$, with $\xi=x/n$ and $w=1+\xi$,
\begin{equation}
1-\mu_n(x)=\frac{3}{\xi^3}\Big[\frac{w^{3-n}-1}{3-n}-\frac{2(w^{2-n}-1)}{2-n}+\frac{w^{1-n}-1}{1-n}\Big],
\label{eq:mun-real}
\end{equation}
so the tail is $1-\mu_n\simeq\frac{3n^n}{3-n}\,x^{-n}$ for $n<3$, while the transition moves little: $\mu_n(1)=0.4206$,
$0.4515$, $0.4557$, $0.4601$, $0.4623$ for $n=1$, $1.62$, $1.75$, $1.91$, $2$. In a one-exponent form such as
$z/(1+z^n)^{1/n}$ the one exponent sets both the transition width and the tail, $1-\mu\simeq z^{-n}/n$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Substitute $w=1+\xi u$ in $3\int_0^1u^2(1+\xi u)^{-n}du$; the leading term at large $\xi$ is $3\xi^{-n}/(3-n)$. The
one-exponent tail is the expansion of $(1+z^{-n})^{-1/n}$.
\end{proof}

\subsection*{22.4\quad Field equation}
\sS\ The Lagrangian $\mathcal L=-\frac{a_c^2}{8\pi G}\,\mathcal F\big(|\nabla\Phi|^2/a_c^2\big)-\rho\Phi$ with
$\mathcal F'(y)=\mu(\sqrt y)$ gives the AQUAL form \cite{BekensteinMilgrom1984}, here with a derived $\mu$,
\begin{equation}
\nabla\cdot\Big[\mu\Big(\frac{|\nabla\Phi|}{a_c}\Big)\nabla\Phi\Big]=4\pi G\rho;
\label{eq:aqual}
\end{equation}
in spherical symmetry Gauss's theorem gives $\mu(g/a_c)\,g=g_{\rm bar}$ exactly.

\subsection*{22.5\quad The scale}
\sD\ In the deep regime $\mu\simeq\tfrac34\,g/a_c$, so $\tfrac34g^2/a_c=g_{\rm bar}$ and $g=\sqrt{\tfrac43a_c\,g_{\rm bar}}$:
\begin{equation}
a_0=\frac43\,a_c=\frac{\sqrt3}{\pi^2}\,cH_0,\qquad\frac{\sqrt3}{\pi^2}=0.175493 ,
\label{eq:a0}
\end{equation}
the coefficient $k_3=3\sqrt3/4\pi^2$ of the first $C_3$ eigenvalue ($a_c=c/\tau_1$, \S5) times the isotropic $\tfrac43$.
Neither factor is adjustable, and neither depends on the exposure law (Theorem~\ref{thm:universal}).
\predict $a_0=1.148\times10^{-10}\ \mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$ at the resonance budget's $H_0=67.35$ (\S25.11); $a_0\propto H_0$, so
$1.245\times10^{-10}$ at the SH0ES $73.04$. The RAR measures $1.20\pm0.02\,(\mathrm{stat})\pm0.24\,(\mathrm{sys})\times10^{-10}$
\cite{McGaugh2016}, so both agree within the systematic band; read in reverse it gives $H_0=70.4\pm1.2\pm14$. Once the
systematic falls below $5\%$, the same measurement will also choose between the calibrations of $H_0$.

\subsection*{22.6\quad The radial acceleration relation}
\sM\ Against the 2696 points of 147 SPARC galaxies \cite{McGaugh2016,Lelli2016}, with mass-to-light ratios
$\Upsilon_{\rm disk}=0.5$ and $\Upsilon_{\rm bul}=0.7$, $a_c$ fixed by $H_0$ and nothing fitted, the residuals
$\log_{10}(g_{\rm obs}/g_{\rm pred})$ have mean $-0.007$, $+0.016$, $+0.024$ dex and rms $0.133$, $0.134$, $0.136$ for $\mu_1$,
$\mu_2$, $\mu_3$ (computed at $H_0=67.12$; at $67.35$ every mean is lower by less than $0.001$ dex, at $73.04$ by
$0.012$--$0.013$). The observed scatter is $0.13$ dex and the best empirical fit reaches $0.133$: with no parameter adjusted the
family reproduces the galaxies to within $0.024$ dex ($6\%$) in the mean (Figure~\ref{fig:rar}). Fitting the scale alone
(errors from a bootstrap over galaxies) gives $a_0=1.105\pm0.071$ ($\mu_1$) and $1.301\pm0.079$ ($\mu_3$), the empirical fit
returning $g_\dagger=1.161\pm0.071$ against the published $1.20$; through~\eqref{eq:a0} the galaxies measure
$H_0=64.8\pm4.2\pm13$ ($\mu_1$) or $76.3\pm4.6\pm15$ ($\mu_3$), bracketing $67.35$. The family deviates from the empirical
function ($g_\dagger=1.20$) by at most $0.022$, $0.038$ and $0.069$ dex for $n=1$, $3$, $\infty$.

\emph{The rotation-curve likelihood.} \sM\ The RAR points take mass-to-light ratios, distances and inclinations as known. The
full likelihood \cite{Li2018} fits the velocities of 153 galaxies (quality flag below 3, inclination $\ge30^\circ$; 3168
points) with 490 per-galaxy nuisance parameters: $\log\Upsilon_{\rm disk}$ and $\log\Upsilon_{\rm bul}$ with lognormal priors of
$0.1$ dex about $0.5$ and $0.7$, and distance and inclination with Gaussian priors from the SPARC table, minimized galaxy by
galaxy while the global scale is scanned. The best $\chi^2$ is $14370$ for $3167$ degrees of freedom, $\chi^2_{\rm red}=4.54$,
so errors are scaled by $\sqrt{\chi^2_{\rm red}}=2.13$ (median per-galaxy reduced $\chi^2$: $2.1$). The threshold family with
real $n$ and TRSD's original one-exponent Torridity form $z/(1+z^n)^{1/n}$ \cite{TRSD2026} are compared, with the empirical
function as a benchmark:
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}llccc@{}}
\toprule
form & $n$ & $a_0$ [$10^{-10}$] & $\Delta\chi^2/\chi^2_{\rm red}$ & cost of $a_0=1.144$ \\
\midrule
threshold & $1$ & $1.063$ & $4.7$ & $3.5$ \\
threshold & $1.38$ (optimum) & $1.045$ & $0$ & $5.5$ \\
threshold & $1.75$ (mid-window) & $1.037$ & $2.2$ & $6.7$ \\
threshold & $2$ & $1.034$ & $5.1$ & $7.2$ \\
threshold & $3$ & $1.029$ & $18.6$ & $8.3$ \\
Torridity & $0.97$ (optimum) & $1.057$ & $4.4$ & $4.0$ \\
Torridity & $\pi/3$ & $1.081$ & $9.2$ & $2.2$ \\
Torridity & $2\pi/3$ & $1.201$ & $397$ & $2.7$ \\
benchmark & -- & $1.009$ & $-13.6$ & -- \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Scale errors are $\pm0.04$ (statistical, scaled). The fourth column is measured from the best threshold member; the last is
$\Delta\chi^2/\chi^2_{\rm red}$ for fixing the scale at the theory value of $H_0=67.12$, measured from the member's own best
scale. At $67.35$ the theory value is $1.148$ and each pull, the square root of that cost, grows by $0.1$ (falls by $0.1$ for
$2\pi/3$).

The shape parameter is not the physics: $n$ is a coordinate on shapes, and the two forms assign different values, $1.38$ and
$0.97$, to nearly the same transition. The galaxies constrain the width of the transition, the planets the tail (\S22.7). The
threshold family fits better than any Torridity exponent (by $4.4$). The empirical function leads it by $13.6$, but its form
was chosen on these same data \cite{McGaugh2016}: it is a benchmark derived from nothing, not a competing prediction.

The scale depends little on the shape: $a_0=1.034$--$1.045$ for $1.38\le n\le2$. With the nuisance parameters fixed at their
nominal values the likelihood returns $g_\dagger=1.206$, reproducing the published $1.20$; profiling them lowers every scale by
about $16\%$, while moving the $\Upsilon_{\rm disk}$ prior centre to $0.4$ or $0.6$ shifts it by only $\pm1.5\%$. With half the
fixed-versus-profiled shift as the systematic, the window of \S22.7 gives
$a_0=1.04\pm0.04\,(\mathrm{stat})\pm0.10\,(\mathrm{nuisance})$: the prediction $1.148$ is $1.0\sigma$ high ($2.7\sigma$ on the
statistical error alone; $1.9\sigma$ for $1.245$ at $H_0=73.04$). The agreement is conditional on the nuisance model.

\subsection*{22.7\quad Newtonian recovery and the transition--tail decoupling}
\sD\ At high acceleration $1-\mu_n=3\int_0^1u^2(1+xu/n)^{-n}du$, and the tails differ:
\begin{equation}
1-\mu_1\simeq\frac{3}{2x},\qquad
1-\mu_2\simeq\frac{12}{x^2},\qquad
1-\mu_3\simeq\frac{81}{x^3}\Big[\ln\frac x3-\frac32\Big],\qquad
1-\mu_\infty\simeq\frac{6}{x^3}.
\end{equation}
For $n=1$ the anomaly $\Delta g\equiv g-g_{\rm bar}$ tends to the constant $\tfrac32a_c=1.29\times10^{-10}\ \mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$
at every planet. An inward anomaly $\Delta g\propto d^{\,k}$ ($d$ the heliocentric distance) turns a perihelion back by
$\Delta\varpi=-(k+2)\pi\,\Delta g\,a^2\sqrt{1-e^2}/GM_\odot$ per orbit ($a$, $e$ the semi-major axis and eccentricity; exact for
$k=0$, leading order in $e$ otherwise): $-1.7\times10^3$ mas/cy for Mercury and $-2.8\times10^3$ mas/cy for the Earth, against
the ephemeris residuals $-2.0\pm3.0$ and $0.19\pm0.19$ mas/cy \cite{PitjevaPitjev2013}. The single-stage tail is excluded by
factors of $6\times10^2$ and $1.5\times10^4$.

A tail $x^{-n}$ has $k=2n-2$. Both perihelia within $2\sigma$ of their residuals (the Earth's binds) require $n\ge1.62$ for
the threshold family and $n\ge1.53$ for the Torridity form; the galaxies give $1.38\pm0.20$ and $0.97\pm0.04$ (\S22.6).

\sD\ The one-exponent form therefore fails for every exponent: the galaxies and the planets ask for different values of its
one parameter, which is why TRSD's first version needed a screening factor. Its original exponent $\pi/3$ lies at
$\Delta\chi^2/\chi^2_{\rm red}=9.2$ and turns the perihelia back by $6\times10^2$ (Mercury) and $1.1\times10^3$ mas/cy (Earth);
its disk-galaxy exponent $2\pi/3$ passes the planets and lies at $397$ (Figure~\ref{fig:tails}). The threshold family
separates transition and tail (Proposition~\ref{prop:decouple}) and meets both in the window $1.62\le n\le1.91$, whose upper
edge is $\Delta\chi^2/\chi^2_{\rm red}=4$. At $n=1.75$ the perihelion shifts are $-0.004$ (Mercury) and $-0.028$ mas/cy
(Earth); at the lower edge the Earth's reaches $-0.20$ mas/cy, the present sensitivity.

\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\begin{tikzpicture}
\begin{axis}[width=0.8\textwidth, height=6.0cm, xmin=0.8, xmax=3.05, ymin=0, ymax=25,
  xlabel={exponent or tail order $n$}, ylabel={$\Delta\chi^2/\chi^2_{\rm red}$ (rotation curves)},
  legend pos=south east, legend style={font=\scriptsize, draw=none},
  tick label style={font=\scriptsize}, label style={font=\small}]
\fill[red!12] (axis cs:0.8,0) rectangle (axis cs:1.534,25);

\draw[gray, dashed] (axis cs:0.8,4) -- (axis cs:3.05,4);

\addlegendentry{threshold family, tail order $n$}
\addplot[red!75!black, thick, mark=square*, mark size=1.4pt] coordinates {(0.8,21.1) (0.9,6.21) (0.971,4.40) (1.0,5.47) (1.0472,9.19) (1.1,15.9) (1.25,47.5)};

\end{tikzpicture}
\caption{Rotation-curve likelihood against the shape parameter of the two forms, from the best threshold member. Red: excluded
by the perihelia of Mercury and the Earth (both forms below $1.53$, the threshold family below $1.62$). Green: the window where
the threshold family meets both. Dashed: $2\sigma$. Wherever the planets allow the Torridity form, the galaxies exclude it.}
\label{fig:tails}
\end{figure}

\begin{proposition}[Running exponent]\label{prop:running}
\sD\ Let the local exponent $m(z)$ at $z=g/a_0$ be the exponent of the one-parameter form through the same point,
$z/(1+z^m)^{1/m}=\mu_n(\tfrac43z)$. For the threshold family with $n<3$, $m\to1$ as $z\to0$ and $m\to n$ as $z\to\infty$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
At small $z$, $\mu_n=z-b_nz^2+O(z^3)$ with $b_n=8(n+1)/15n>0$ (\S22.3), while the one-parameter form is $z-z^{1+m}/m+\dots$;
matching gives $m-1=\ln(b_nm)/\ln z\to0$. At large $z$, $1-\mu_n\simeq\frac{3n^n}{3-n}(\tfrac43z)^{-n}$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:decouple}) against $z^{-m}/m$, so $m\to n$.
\end{proof}
There is therefore no universal exponent: it runs from $1$ far out to the tail order close in, and each system reads it at
its own acceleration:
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccc@{}}
\toprule
& $\log_{10}g$ & $m$ ($n=1.62$) & $m$ ($n=1.75$) & $m$ ($n=1.91$) \\
\midrule
far out & $-14$ & $1.014$ & $1.017$ & $1.020$ \\
galaxy outskirts & $-11.5$ & $1.032$ & $1.038$ & $1.045$ \\
galaxy transition & $-10.5$ & $1.059$ & $1.070$ & $1.082$ \\
inner galaxies & $-9.5$ & $1.135$ & $1.161$ & $1.190$ \\
Saturn & $-4.19$ & $1.507$ & $1.612$ & $1.740$ \\
the Earth & $-2.23$ & $1.534$ & $1.646$ & $1.782$ \\
Mercury & $-1.40$ & $1.542$ & $1.656$ & $1.794$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Galaxies ($10^{-12}$--$10^{-9}\ \mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$) read the exponent between $1.03$ and $1.28$, the planets between $1.51$
and $1.79$. A one-parameter form must use one value at every acceleration, and fails; the running exponent meets both. The
planets' requirement on a one-parameter form, $m\ge1.534$ at the Earth, is what the lower edge of the window reads there: the
two planetary conditions are one, to $0.003$. TRSD's original exponent $\pi/3=1.047$ is the running exponent of galaxy
outskirts ($m=1.04$--$1.06$ at $g=10^{-11}\ \mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$ across the window), measured where it holds; the regime
dependence TRSD anticipated with multiples of $\pi/3$ is realized by the running exponent, but $2\pi/3$ is not what disks read.

A multiplicative screening factor cannot play this role: $1-\mu_{\rm scr}=(1-\mu_1)(1+x)^{-\beta_{\rm s}}$ gives
$\mu_{\rm scr}\simeq(\tfrac34+\beta_{\rm s})x$ at small $x$, changing the deep-regime coefficient and with it $a_0$. TRSD's
$\beta_{\rm s}=\pi/2$ would lower the scale to $a_c/(\tfrac34+\tfrac\pi2)=0.43\,a_c$ and move the fixed-scale fit to SPARC by
$+0.18$ dex (rms $0.235$). Every exposure law leaves $\tfrac34$ untouched.

\sD\ The Solar-System test that probes $\mu$ near $x\sim2$ is the quadrupole induced by the Galactic external field
\cite{Milgrom2009}, which Cassini ranging constrains tightly \cite{Hees2014}: across the window it is
$(3.0$--$3.2)\times10^{-26}\ \mathrm{s^{-2}}$ for the baryon-only equation, excluded at $16\sigma$ (\S49.11). In the complete
equation (\S46--47) and the rotation reading (\S23) the planets see no bound medium, and the quadrupole is the Galactic tide
alone, at $-0.9\sigma$ (\S49.10).

\subsection*{22.8\quad Status of the TRSD regime scales}
TRSD's dwarf and cluster scales, written as (pure number)$\times10^{-10}\ \mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$, depend on the choice of the metre
and the second and cannot be framework statements (Theorem~\ref{thm:c-alone}). GRUT retains the single scale
$a_0=(\sqrt3/\pi^2)\,cH_0$; a dwarf or cluster scale would enter as a dimensionless multiple of $a_0$ derived from the geometry.

\subsection*{22.9\quad The dynamic exponent}
\sD\ Each symmetry-preserving level $j$ of the cascade (\S44, TRSD) is a closed $C_3$ structure with $N_a=2j+1$ arcs enclosing
$N_s=2j$ regions; its arcs are exponential exposure stages shared equally by its regions. Dividing the level's exposure,
$\mathrm{Gamma}(N_a)$, into $N_s$ independent equal parts takes the unique root $(1-it)^{-N_a/N_s}$ of its characteristic
function $(1-it)^{-N_a}$, so each region is exposed with order
\begin{equation}
n_j=\frac{N_a}{N_s}=\frac{2j+1}{2j}=\tfrac32,\ \tfrac54,\ \tfrac76,\ \dots
\label{eq:nj}
\end{equation}

\sI\ An element is exposed at level $j$ in proportion to the fraction of structure still open there, the closure weight
$\kappa_{2j-1}=1/[2j(2j+1)]=\tfrac1{2j}-\tfrac1{2j+1}$ of the full ladder; over the symmetric ladder these sum to $1-\ln2$, so
\begin{equation}
w_j=\frac{1}{(1-\ln2)\,2j(2j+1)}=0.543,\ 0.163,\ 0.078,\ \dots
\label{eq:wj}
\end{equation}
Every level is exposed over the same horizon period (\S22.10), so the levels differ in order, not in scale:
\begin{equation}
\boxed{f(y)=\sum_{j\ge1}w_j\,f_{n_j}(y),\qquad
\mu(x)=3\int_0^1u^2f(xu)\,du=\sum_{j\ge1}w_j\,\mu_{n_j}(x),}
\label{eq:mu-dynamic}
\end{equation}
each $\mu_{n_j}$ from~\eqref{eq:mun-real}. Nothing is fitted: the orders are the arc-to-sphere ratios and the weights the
closure weights.

\emph{The order is dynamic.} The boost is carried by the surviving elements (\S46.6), whose exposure order at load $x$ is
\begin{equation}
\bar n(x)=\frac{\sum_jw_jn_j\,[1-\mu_{n_j}(x)]}{\sum_jw_j\,[1-\mu_{n_j}(x)]},\qquad
\bar n(0)=\sum_j\frac{1}{4j^2(1-\ln2)}=\frac{\pi^2}{24(1-\ln2)}=1.340 ;
\label{eq:nbar}
\end{equation}
it falls to $1.307$ at $x=10$ and $1.239$ at $x=100$: under strong load only the deepest levels, of order near one, still
carry survivors. In TRSD's Torridity form the same law has the running exponent of Proposition~\ref{prop:running}, here $n(z)$:
\begin{equation}
\mu=\frac{z}{\big(1+z^{\,n(z)}\big)^{1/n(z)}},\qquad z=\frac{g}{a_0},
\label{eq:torridity-dynamic}
\end{equation}
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lccccccccc@{}}
\toprule
$\log_{10}g$ [m\,s$^{-2}$] & $-13$ & $-12$ & $-11.5$ & $-11$ & $-10.5$ & $-10$ & $-9.5$ & $-9$ & $-8$ \\
\midrule
$n(z)$ & $1.008$ & $1.011$ & $1.014$ & $1.018$ & $1.026$ & $1.040$ & $1.065$ & $1.095$ & $1.144$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
It tends to one in the deep regime, where the first correction to $\mu$ is analytic in $x$, rises through the transition,
passes $\pi/3$ at $g=1.27\,a_0$---TRSD's exponent is the dynamic exponent at the transition---and peaks at $1.158$ near
$10^3a_0$.

\emph{Limits.} Every level has unit mean exposure, so $\mu=\tfrac34x+O(x^2)$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:universal}) and
$g^2=a_0g_{\rm bar}$ in the deep regime. Toward the Newtonian regime $1-\mu$ falls only slightly faster than $1/x$
($x(1-\mu)=0.63$, $0.41$, $0.29$ at $x=10^2$, $10^3$, $10^4$), because the levels of order near one dominate the approach. At
planetary loads such a tail is excluded (\S22.7); capture switches it off there, the Sun holding none of the medium
($\Gamma=7\times10^{-9}$, \S49.10).

\sP\ The boost is $g/g_{\rm bar}=3.67$, $1.58$, $1.067$ and $1.019$ at $g_{\rm bar}=0.1$, $1$, $10$ and $30\,a_0$, and the excess
$g-g_{\rm bar}$ peaks at $0.70\,a_0$ at $g_{\rm bar}=4.7\,a_0$ (\S47.4).

\emph{The MOND-like equation.} With the capture fraction $\Gamma$ of \S47 the equation is
\begin{equation}
\boxed{g\,\Big[1-\Gamma\big(1-\mu(g/a_c)\big)\Big]=g_{\rm bar},\qquad
\mu(x)=\sum_{j\ge1}w_j\,\mu_{n_j}(x),\qquad a_c=\frac{3\sqrt3}{4\pi^2}\,cH_0 .}
\label{eq:mond-complete}
\end{equation}
Full capture gives $g^2=a_0g_{\rm bar}$ in the deep regime and $g\to g_{\rm bar}$ in the Newtonian one; no capture gives Newton
at every acceleration; partial capture, $0<\Gamma<1$, gives a constant enhancement $g\simeq g_{\rm bar}/(1-\Gamma)$ in the deep
regime instead of the square-root law. The exponent is no longer an input: the cascade levels that still carry survivors set it
at each acceleration, and the equation changes with acceleration, environment and epoch through $n(z)$ and $\Gamma$
(\S22.6--22.7 test fixed orders). At the horizon's own load, $z_H=G\MH/(r^2a_0)=\pi^2/2\sqrt3$, the exponent is $1.0654$ and
fixes the baryon ratio (\S25.7).

% =====================================================================
\subsection*{22.10\quad The map \texorpdfstring{$a_c=c/\tau_1$}{ac=c/tau1} and the exposure law}
\sD\ An accelerated clock approaches its horizon at the rate $a/c$ (\S43B.8). With activations at this rate and the exposure
one period of the fundamental $C_3$ mode, $\tau_1=2\pi/\omega_1=4\pi^2r/(3\sqrt3\,c)$ (\S5), the mean number of events in an
exposure is
\begin{equation}
\lambda=\frac{a\,\tau_1}{c}=\frac{a}{a_c},\qquad a_c=\frac{c}{\tau_1}=\frac{3\sqrt3}{4\pi^2}\,cH ,
\end{equation}
so the map $a_c=c/\tau_1$ states that the threshold is one event per cycle ($\tau_1=110.3$ Gyr and
$c/\tau_1=8.612\times10^{-11}\ \mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$ today) and that the hazard is linear in the load: hypothesis (a) of
Theorem~\ref{thm:universal}, derived, as the Tully--Fisher slope requires (Proposition~\ref{prop:hazard}). If the cycle
completes in $n$ exponential stages, the exposure $U$ in units of $\tau_1$ is Gamma-distributed with unit mean, and the
survival $\langle e^{-\lambda U}\rangle=(1+x/n)^{-n}$ is the threshold family; its order is set by the cascade (\S22.9), and a
fixed order is confined to the window of \S22.7.

\sD\ \emph{The exposure law from the Unruh response.} A mode carried with proper acceleration $a$ sees the vacuum as the Rindler
thermal state \cite{Unruh1976}. A detector of gap $\omega$ coupled with strength $\epsilon$ to a massless field is excited at the
rate per unit proper time
\begin{equation}
\dot F(\omega)=\frac{\epsilon^2}{2\pi}\,\frac{\omega}{e^{2\pi c\,\omega/a}-1}\;\xrightarrow{\;\omega\to0\;}\;\frac{\epsilon^2a}{4\pi^2c},
\end{equation}
so a gapless mode is excited at a rate linear in the acceleration, that is, in the load. At weak coupling the transitions in the
thermal state are independent (Fermi's rule): their count is Poisson and the stages between them memoryless. One activation per
unit of horizon approach, $a\,d\tau/c$, is the normalization $\epsilon^2=4\pi^2$, and it follows from the mode's extent: the
activated mode is the whole $C_3$ cycle, at unit radius the circle $S^1(2\pi)$ of the thermal factor (\S43F), and with unit
coupling per unit length, the only normalization that introduces no further scale, its coupling is the cycle's length,
$\epsilon=2\pi$. I4 is derived.
\section{The Rotation of the Universe}\label{sec:rotation}
% =====================================================================
\sH\ The universe rotates as a whole: the phase $\theta$ of the conjugate pair $\phi_\pm=\phi\,e^{\pm i\theta}$ turns in time at one
rate, the final expansion rate $H_\infty=c\sqrt{\Lambda/3}$, one quantum of the horizon's spin (\S36.14). Today, at the resonance (H3,
\S36.7), it equals the frequency of the horizon's lowest $C_3$ mode, of eigenvalue $\lambda_1=27/4\pi^2r^2$:
\begin{equation}
\Omega_0=H_\infty,\qquad \Omega_0=c\sqrt{\lambda_1}=\frac{3\sqrt3}{2\pi}H_0=0.827\,H_0\ \ \text{today}.
\label{eq:omega0-rot}
\end{equation}

\sD\ \emph{An internal rotation.} Space does not rotate. On the background $\theta=\Omega_0t$, and the flow $u_\mu=\partial_\mu\theta/\sqrt X$
(\S23.1) is a gradient, so its vorticity vanishes identically: the CMB bound on vorticity and vector shear,
$(\sigma_V/H)_0<4.7\times10^{-11}$ at $95\%$ \cite{Saadeh2016}, which a rotation of space at $0.827H_0$ would exceed by ten orders of
magnitude, is met exactly. Nor do windings carry angular momentum: the phase is single-valued on the closed horizon (a section of a
trivial line bundle), so its integer windings around the three defects sum to zero and, equal by the $C_3$ symmetry, each vanishes, and
with them the phase's momentum density (\S52.2), $T_{\hat t\hat\varphi}=\chi_0^2\,n\,\Omega_0/(c\varrho)$ at distance $\varrho$ from the
axis. A global frame dragging (\S52.2) is excluded by the vorticity bound; the extra gravity of galaxies is carried by the rotation's
medium bound around the baryons (\S23.1).

\subsection*{23.1\quad The cosmic share: the rotation held at its rate}
\sD\ The fixed rate is a constraint. With a Lagrange multiplier $\lambda$,
\begin{equation}
S_{\rm rot}=\int d^4x\,\sqrt{-g}\;\lambda\,\big(X-X_0\big),\qquad X=-g^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\theta\,\partial_\nu\theta,\qquad X_0=\Big(\frac{\Omega_0}{c}\Big)^2=\frac\Lambda3 ,
\label{eq:rot-constraint}
\end{equation}
with no new parameter (I8, derived up to an integer in \S36.14). Varying $\lambda$ holds $X=X_0$; varying $\theta$ gives the conserved
flow $\nabla_\mu(\lambda\,\partial^\mu\theta)=0$, so $\lambda\propto a^{-3}$; on the constraint $T_{\mu\nu}=2\lambda\,\partial_\mu\theta\,\partial_\nu\theta$,
pressureless dust:
\begin{equation}
\rho_{\rm rot}=2\lambda X_0\propto a^{-3},\qquad p=0,\qquad c_s^2=0\ \text{on every background}.
\end{equation}
Its amount is a constant of integration: dark matter without a particle (mimetic dark matter \cite{ChamseddineMukhanov2013}), with
cold-dark-matter perturbations. The constraint vanishes on its solutions, adding no vacuum energy: dark energy remains space, the
capacity energy of \S48.10. The kinetic function $K(X-X_0)^2$ \cite{Scherrer2004} is the same physics with finite stiffness,
\eqref{eq:rot-constraint} its rigid limit. Without the constraint the scalar's own kinetic function, $P=f^2X+f^4X^2/8$ with the radial
mode integrated out ($f$ its normalization), has its only extremum at a spacelike gradient, $X=-4/f^2$, where $\Xi=\phi_+\phi_-=0$: the
constraint is what holds the time rotation.

\sI\ The dust is the cosmic share $\Omega_c$ of the budget; the Boltzmann fit of \S26.7 treats it as pressureless dust of zero sound
speed.

\sD\ \emph{Dwarf profiles.} Around a cored baryonic system the medium's effective density follows from the law alone, with no Jeans
length. For a Plummer sphere (mass $M$, scale $b$) in the deep regime, $g=\sqrt{a_0g_N}$ and $4\pi G(\rho_b+\rho_{\rm ph})=r^{-2}\,d(r^2g)/dr$
give
\begin{equation}
\rho_{\rm ph}\simeq\frac{5}{8\pi}\sqrt{\frac{Ma_0}{G}}\;b^{-3/2}\,r^{-1/2}\quad(r\ll b),\qquad
\rho_{\rm ph}\simeq\frac{1}{4\pi}\sqrt{\frac{Ma_0}{G}}\;r^{-2}\quad(r\gg b):
\label{eq:dwarf-profile}
\end{equation}
a cusp of logarithmic slope $-\tfrac12$ inside the baryonic core, isothermal outside, in place of the Jeans-length cores of \S46.5.

\sD\ \emph{One quantity at two scales.} The dust is the modulation the baryons tune ($\delta\omega_b$, \S49.2): unbound, cold dust; bound
in a galaxy, a polarization of permittivity $\mu$ (\S46.6) whose effective density is the phantom density of \S46 (\S49.11); bound in a
cluster, the infall share of \S47. Pressureless, it follows the galaxies through a cluster collision and leaves the gas, each stream a
branch of the multivalued phase. Dust and vacuum are spacetime excited and unexcited, two states of the spin's quanta; what lensing
infers as dark matter is the dilation (\S53.4), and the Bullet Cluster's offset follows from the dust's geodesic flow (\S53.6).

\subsection*{23.2\quad What this means for the missing mass}
No dark particle, no new force: the extra gravity is the universe's rotation, turning everywhere with zero windings, modulated by the
baryons and bound around them (\S23.1, \S49.11).

\subsection*{23.3\quad What this is}
General relativity with a scalar whose phase turns at a fixed rate; the carrier of the extra gravity is the universe's own rotation,
which the baryons tune rather than generate.

\subsection*{23.4\quad The dark-sector sections, read through the rotation}
The condensate of Sections~\ref{sec:DM}, \ref{sec:complete} and~\ref{sec:condensation} is the rotation's modulation held
by~\eqref{eq:rot-constraint}, not a population of particles: their equations stand with the rotation as carrier, and the capture and
relaxation of \S47 are the bound medium's. The radial mode keeps its mass $m_\delta$ (\S37) as an excitation of the field.

\section{The Three Currencies}\label{sec:currencies}
% =====================================================================

\subsection*{24.1\quad Mass, energy, information}
With $F=c^4/G$, $r=c/H_0$ and $N$ the horizon's cell count,
\begin{equation}
\MH=\frac{Fr}{2c^2}\ (\propto r),\qquad \EH=\frac{Fr}{2}\ (\propto r),\qquad
I=\frac N2=\frac{\pi r^2}{\lP^2}\ (\propto r^2).
\end{equation}

\subsection*{24.2\quad Exchange rates}
The horizon has the Gibbons--Hawking temperature \cite{GibbonsHawking1977}, $k_B\TGH=\hbar c/2\pi r=\hbar H_0/2\pi$
($\TGH=2.653\times10^{-30}$ K), and energy and information exchange at exactly that rate:
\begin{equation}
k_B\TGH\,I=\frac{\hbar c}{2\pi r}\cdot\frac{\pi r^2c^3}{\hbar G}=\frac{c^4r}{2G}=\EH .
\label{eq:ETS}
\end{equation}
So $\EH/\MH=c^2$ and $\EH/I=k_B\TGH=3.662\times10^{-53}$ J per nat; a bit costs $k_B\TGH\ln2=2.539\times10^{-53}$ J, Landauer's
erasure energy \cite{Landauer1961} at the horizon temperature, of mass $2.82\times10^{-70}$ kg.

\sS\ For the de Sitter horizon the exchange law is holographic equipartition \cite{Padmanabhan2010}: the Komar energy $2\EH$ equals
$\tfrac12N_{\rm sur}k_B\TGH$ with $N_{\rm sur}=4\pi r^2/\lP^2=2N$. GRUT reads it as the exchange rate between mass and information.

\subsection*{24.3\quad The cone rate}
The TRSD rate $\hbar H_0/8\pi$ is the one-cone value $k_B\TGH/4$, matching $M_{\rm cone}=\MH/4$.

\subsection*{24.4\quad Consistency}
\begin{equation}
\MH c^2=\EH=k_B\TGH\,I .
\end{equation}
Mass, energy and information are one quantity in three units, exchanged at $c^2$ and at the horizon temperature. The exchange is the
horizon's spin: $\EH=\omega L_{\rm H}$ with $\omega=c/r$ and $L_{\rm H}=c^3r^2/2G$; $k_B\TGH=\hbar\omega/2\pi$ and $I=2\pi L_{\rm H}/\hbar$
(\S36.11).

\section{The Cosmic Budget}\label{sec:budget}
% =====================================================================

\subsection*{25.1--25.3\quad The $C_3$ budget}
\sI\ The baryon ratio is TRSD's product $\Omega_b/\Omega_c=\alpha_{\rm TRSD}\,n$ of the acceleration coefficient
$\alpha_{\rm TRSD}=\sqrt3/\pi^2$ ($a_0=\alpha_{\rm TRSD}cH_0$, \S22.5) and the Torridity exponent $n$: $\sqrt3/3\pi=0.18378$ at $n=\pi/3$,
$0.18697$ as derived (\S25.7). The dark ratio was taken from the $C_3$ geometry too (I5), as the defect power ratio
$\Omega_\Lambda/\Omega_c=C_3/C_2=5/2$ (\S39.3). \emph{That power ratio is withdrawn} (\S52.6): the CMB spectra disfavour it at $4.6\sigma$
(\S25.9), the dynamics leaves the dark amount free (\S23.1), and the resonance gives $2.580$ (\S25.11).

\sD\ \emph{The dark sectors by axial charge.} The three-defect source (\S39.3) admits at lowest order the quadrupole $\ell=2$, $m=0$,
invariant under every rotation about the axis, and the octupole $\ell=3$, $m=\pm3$, invariant only under $C_3$. The internal rotation is a
continuous symmetry, so the cosmic share is the axial quadrupole; the three-arc cells of space carry the discrete pattern, so the vacuum
is the triangular octupole. The assignment fixes harmonics, not densities.

\subsection*{25.4\quad Matter as the frame}
By the partition~\eqref{eq:partition}, matter holds the fraction $\Omega_m$ of the horizon radius and the vacuum holds the rest.

\subsection*{25.5\quad Totals}
$M_{\rm total}=\MH/4=c^3/8GH_0$, $E_{\rm total}=\EH/4=c^5/8GH_0$, and $N=2\pi c^5/\hbar GH_0^2$: the one-cone values.

\subsection*{25.7\quad The baryon ratio}
A derivation must tie baryons, which carry $\mathbb Z_3$ triality, to the cosmic share. Four mechanisms fail (at $\Omega_m\approx\tfrac13$):
(i) the baryons' phantom mass at the horizon, $M_b/\mu$ at the load $g/a_c=\Omega_m/2k_3=1.27$ ($a_c=\tfrac34a_0$, $k_3=3\sqrt3/4\pi^2$),
gives $\Omega_b/\Omega_c\approx0.9$--$1.0$, five times too large: cosmic dark matter is not horizon-scale phantom mass, as the CMB's third
peak (a component clustering before recombination) also says; (ii) deep-MOND scaling, $M_{\rm D}^2=a_0r^2M_b/G$, gives
$\Omega_c^2=2\alpha_{\rm TRSD}\Omega_b$ and $0.595$; (iii) equal number densities need a $5$ GeV particle, not the ultralight field of \S37;
(iv) equipartition over the three $C_3$ modes gives $1{:}2$.

\sD\ \emph{(v) The dynamic exponent at the horizon's own load.} The exponent runs with the acceleration (\S22.9); the universe reads it
at its horizon's load. By the horizon condition $r=2G\MH/c^2$ (\S10.2), with $r=ct_H$ on the seed cone,
\begin{equation}
g_H=\frac{G\MH}{r^2}=\frac{c^2}{2r}=\frac{r}{2t_H^2},\qquad z_H=\frac{g_H}{a_0}=\frac{\pi^2}{2\sqrt3}=2.8491 ,
\end{equation}
a pure number in every epoch because the scale tracks the horizon, $a_0\propto H$ (\S36.15), as the rising scale of high-redshift
rotation curves requires (a scale fixed by the spin would make $z_H$ run).

\sI\ Reading the exponent there, the dynamic law of \S22.9 gives $n(z_H)=1.06540$ and
\begin{equation}

\label{eq:baryon-ratio}
\end{equation}
against Planck's $\omega_b/\omega_c=0.1864\pm0.0022$ ($+0.3\sigma$; $\sqrt3/3\pi$ lay at $-1.2\sigma$). Nothing is fitted: the load is the
horizon condition, the exponent the cascade's, the ratio TRSD's product. Both components are made at the conformal reset from one
boundary energy, by defect nucleation and pair production, so the ratio must also be that energy's branching between the radial (neutral)
and winding (charged) channels (\S25.8); until that is computed, a data-driven alternative such as $3\sqrt6/4\pi^2=0.18614$
(Appendix~\ref{app:numbers}) has equal standing.

\subsection*{25.8\quad The baryon ratio from the reset}
\sD\ The rotation cannot supply the asymmetry: as a chemical potential for baryon number through the anomaly,

\begin{equation}

\end{equation}
$+0.7\sigma$ in $\omega_b$ from Planck's $6.13\times10^{-10}$ and $+0.4\sigma$ from deuterium burning's $\omega_b=0.02233\pm0.00036$
\cite{Mossa2020}. The $5/2$ budget's $+2.5\sigma$ in $\omega_b$ was the dark ratio's.

\sD\ Sakharov's conditions hold without a new coupling: the reset is out of equilibrium (\S45); the electroweak anomaly (charges in \S31)
violates baryon number, sphalerons turning a lepton asymmetry into $B=\tfrac{28}{79}(B-L)$; CP violation enters through the mixing matrix
(\S42), its sign set by the two-C/S handedness (\S39.4), the circulant phase $\delta_\ell$ (\S42.7) carrying none (conjugation,
$\delta_\ell\to-\delta_\ell$, only relabels the generations).

\sI\ The reset's branching realizes the asymmetry: a lepton asymmetry made out of equilibrium and converted by sphalerons, in the
budget's amount and with the ratio of \S25.7(v).

\sO\ Open is the computation of the branching: Bogoliubov coefficients of the boundary action with the three-defect boundary condition
and the radial mode's amplitude (\S37.7), with the Majorana masses' lepton-number violation (\S42.4) and the mixing's CP phase (\S42.5),
out of equilibrium. No prediction waits on it.
