\item \emph{Neutrinoless double beta decay.} The normal branch lies below the reach of
next-generation experiments. A detection between $5$ and $17$ meV falsifies both
branches.
\end{itemize}
The normal-branch neutrino density, $\Omega_\nu h^2=\sum m_\nu/93.14\ \mathrm{eV}
=6.35\times10^{-4}$, is the value already used in the $H_0$ derivation of \S26.7, so
the lepton and cosmology sectors of GRUT close on each other.

\subsection*{42.5\quad Prediction 3: the mixing column}
In flavour space the radial directions of the three generators are
$(2,-1,-1)/\sqrt6$, $(-1,2,-1)/\sqrt6$ and $(-1,-1,2)/\sqrt6$, one per charged lepton.
\hyp (H2) The conjugate-branch neutrino $\nu_1$---the one with $\sqrt{m_1}<0$ in
\S42.4---points along the electron generator's radial direction:
\begin{equation}
(|U_{e1}|,|U_{\mu1}|,|U_{\tau1}|)=\frac{1}{\sqrt6}\,(2,1,1).
\end{equation}
This is the tri-maximal TM1 pattern \cite{AlbrightRodejohann2009}. It arises
naturally from the $A_4$ symmetry of the tetrahedron \cite{AltarelliFeruglio2005},
which is GRUT's closure group. From $|U_{e1}|^2=\cos^2\theta_{12}\cos^2\theta_{13}=\tfrac23$,
\begin{equation}
\sin^2\theta_{12}=1-\frac{2}{3\cos^2\theta_{13}}=0.3180 ,
\label{eq:tm1}
\end{equation}
with $\sin^2\theta_{13}=0.02248$ (NuFIT~6.1): $+1.4\sigma$ from the global
$0.3088\pm0.0067$ \cite{NuFIT61} and $+1.0\sigma$ from JUNO's $0.3092\pm0.0087$ \cite{JUNO2026}. From $|U_{\mu1}|^2=\tfrac16$ the CP phase
is tied to $\theta_{23}$:
\begin{equation}
\cos\delta_{\rm CP}=\frac{\tfrac16-\sin^2\theta_{12}\cos^2\theta_{23}-\cos^2\theta_{12}\sin^2\theta_{23}\sin^2\theta_{13}}
{2\sin\theta_{12}\cos\theta_{12}\sin\theta_{23}\cos\theta_{23}\sin\theta_{13}} .
\label{eq:tm1delta}
\end{equation}
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccccc@{}}
\toprule
$\sin^2\theta_{23}$ & $0.45$ & $0.47$ & $0.50$ & $0.53$ & $0.56$ & $0.59$ \\
\midrule
$|\delta_{\rm CP}|$ & $102.6^\circ$ & $97.5^\circ$ & $90.0^\circ$ & $82.5^\circ$ & $74.8^\circ$ & $66.6^\circ$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Maximal $\theta_{23}$ forces maximal CP violation. The T2K+NOvA value
$\sin^2\theta_{23}=0.56^{+0.03}_{-0.05}$ \cite{T2KNOvA2025} gives
$|\delta_{\rm CP}|\approx75^\circ$. At the NuFIT~6.1 value $\sin^2\theta_{23}=0.470$ the
relation gives $\delta_{\rm CP}=262.5^\circ$ (or $97.5^\circ$); the global normal-ordering fit,
$212^{+26}_{-36}$ degrees \cite{NuFIT61}, lies $1.9\sigma$ from the first.

The other geometric alignment---$\nu_2$ along the cone axis $(1,1,1)/\sqrt3$, the TM2
pattern---gives $\sin^2\theta_{12}=0.341$ and is excluded at $4.8\sigma$ by NuFIT~6.1. The
generator direction, not the axis, is the one that survives.

\emph{Tests.} JUNO's sub-percent measurement of $\sin^2\theta_{12}$
against~\eqref{eq:tm1}. DUNE and Hyper-Kamiokande on the $(\theta_{23},\delta_{\rm CP})$
correlation~\eqref{eq:tm1delta}. A value of $\sin^2\theta_{12}$ more than $3\sigma$ from
$0.318$, or a $(\theta_{23},\delta_{\rm CP})$ pair off the curve, falsifies the
alignment.

\subsection*{42.6\quad What is new}
Koide's relation \cite{Koide1983}, its reading as a $45^\circ$ cone \cite{Foot1994}, the
neutrino negative root \cite{Brannen2006} and tri-maximal TM1 mixing
\cite{XingZhou2007,AlbrightRodejohann2009} are prior results. GRUT contributes four
things:
\begin{itemize}\itemsep0pt
\item the identification of Foot's $45^\circ$ cone with the spacetime seed cone
$h=r$ of \S1.10: one primitive acting in two spaces;
\item the reading of the negative root as the conjugate lobe of the pair (\S1.8),
which makes it required rather than chosen: with every root positive, $Q_\nu$ cannot
exceed $0.585$ (normal) or $0.500$ (inverted), so the seed cone forces the conjugate
branch;
\item hypothesis H2, which ties the two neutrino facts together---the
conjugate-branch state lies along the electron generator---so that the mass spectrum and
the TM1 column stand or fall together;
\item the joint prediction set with 2025--26 inputs (JUNO, NuFIT~6.1, PDG~2024), each
member with a falsification condition in Appendix~\ref{app:register}.
\end{itemize}
The lepton predictions share no fitted parameter with the cosmological ones; they share
the primitives. They stand or fall together with \S26.7 only in that sense.

% =====================================================================
\subsection*{42.7\quad The charged-lepton triangle and the quark cones}
\sM\ Fitting $\sqrt{m_k}=\mu_\ell\,[1+\sqrt2\cos(\delta_\ell+2\pi k/3)]$ to the pole masses
gives $\delta_\ell=0.222226\pm0.000006$ (the error from $m_\tau$) and
$\mu_\ell^2=313.851\pm0.013$ MeV: $\delta_\ell=2/9$ holds at $0.7\sigma$. Koide's ratio is
$Q=0.6666645$ for the pole masses and $0.6679287$ for the running masses at $M_Z$
\cite{XingZhangZhou2012}. \sD\ The ratio is invariant under a flavour-universal rescaling
$m_k\to Zm_k$, so universal running preserves it; QED running depends on flavour through
$\ln(\mu/m_k)$ and breaks it at $O(\alpha)$. The cone is a statement about physical masses, which
is why pole masses obey it. For the quarks, with the PDG masses \cite{PDG2024}, the triplet
$(c,b,t)$ gives $Q=0.6692$, on the cone to $0.4\%$, within the scheme dependence of quark masses;
$(d,s,b)$, $(u,c,t)$, $(u,d,s)$ and $(s,c,b)$ give $0.731$, $0.849$, $0.567$ and $0.458$.
\sD\ The phase is fixed far more sharply by the electron and muon than by the $\tau$. With the
cone's amplitude $\sqrt2$ exact, $m_e$ and $m_\mu$ give $\delta_\ell=0.222\,222\,047(1)$, that is $2/9-1.75\times10^{-7}$:
within $8\times10^{-7}$ of $2/9$, but $450\sigma$ from it, so $Q=\tfrac23$ and $\delta_\ell=\tfrac29$ cannot both be exact.
With $\delta_\ell=2/9$ exact instead, the amplitude is $1.414\,213\,26$ and $Q=\tfrac23(1-2.1\times10^{-7})$. Both
branches give $m_\tau=1776.97$ MeV, $0.4\sigma$ from $1776.93(9)$ \cite{PDG2024}. The cone with $3\delta_\ell=Q$ fixes
the masses from one scale: with both relations exact, $m_\mu/m_e=206.7703$ against the measured $206.7683$,
$10$ parts per million, and $m_\tau/m_e=3477.47$ against $3477.37\pm0.18$. The two numbers hold together to
$2\times10^{-7}$, and one of them carries a correction of that size; a phase landing that close to a simple
fraction by chance has a probability near $10^{-5}$. They are one statement. Since
$\sum_k\cos^3(\delta+2\pi k/3)=\tfrac34\cos3\delta$, the cubic invariant of the masses fixes
$\cos3\delta_\ell=(\sqrt2/3)\sum_kx_k^3$ with $x_k=\sqrt{m_k}/\mu_\ell-1$, and the observed pair is
\begin{equation}
3\,\delta_\ell=Q=\tfrac23 :
\end{equation}
the cubic invariant's angle equals the seed cone's ratio. \sI\ With the cone (I6) the relation gives
$\delta_\ell=2/9$; the relation itself is identified. The scale, $\mu_\ell^2=313.856$ MeV from $m_e$ and $m_\mu$, is the
lepton sector's input: no combination of the framework's scales tested---$m_p/3$, $\alpha\,\alpha_{\rm TRSD}v_{\rm EW}$,
$m_W/256$---matches it within its error.

% =====================================================================
\subsection*{42.8\quad The cone is the vacuum}
\sD\ The seed condition in generation space follows from the vacuum of the axiom. The three generators carry the phases
$\omega^k=e^{2\pi ik/3}$ on the unit circle (\S15.3), and the conjugate pair $\Phi=\phi\,e^{i\theta}$ lies in the same plane, in the units in
which the potential is $V(\Xi)=\tfrac12(\Xi-2)^2$ with $\Xi=|\Phi|^2$ (\S37.2). Let each generation's amplitude be the rise of its generator's
invariant when the pair is added to it,
\begin{equation}
A_k=|\omega^k+\Phi|^2-|\omega^k|^2=\Xi+2\sqrt\Xi\,\cos\!\Big(\theta-\frac{2\pi k}{3}\Big),\qquad k=0,1,2 .
\label{eq:dressed}
\end{equation}
The two invariants of the triple are then fixed by $\Xi$ alone, $\sum_kA_k=3\Xi$ and $\sum_kA_k^2=3\Xi^2+6\Xi$, so with
$\sqrt{m_k}\propto A_k$
\begin{equation}
\boxed{Q=\frac{\sum_kA_k^2}{\big(\sum_kA_k\big)^2}=\frac{\Xi+2}{3\Xi}},
\label{eq:Q-Xi}
\end{equation}
and $Q=\tfrac23$ exactly when $\Xi=2$: \emph{Koide's relation is the vacuum.} The parametrization~\eqref{eq:koidepar} follows with its
amplitude $\sqrt2=2/\sqrt{\Xi_0}$, its scale $\mu\propto\Xi_0$, and its phase $\delta=\theta$, the orientation of the pair against the
generators.

\emph{The geometry.} The circle $|\Phi|=\sqrt2$ is the director circle of the generators' unit circle: from each of its points the unit
circle subtends a right angle, the apex angle of the seed cone $h=r$ (\S3). Foot's $45^\circ$ and the vacuum $\Xi_0=2$ of the potential are
one number: the vacuum sees the generators under the seed's angle. $A_k$ is the power of the point $\Phi$ with respect to the unit circle
centred at $-\omega^k$, negative when $\Phi$ lies inside that circle; the negative root that the neutrinos require (\S42.4) is the pair
inside the circle of one generator, the conjugate branch read in the plane.

\sM\ Read backwards, Koide's ratio measures the pair's invariant, $\Xi=2/(3Q-1)$. The charged leptons give
\begin{equation}
\Xi_\ell=2.000013\pm0.000031 ,
\end{equation}
the vacuum to $1.5$ parts in $10^5$ ($0.4\sigma$, the error from $m_\tau$). The families that carry triality sit off it: $(c,b,t)$ at
$\Xi=1.985$, $(d,s,b)$ at $1.676$ and $(u,c,t)$ at $1.293$, within the scheme dependence of quark masses.

\sS\ Mechanisms proposed for Koide's relation place the masses in the vacuum structure of a flavour scalar \cite{Koide2008} or protect the
relation with a family gauge symmetry \cite{Sumino2009}; the geometric readings are Foot's cone \cite{Foot1994} and Kocik's configurations of circles crossing
at $\arccos\tfrac23$ \cite{Kocik2012}. \sD\ Here the potential is the axiom's own and the minimum is its vacuum. \sI\ What I6 now identifies
is~\eqref{eq:dressed}, the amplitude of a generation as its generator's invariant raised by the pair; the null condition of \S42.1 is no longer
assumed. The phase $\delta$ is a relative angle: mass ratios constant to $10^{-16}$ per year require it fixed, so the generators turn with the
pair, and the universe's rotation enters the masses only as their common phase, which the chiral rotation of \S49.12 removes and which gives
$\kappa$. \sO\ The relation $3\delta_\ell=Q$ (\S42.7) and its $2\times10^{-7}$ correction are not explained by~\eqref{eq:dressed}: in this form
the branch with $\delta_\ell=2/9$ exact places the charged leptons $4.2\times10^{-7}$ above the vacuum, $\Xi_\ell=2(1+4.2\times10^{-7})$, and the
branch with the cone exact leaves the phase $1.75\times10^{-7}$ below $2/9$.

% =====================================================================
\section{Causal Time and the Electric Coupling}\label{sec:causal}
% =====================================================================
This section answers the requests of working notes 42A--42C. Those notes ask four
questions:
\begin{enumerate}
\item Do the existing primitives produce a bounded causal-time variable $\Theta$?
\item Does the inward/outward momentum picture have an invariant form?
\item Does radiation pressure follow from the electromagnetic stress tensor?
\item Does the same geometry fix $\alpha$?
\end{enumerate}
The first three are answered below from the primitives and established relativity.
The fourth is stated with its exact target.

\subsection*{43B.1\quad The time-rate function}
For a particle of mass $m$ and energy $E$ in a local inertial frame, $d\tau^2=-ds^2/c^2$
and the mass shell (\S19) give
\begin{equation}
\Theta\equiv\frac{d\tau}{dt}=\frac{mc^2}{E}=\sqrt{1-\frac{v^2}{c^2}}=\operatorname{sech}\zeta ,
\label{eq:theta}
\end{equation}
where $\zeta$ is the rapidity, the hyperbolic angle between the worldline and the time
axis. Hence $0<\Theta\le1$ for $m>0$, with $\Theta=1$ at rest. On the seed cone itself
($m=0$, $\zeta\to\infty$), $\Theta=0$. So $\Theta$ measures how deep a worldline lies
inside the seed cone. The front of the defect (sound) cone, speed $c/\sqrt3$, sits at
$\Theta=\sqrt{2/3}$.

\subsection*{43B.2\quad The unified expression}
Write a static spacetime as $ds^2=-N^2c^2dt^2+h_{ij}dx^idx^j$. Here $t$ is the Killing
time normalized at infinity, which serves as the reference time $t_{\rm ref}$, and $N$
is the lapse.

Consider a particle moving at speed $v$ relative to the local static observer. That
observer's clock advances by $N\,dt$, and the particle covers $d\ell=vN\,dt$, so
$ds^2=-N^2c^2dt^2(1-v^2/c^2)$ and
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\ \Theta=\frac{d\tau}{dt}=N\sqrt{1-\frac{v^2}{c^2}}=N\,\frac{mc^2}{E_{\rm loc}}
=N^2\,\frac{mc^2}{E_\infty}\ }
\label{eq:thetaunified}
\end{equation}
Here $E_{\rm loc}$ is the energy measured by the static observer, and $E_\infty=NE_{\rm loc}$
is the conserved Killing energy. For static observers the product of the kinematic and
gravitational factors is therefore exact, not an assumption.

The four requested limits follow:
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lll@{}}
\toprule
Limit & Condition & $\Theta$ \\
\midrule
special relativity & $N=1$ & $mc^2/E=\sqrt{1-v^2/c^2}$ \\
gravitational time dilation & $v=0$ & $N=\sqrt{1-2GM/rc^2}$ \\
null trajectory & $m=0$ & $0$ \\
horizon & $N\to0$ & $0$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
No new object is introduced. The seed cone supplies $\operatorname{sech}\zeta$, the mass
shell supplies $mc^2/E$, and the horizon condition supplies $N$ through
$r_s=2GM/c^2=2Mc^2/F$.

\subsection*{43B.3\quad Two horizons and the balance radius}
Add the vacuum of \S26.4 ($w=-1$, $\Lambda=3\Omega_\Lambda H_0^2/c^2$). Static observers
around a mass $M$ then have the Schwarzschild--de Sitter lapse
\begin{equation}
N^2=1-\frac{r_s}{R}-\frac{R^2}{r_\Lambda^2},\qquad r_\Lambda=\frac{r}{\sqrt{\Omega_\Lambda}} .
\end{equation}
$\Theta$ vanishes at both horizons: the black-hole horizon, which is the inward
endpoint, and the cosmic horizon, which is the outward endpoint. It is largest where
$dN^2/dR=r_s/R^2-2R/r_\Lambda^2=0$:
\begin{equation}
R_*=\Big(\frac{r_sr_\Lambda^2}{2}\Big)^{1/3}=\Big(\frac{3GM}{\Lambda c^2}\Big)^{1/3},\qquad
\Theta_{\max}=\sqrt{1-3\Big(\frac{r_s}{2r_\Lambda}\Big)^{2/3}} .
\label{eq:Rstar}
\end{equation}
At $R_*$ the inward pull of the mass and the outward push of the vacuum cancel, and a
static observer there is in free fall. This is the invariant home of the inward/outward
balance.

$R_*$ is the maximum turnaround radius \cite{PavlidouTomaras2014}: no structure of mass
$M$ stays bound beyond it. For $10^{12}M_\odot$, $R_*=1.12$ Mpc with the budget ($1.11$ Mpc with Planck's $\Lambda$),
comparable to the Local Group's zero-velocity
surface.

\subsection*{43B.4\quad Momentum direction}
The sign of $p^{\hat r}$ depends on the observer. The invariant replacement is the
expansion $\theta_+$ of the outgoing radial null congruence orthogonal to the spheres of
area $4\pi R^2$. In ingoing Eddington--Finkelstein form, with the outgoing null normal
regular across the horizon,
\begin{equation}
\theta_+=\frac{N^2}{R} .
\end{equation}
Outward light increases the area where $\theta_+>0$. At each horizon $\theta_+=0$, and
inside a horizon $\theta_+<0$ (trapped).

Hypothesis H2 of the notes says the causal boundary occurs where the outward null
direction ceases to increase the radial coordinate. That is exactly the
marginally-trapped condition $\theta_+=0$. In a static spacetime it coincides with the
static-observer condition $\Theta=0$, since both reduce to $N^2=0$.

\subsection*{43B.5\quad Radiation pressure from the electromagnetic stress tensor}
Radially streaming radiation of luminosity $L$ crossing a sphere of radius $R$ is a
null fluid, $T^{\mu\nu}_{\rm EM}=\frac{L}{4\pi R^2c}\,k^\mu k^\nu$, with
$k^{\hat\mu}=(1,1,0,0)$ in the static observer's orthonormal frame. Its nonzero
components are
\begin{equation}
T^{\hat t\hat t}_{\rm EM}=T^{\hat t\hat r}_{\rm EM}=T^{\hat r\hat r}_{\rm EM}=\frac{L}{4\pi R^2c}\equiv\Phi_{\rm out}.
\end{equation}
The outward momentum flux of the notes is therefore the radial--radial component of the
Maxwell stress tensor for radially streaming light. It is exact in the local frame,
not a Newtonian analogy.

\subsection*{43B.6\quad The inward flux and the critical luminosity}
For a uniform sphere in Newtonian hydrostatic equilibrium, the central pressure is
$P_c=3GM^2/8\pi R^4=\tfrac32\Phi_{\rm in}$. So $\Phi_{\rm in}=GM^2/4\pi R^4$ is the
gravitational pressure scale. Balancing $\Phi_{\rm out}=\Phi_{\rm in}$ gives
\begin{equation}
L_{\rm crit}=\frac{GM^2c}{R^2}=P_{\max}\Big(\frac{r_s}{R}\Big)^2,\qquad P_{\max}=F_{\rm cone}\,c=\frac{c^5}{4G}.
\label{eq:Lcrit}
\end{equation}
At the horizon, $R=r_s$, the critical luminosity equals the maximum power allowed by
the axiom. No luminosity can exceed $P_{\max}$, so radiation cannot hold a body up at
its own horizon. The outward route closes exactly where the inward route closes. The brightest measured
source, GW150914, peaked at $0.4\%$ of $\Pmax$ \cite{LIGO2016}; on the maximum-luminosity
conjecture see \cite{Cardoso2018}.

\subsection*{43B.7\quad Dynamical closure versus causal closure}
A static star of uniform density has an exact interior solution (Schwarzschild). Its
central lapse and central pressure are
\begin{equation}
N_c=\frac32\sqrt{1-\frac{r_s}{R}}-\frac12,\qquad
\frac{P_c}{\rho c^2}=\frac{1-\sqrt{1-r_s/R}}{3\sqrt{1-r_s/R}-1}.
\end{equation}
Both reach their limit together. As $R\to\tfrac98r_s$, $P_c\to\infty$ and $N_c\to0$:
the central clock reaches $\Theta=0$ while the surface still has
$\Theta=\sqrt{1-8/9}=\tfrac13$. Buchdahl's theorem \cite{Buchdahl1959} extends this to
any static fluid whose density does not increase outward: no static configuration
exists below $R=\tfrac98r_s$.

So the two closures sit at different radii:
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{dynamical closure} (static support fails) at $R=\tfrac98r_s$;
\item \emph{causal closure} (horizon, $\theta_+=0$) at $R=r_s$.
\end{itemize}
They are related but not identical, as the notes anticipated. Between the two radii a
body can only collapse.

\subsection*{43B.8\quad How the clock stops}
Consider a body collapsing through $R=r_s$. A distant static observer receives its
signals with a rate that decays at late times as
\begin{equation}
\Theta_{\rm obs}\propto e^{-\kappa t},\qquad
\kappa=\frac{g_{\rm H}}{c}=\frac{c^3}{4GM}=\frac{F_{\rm cone}}{Mc} .
\label{eq:relax}
\end{equation}
The late-time law is therefore $d\Theta/dt=-(F_{\rm cone}/Mc)\,\Theta$: the external
clock freezes at a rate set by the one-cone force divided by the mass. For
$10M_\odot$ the e-folding time is $0.197$ ms.

The kinematic endpoint behaves the same way. A body with constant proper acceleration
$a$ has $\Theta=\operatorname{sech}(a\tau/c)\to2e^{-a\tau/c}$, and an accelerated
observer sees sources crossing its horizon redshift as $e^{-a\tau/c}$. Both endpoints
are approached exponentially at the rate (acceleration)$/c$: proper acceleration for
motion, surface gravity for gravity. That is the equivalence principle applied to the
two routes.

This answers the question $d\Theta/d\tau=\,?$ for the approach to each endpoint. The
full trajectory between the endpoints needs the field equations with matter: the TOV
equation for static bodies, or a collapse solution.

\subsection*{43B.9\quad The time-rate of a system}
For a composite system, $\Theta_{\rm sys}=M_{\rm inv}c^2/E_{\rm tot}$. A closed system
conserves its four-momentum, so $\Theta_{\rm sys}$ is constant; it changes only when
energy leaves the system.

Isotropic radiation keeps its invariant mass: a box of light weighs $E/c^2$. Only a
beam in a single direction has zero invariant mass. So ``radiating all rest mass as
light'' drives $\Theta_{\rm sys}\to0$ only for fully collimated emission, as in a photon
rocket. This refines the momentum partition of the notes.

\subsection*{43B.10\quad Status}
$\Theta$ is derived from existing primitives---the seed cone, the mass shell and the
horizon---and it reproduces special and general relativity exactly. It meets every
falsification criterion of note 42B.15: no new object, no new calibration, and the
correct limits.

This is the reinterpretation level of the notes, not yet a new prediction. The
quantitative content GRUT adds is:
\begin{itemize}
\item $L_{\rm crit}(r_s)=P_{\max}$, the axiom's power, equation~\eqref{eq:Lcrit};
\item the relaxation rate $F_{\rm cone}/Mc$, equation~\eqref{eq:relax};
\item the balance radius $R_*$ evaluated with GRUT's vacuum, equation~\eqref{eq:Rstar}.
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{43A/43C\quad The fine-structure conjecture}
\emph{Observation.} Let $S\equiv4\pi^3+\pi^2+\pi=137.036\,303\,776$. Against
$1/\alpha=137.035\,999\,177(21)$ (CODATA 2022 \cite{CODATA2022}),
\begin{equation}
\frac1\alpha=S-\delta,\qquad \delta=0.000\,304\,599 .
\end{equation}
The residual is subtracted: $S$ lies $2.22$ ppm above $1/\alpha$. Writing $\delta=K\alpha$
gives $K=0.041\,741\,012$. Because $1/\alpha$ is known to $1.5\times10^{-10}$, $K$ is
known to $7\times10^{-5}$ of itself, so a derivation must return
\begin{equation}
K=0.041741(3).
\end{equation}
The nearest simple fraction, $K=1/24$, gives $1/\alpha=137.035\,999\,720$, which is
$26\sigma$ away.

\emph{What a derivation must supply.}

(a) \emph{The coefficients.} The reading ``one $\pi$ per dimension'' does not match
geometry. Unit balls have volumes $2,\ \pi,\ 4\pi/3$ and unit spheres have areas
$2,\ 2\pi,\ 4\pi,\ 2\pi^2$; $\pi^3$ is not a three-dimensional volume coefficient. A
structural reading that does exist is
\begin{equation}
4\pi^3=|S^1|\cdot|S^3| ,
\end{equation}
the volume of the unit $S^1\times S^3$. Since the spatial section is flat and $S^3$ is the group manifold of $SU(2)$ (\S50.7),
$S^1\times S^3$ is the group manifold of $U(1)\times SU(2)$, the electroweak group of \S42.2, at unit
radii; it is also the Euclidean manifold of a thermal field at temperature $1/2\pi$. A spectral calculation on it---a heat-kernel
expansion, which yields a volume term followed by curvature corrections---naturally
produces a leading term with lower-order corrections. Whether it yields $+\pi^2+\pi$ and
then $K\alpha$ is the concrete calculation to do.

(b) \emph{The scale.} $\alpha$ runs, from $1/137.036$ at zero momentum transfer to about
$1/128$ at the $Z$ mass, because charged particles polarize the vacuum. A geometric
formula must say which scale it describes and reproduce the running from there; the running from
the zero-momentum value is \S31.5, so the formula must describe that value.

(c) \emph{The cost of looking.} Consider polynomials $a\pi^3+b\pi^2+c\pi+d$:
\begin{itemize}
\item with $a\le4$ and $|b|,|c|,|d|\le4$, a random target between $100$ and $200$ is
matched to $2.2$ ppm $0.5\%$ of the time;
\item with coefficients up to $6$, the rate is $3.8\%$.
\end{itemize}
Many other families have been tried. Wyler's formula matched $\alpha$ to $0.6$ ppm
before later measurements excluded it \cite{Wyler1969}. A $2.2$-ppm match is suggestive,
not evidential, until $K$ is derived.

\emph{The link to $\Theta$.} A photon has $\Theta=0$ exactly. The vacuum's static
observers have $\Theta_{\rm vac}=\sqrt{1-R^2/r_\Lambda^2}$ (\S43B.3). A ``mismatch''
between them becomes a number only through a coupling. The physical candidate is the
vacuum polarization of the photon on $S^1\times S^3$, which is the same calculation as in (a).

\emph{The spectral route, tested.} The decomposition is exact:
$4\pi^3+\pi^2+\pi=|S^1||S^3|+\tfrac12|S^3|+\tfrac12|S^1|$, bulk volume plus half of each
factor. The heat-kernel expansion does not produce it. On $S^1\times S^3$ with unit
radii, $\mathrm{Tr}\,e^{-t\Delta}=(4\pi t)^{-2}\sum_ka_kt^k$ with $a_0=\mathrm{Vol}$ and
$a_1=\tfrac16\int R$; since $R=6$ there, $a_1=a_0$, and every coefficient is proportional
to the same volume. The series generates powers of $t$, not the two lower-dimensional
volumes, and no weighting in it halves each factor. Nor does any simple constant reach
the target: $1/24$ is $26\sigma$ away, $1/8\pi$ and $\pi/75$ much further.

\emph{The calculated weight.} \sD\ The one-loop effective action on $S^1(2\pi)\times S^3(1)$ (\S43F) gives
the correction its thermal weight: $K=\tfrac1{24}\big(1+e^{-2\pi}\big)$, so
\begin{equation}
\frac1\alpha=4\pi^3+\pi^2+\pi-\frac{\alpha}{24}\big(1+e^{-2\pi}\big)=137.035\,999\,151\,7 .
\label{eq:alpha-closed}
\end{equation}
Every piece belongs to $S^1(2\pi)\times S^3(1)$: the volume and the two half factors; half the Casimir
coefficient of the $S^1$ factor, $\tfrac1{24}$; and the first thermal correction at the horizon temperature
$1/2\pi$, carried by the one conformally coupled scalar of the reset, the conformal factor of the gluing
between aeons (\S43F). Against CODATA 2022,
$137.035\,999\,177(21)$ \cite{CODATA2022}, it lies $1.2\sigma$ low; against the electron magnetic moment,
$137.035\,999\,166(15)$ \cite{Fan2023}, $0.95\sigma$ low; the atom-recoil values $137.035\,999\,206(11)$ (Rb)
\cite{Morel2020} and $137.035\,999\,046(27)$ (Cs) \cite{Parker2018}, which disagree with each other by $5.5\sigma$,
lie at $-4.9\sigma$ and $+3.9\sigma$. With every massless term at $\beta=2\pi$ the weight is $1.0197$ and
$1/\alpha=137.035\,999\,140\,5$, $1.7\sigma$ low.

\emph{The circle weight.} The weight $3/\pi=E_2(i)$, found by inspection in draft 37, returns
$137.035\,999\,177\,3$, $0.015\sigma$ from CODATA, but the calculation does not produce it: it is the circle
factor's own number, $-E_2(i)/24=-1/8\pi$ being the exact energy of a chiral boson on the self-dual torus. Its
match is a coincidence at the few-percent level. The two forms differ by $2.6\times10^{-8}$ in $1/\alpha$,
which the next generation of measurements resolves.

\emph{The half factors.} \sD\ A cone of deficit $\pi$ is the orbifold $\mathbb R^2/\mathbb Z_2$: the framework's deficit
is a $\mathbb Z_2$ identification. The electroweak group carries exactly one such $\mathbb Z_2$ between its factors,
$U(2)=(U(1)\times SU(2))/\mathbb Z_2$ with $\mathbb Z_2=\{(1,1),(-1,-1)\}$. Taken from each factor it leaves $U(1)/\mathbb Z_2$,
a circle of length $\pi=\tfrac12|S^1|$, and $SU(2)/\mathbb Z_2=SO(3)=\mathbb{RP}^3$, of volume $\pi^2=\tfrac12|S^3|$. Hence
\begin{equation}
S=\mathrm{vol}\big(U(1)\times SU(2)\big)+\mathrm{vol}\big(SO(3)\big)+\mathrm{vol}\big(U(1)/\mathbb Z_2\big)=4\pi^3+\pi^2+\pi :
\end{equation}
the electroweak group at unit radii and its two factors, each closed by the deficit $\pi$. \sD\ The coefficient $\tfrac1{24}$ is the generator defect's tension in units of the axiom force. The defect
map gives $f_\delta^2=\delta c^4/8\pi G$, so for $\delta_{\rm gen}=\pi/3$,
\begin{equation}
\frac{f_{\rm gen}^2}{F}=\frac{\delta_{\rm gen}}{8\pi}=\frac1{24}
\end{equation}
\cite{TRSD2026}; it coincides with the zero-point energy of one chiral boson on the unit circle, $c/24$ with $c=1$.
The correction is therefore built entirely from the framework, $K=(f_{\rm gen}^2/F)\,(1+e^{-2\pi})$: the generator
tension, dressed by the boundary's calculated thermal factor. \sI\ That $1/\alpha$ is the volume sum, and that
its correction is $\alpha$ times this dressed tension, are the identifications on which the relation rests. The paper's other predictions do not depend on it.

\subsection*{43D\quad The electroweak scale}
The hierarchy between the defect scale and the electroweak scale, with
$f_\delta=M_{\rm P}/\sqrt8=4.317\times10^{18}$ GeV and $v_{\rm EW}=246.22$ GeV, is
\begin{equation}
\Big(\frac{v_{\rm EW}}{f_\delta}\Big)^2=3.25\times10^{-33},\qquad
j=\log_4\!\Big(\frac{f_\delta^2}{v_{\rm EW}^2}\Big)=54.0 .
\end{equation}
Read as a cascade depth of $54$ levels at a factor of $4$ each, this is the measured
ratio rewritten in base $4$: any ratio returns some $j$, and the agreement is exact by
construction. It becomes a derivation only when a second condition fixes the depth---a
termination rule for the cascade, or another observable that lands at the same $j$.

A second gap sits in the same sector. The Higgs mass requires a quartic
$\lambda_H=m_H^2/2v_{\rm EW}^2=0.129$, while the potential of the axiom gives
$\lambda_\chi/4!=0.5$, a factor of $3.9$ away. The structure of the weak sector---$SU(2)$
from the two axes, chirality from the two lobes, a doublet Higgs and therefore
$\rho=1$---follows from the geometry (\S42.2); the scale and the quartic do not.


% =====================================================================
\subsection*{43E\quad The full trajectory of $\Theta$}
\sD\ Outside a collapsing dust ball the metric is Schwarzschild (Birkhoff), and the surface
falls from rest at $R_0$ on the cycloid $R=\tfrac{R_0}{2}(1+\cos\eta)$. A radial photon emitted at
$R$ reaches a distant observer at $t_{\rm obs}=t+[r_*(\infty)-r_*(R)]/c$, with
$r_*=r+r_s\ln|r/r_s-1|$, so the received clock rate is
\begin{equation}
\Theta_{\rm obs}(R)=\frac{d\tau}{dt_{\rm obs}}=\frac{1-r_s/R}{E+\sqrt{E^2-1+r_s/R}},\qquad
E=\sqrt{1-\frac{r_s}{R_0}} .
\label{eq:theta-full}
\end{equation}
It runs from $\Theta=E$ at the start---the static redshift, which tends to $1$ as
$R_0\to\infty$---to $\Theta=0$ at the horizon, approached as $(1-r_s/R)/2E\propto e^{-\kappa
t_{\rm obs}}$ with $\kappa=c/2r_s=c^3/4GM$, the rate of \S43B.8. For $R_0=10r_s$ the values at
$R/r_s=10,5,2,1.5,1.1,1.01$ are $0.949$, $0.632$, $0.316$, $0.196$, $0.049$ and $0.0052$, and the
late-time logarithmic slope is $-0.5000\,c/r_s$.

\subsection*{43F\quad The fine-structure constant}
\sM\ The natural spectral invariants of $S^1\times S^3$ at unit radii---the Casimir energies of
spin $0$, $\tfrac12$ and $1$ on $S^3$ ($\tfrac1{240}$, $\tfrac{17}{960}$, $\tfrac{11}{120}$),
$\zeta(-1)$, $\zeta(-3)$, and half the $S^1$ Casimir coefficient, $\tfrac1{24}$---do not return
$K=0.041741(3)$; the nearest, $\tfrac1{24}$, is $25\sigma$ away. Its residual,
$(K-\tfrac1{24})/\tfrac1{24}=(1.784\pm0.072)\times10^{-3}$ given the uncertainty of $K$, has the size of the
first thermal correction on $S^1_{2\pi}\times S^3$, $e^{-2\pi}=1.867\times10^{-3}$, from which it differs by
$1.2\sigma$: not significantly. \sI\ It equals $E_2(i)\,e^{-2\pi}=(3/\pi)\,e^{-2\pi}=1.7833\times10^{-3}$, where
$E_2(i)=3/\pi$ is the quasi-modular Eisenstein series at the self-dual point of the $S^1(2\pi)$ factor, so
\begin{equation}
K=\frac1{24}\Big[1+\frac3\pi\,e^{-2\pi}\Big]=0.0417410 ;
\end{equation}
the value $E_2(i)=3/\pi$ is exact, from the transformation $E_2(-1/\tau)=\tau^2E_2(\tau)+6\tau/\pi i$ at its fixed point
$\tau=i$. The relation of \S43A then returns $1/\alpha=137.035\,999\,177\,3$, $0.015\sigma$ from CODATA 2022
(equation~\eqref{eq:alpha-closed}). The precision of $K$ tests the thermal weight to $\pm3.7\%$: $3/\pi$ lies at
$0.015\sigma$, and the bare weight $1$, one conformal scalar's first term, at $1.2\sigma$. The conjecture is a low-energy statement: at $M_Z$, where
$1/\alpha=127.95$ \cite{PDG2024}, it would need $K=0.071$. \sD\ \emph{The calculation.} The one-loop effective action of the GRUT field content on
$S^1(2\pi)\times S^3(1)$---the conformal boundary at the horizon temperature---is $\ln Z=-2\pi E_c+\ln Z_{\rm th}$. On
$S^3(1)$ a term of order $e^{-2\pi}$ needs a mode with $\omega=1$, and only a massless conformally coupled scalar
has one, with degeneracy $1$: each such real scalar adds exactly one unit of $e^{-2\pi}$. Every other field
starts higher: a Weyl fermion at $\omega=\tfrac32$, the photon at $\omega=2$ (degeneracy $6$), the graviton at
$\omega=3$; the reset carrier of \S45, minimally coupled with $m^2/H^2\simeq-0.04$, at $\omega\simeq1.72$; the radial
mode, $m_\delta R=\sqrt8$, at $\omega=3$; and the phase, held at its rate by \eqref{eq:rot-constraint}, has zero
sound speed and no thermal modes. Without a conformal scalar the weight is zero, $K=\tfrac1{24}$, and the relation
misses by $26\sigma$; with two it misses by $28\sigma$. The calculation requires exactly one, and the reset
supplies exactly one: the conformal factor of the gluing between aeons, which in conformal cyclic cosmology
obeys the conformally invariant equation of a conformally coupled scalar with quartic self-coupling
\cite{Penrose2010}---the boundary field $\chi$ with $-\tfrac1{12}R\chi^2$ of the companion paper's terminal state
\cite{TRSD2026}. At $\beta=2\pi$ its full energy is exact,
\begin{equation}
E_\chi=\frac1{240}+\sum_{n\ge1}\frac{n^3}{e^{2\pi n}-1}=\frac{E_4(i)}{240}=\frac{\Gamma(\tfrac14)^8}{5120\,\pi^6}=0.006\,065\,679 ,
\end{equation}
and its complete thermal part, with the photon's, is $\ln Z_{\rm th}=(1.0084+0.0113)\,e^{-2\pi}$. The weight is
therefore $1$, not $3/\pi$, and the relation of \S43A gives $1/\alpha=137.035\,999\,151\,7$
(equation~\eqref{eq:alpha-closed}), $1.2\sigma$ from CODATA 2022.

\sD\ The mode that carries the weight, at frequency $c/R_\infty=H_\infty$ on the final horizon's three-sphere, is one
quantum of the spin (\S36.11), the quantum that each quantum of the rotation carries (\S36.14), and $e^{-2\pi}=e^{-\hbar\omega/k_BT_\infty}$ is
its Boltzmann weight at the horizon's temperature. The correction is the generator tension dressed by the occupation of one quantum of the
spin, $K=(f_{\rm gen}^2/F)\,(1+e^{-\hbar\omega/k_BT_\infty})$: both factors are quantities of the framework. \sI\ What remains identified is that the
volume sum is the inverse coupling at zero momentum; the calculation that would return it is open, and the weight $1$ against $3/\pi$ is
decided by $2.6\times10^{-8}$ in $1/\alpha$.

\section{TRSD and GRUT: the formal bridge}\label{sec:bridge}
% =====================================================================
TRSD and GRUT are one framework stated twice: TRSD as a variational principle, GRUT as
a geometry with a force axiom. This section proves the equivalence, gives the dictionary
that converts every TRSD quantity into the standardized $F$, and lists exactly what
remains an input after both axioms are in place.

\subsection*{44.1\quad The two axioms}
\textbf{Axiom G (GRUT).}
\begin{equation}
F\,G=c^4 ,
\end{equation}
with $F$ the force of the whole horizon sphere, together with the geometry of \S1--\S4:
the seed $h=r$ and the $C_3$ triangle.

\textbf{Axiom T (TRSD).}
\begin{equation}
\delta\!\int d^4x\sqrt{-g}\left[\frac{c^4R}{16\pi G}
-\frac{\delta c^4}{16\pi G}g^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\phi_+\partial_\nu\phi_-
-\varepsilon_VV(\Xi)\right]=0,\qquad \Xi=\phi_+\phi_- .
\end{equation}
Nothing else is assumed in either column. Every statement below is a consequence.

\subsection*{44.2\quad Bridge theorem B1: the dictionary}
\emph{Given Axiom G, every coefficient of Axiom T is a pure number times $F$, and every
TRSD quantity is a pure number times a monomial in $c$, $r$, $F$ and $\lP$.}

\begin{proof}
$G=c^4/F$ by Axiom G. Substituting into the two coefficients of Axiom T gives
$c^4/16\pi G=F/16\pi$ and $\delta c^4/16\pi G=\delta F/16\pi$. TRSD's normalization is
$f_\delta^2=\delta c^4/8\pi G=\delta F/8\pi$, and its Einstein coupling is
$8\pi G/c^4=8\pi/F$. Every other TRSD quantity is built from these with $c$, $r$ and
$\lP$ by the unit basis of \S0.2.
\end{proof}

\begin{center}
\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{@{}llll@{}}
\toprule
TRSD quantity & TRSD form & $F$-form & Value ($H_0=73.04$) \\
\midrule
normalization & $f_\delta^2=\delta c^4/8\pi G$ & $\delta F/8\pi$ & $F/8$ at $\delta=\pi$ \\
$f_\delta(\pi)$ & $\sqrt{c^4/8G}$ & $\sqrt{F/8}$ & $3.889\times10^{21}\ \sqrt{\mathrm{J/m}}$ \\
$f_\delta(\pi/3)$ & $\sqrt{c^4/24G}$ & $\sqrt{F/24}$ & $2.245\times10^{21}\ \sqrt{\mathrm{J/m}}$ \\
Einstein coupling & $8\pi G/c^4$ & $8\pi/F$ & $2.077\times10^{-43}$ N$^{-1}$ \\
maximum force & $c^4/4G$ & $F/4=F_{\rm cone}$ & $3.026\times10^{43}$ N \\
total mass & $c^3/8GH_0$ & $Fr/8c^2$ & $2.132\times10^{52}$ kg \\
total energy & $c^5/8GH_0$ & $Fr/8$ & $1.916\times10^{69}$ J \\
cell count & $2\pi r^2c^3/\hbar G$ & $2\pi Fr^2/\hbar c$ & $3.858\times10^{122}$ \\
information & $N/2$ & $\pi Fr^2/\hbar c$ & $1.929\times10^{122}$ nats \\
temperature & $\hbar H_0/2\pi k_B$ & $\hbar c/2\pi k_Br$ & $2.878\times10^{-30}$ K \\
dark energy & $\Omega_\Lambda\,3H_0^2c^2/8\pi G$ & $\Omega_\Lambda\,3F/8\pi c^2r^2$ & $5.24\times10^{-10}$ J\,m$^{-3}$ \\
defect length & $8\pi GM_*/c^2$ & $8\pi M_*c^2/F$ & $0.18$ pc at $1.5\times10^{11}M_\odot$ \\
critical acceleration & $3\sqrt3\,cH_0/4\pi^2$ & $3\sqrt3\,c^2/4\pi^2r$ & $9.340\times10^{-11}$ m\,s$^{-2}$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Only the labels change: TRSD's ``maximum force'' is the one-cone force $F/4$, and its
``total'' mass and energy are the one-cone quarters of the sphere values (\S17--\S18).
No numerical value in TRSD moves.

\subsection*{44.3\quad Bridge theorem B2: Axiom G follows from Axiom T}
\emph{Axiom T plus the closure of \S3.12 implies $FG=c^4$.}

\begin{proof}
Varying Axiom T with respect to $g^{\mu\nu}$ gives $G_{\mu\nu}+\Lambda g_{\mu\nu}
=(8\pi G/c^4)T_{\mu\nu}$ (\S30.1), whose static spherical vacuum solution is
Schwarzschild with $r_s=2GM/c^2$. Its horizon surface gravity is $g_{\rm H}=c^2/2r_s
=c^4/4GM$, so the force on the mass at its own horizon is $Mg_{\rm H}=c^4/4G$,
independent of $M$: this is $F_{\rm cone}$ (\S8.2). The closure $V_s=4V_c$ of \S3.12,
proved from the seed by Cavalieri, gives four such cones per sphere, so the sphere
carries $F=4F_{\rm cone}=c^4/G$, i.e.\ $FG=c^4$.
\end{proof}

\subsection*{44.4\quad Bridge theorem B3: Axiom T's geometric sector follows from Axiom G}
\emph{Axiom G plus \S\S1--4 fixes both coefficients of Axiom T; it does not fix the
potential.}

\begin{proof}
Lovelock's theorem in four dimensions (\S30.1) admits only $aG_{\mu\nu}+bg_{\mu\nu}$, so
the gravitational term is $\kappa^{-1}R$ with a single constant; Axiom G fixes it,
$\kappa^{-1}=c^4/16\pi G=F/16\pi$. The kinetic coefficient is the defect tension
$f_\delta^2/2=\delta F/16\pi$, with $\delta=\pi$ derived in \S4.4 from the three
equilateral faces and checked by the curvature closure $4\dtot=4\pi$. The potential
$V(\Xi)$ and its scale $\varepsilon_V$ are not fixed by Axiom G: the geometry constrains
the vacuum to be stable and non-degenerate but selects no particular function.
\end{proof}

So the bridge is exact in the geometric sector and one function short in the scalar
sector. The residual freedom is a single potential; of the two numbers it carries,
$\varepsilon_V$ is fixed by the dark-matter length (\S37.2) and $V_0$ vanishes (\S38.6).

\subsection*{44.5\quad The coil}
The coil of the TRSD prototype is the generator of the same geometry. A lobe is
\begin{equation}
\mathbf r_\pm(t)=\big(R_0\sin t\cos(\pm\omega t),\ R_0\sin t\sin(\pm\omega t),\ \pm pt\big),
\qquad t\in[0,\pi],
\end{equation}
pinching at $t=0,\pi$. Three statements follow, and one does not.

\emph{(i) The pair.} The two lobes are the conjugate pair $p_\pm$ of \S1.2, so the fold
and the double cover of \S1.8 apply unchanged.

\emph{(ii) The envelope fixes the pitch.} The bicone envelope has
$\tan\alpha=R_0/p$. The seed cone $h=r$ requires $\alpha=45^\circ$, hence $p=R_0$; the
defect cone requires $\alpha=30^\circ$, hence $p=\sqrt3\,R_0$ (\S4.4). The pitch-to-radius
ratio is therefore not free: it is $1$ for the causal cone and $\sqrt3$ for the defect
cone.

\emph{(iii) Three nodes.} The lobes meet where $\sin(\omega t)=0$, i.e.\ at
$t_n=n\pi/\omega$. Within $t\in[0,\pi]$ there are exactly three such crossings if and
only if $2\le\omega<3$.

\emph{(iv) What does not follow.} The azimuth of a crossing is $\omega t_n=n\pi$, so
consecutive crossings are separated by $\pi$ in azimuth for every $\omega$, not by
$2\pi/3$. The equal spacing of the three nodes is therefore not a consequence of the
crossing condition; it follows from the symmetry statement of \S4.2, that no node is
preferred, which is the same condition that makes the generating triangle equilateral.
The prototype's claim that the crossings are ``equally spaced in azimuth by $2\pi/3$''
should be replaced by this.

\subsection*{44.6\quad Bridge theorem B4: what remains an input}
\emph{With Axiom G and Axiom T both in place, the irreducible inputs are: two unit
conventions, one boundary datum, and the dimensionless constants that neither axiom
determines.}

\begin{proof}
By Theorem~\ref{thm:c-alone}, three dimensionful anchors are conventions. Axiom G
supplies one relation between $F$ and $G$, so one of the pair is fixed by the other, and
$c$ and $\hbar$ (equivalently $\lP$) remain as the two conventions. The scale $r$ is not
fixed by either axiom, since both are invariant under $r\to\lambda r$ with the
dimensionless content unchanged; one datum---$N$, equivalently $r_0/\lP$---must be
supplied. Every remaining quantity is a pure number times a monomial in $c$, $r$, $F$,
$\lP$ (B1), so anything not determined is a pure number.
\end{proof}

\begin{center}
\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{@{}lll@{}}
\toprule
Input & Why it is not derivable & Fixed by \\
\midrule
$c$, $\hbar$ & unit conventions (Theorem~\ref{thm:c-alone}) & SI definitions \\
$N$ (equivalently $H_0$) & scale invariance of both axioms & measurement (\S7); by $\alpha$ if H4 holds (\S48.12) \\
$\alpha$ & not in either axiom & measurement; \S43A/C target \\
$\varepsilon_V$ & tied to $L_*$ by normalization; $V_0=0$ & \S37.2, \S38.6 \\
$L_*$ (or $m_\delta$), $\phi_i$ & scalar sector scales & \S37.2, \S37.7 \\
$\alpha_s$ (the strong scale) & not in either axiom & measurement; tied to $N_\infty$ by the nucleon, \S50.8--50.10 \\
$v_{\rm EW}$, $\lambda_H$ & not in either axiom & measurement, \S43D \\
$\sin^2\theta_W$ & not in either axiom & measurement; the pair's closure, \S54.7 \\
$\mu_\ell$, $\mu_\nu$, $\delta_\nu$ & scales and phase of the lepton cones & $m_e$, $m_\mu$, the splittings; \S0.3, \S42 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The baryon ratio, an input in earlier versions, is derived in \S25.7(v), and the dark ratio, identified in earlier versions (I5), follows from
the resonance (\S25.11). That list, with the ledger of \S0.3, is complete: every other number in this paper is derived from the two axioms
and the geometry.

\subsection*{44.7\quad Conditions the bridge imposes on the prototype}
Keeping both columns formal requires five statements of the TRSD prototype to be
restated. Each is corrected in the sections named.
\begin{enumerate}
\item Quantities of the form (pure number)$\times10^{-10}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$---the
Torridity scales and their sequences---are unit-dependent and cannot be framework
statements (\S22.8).
\item The threshold parameter $\beta$ cancels in the normalized survival function, so
no value of it, including $4$, is fixed or needed (\S22.1).
\item $a_c=c/\tau_1$ is not a centripetal acceleration, whose value would be $2\pi$ larger (\S5); it is the
acceleration at which one activation occurs per cycle of the fundamental mode (\S22.10).
\item $\delta=8\pi GM/c^2$ is dimensionless only for a tension, $\mu=M/r$; the correct
form is $\delta(M)=4\pi M/\MH$ (\S18.1).
\item Horizon information is counted in nats; ``bits'' differs by $\ln2$ (\S20).
\end{enumerate}
With those five restatements, and the dictionary of \S44.2, the two columns are the same
theory: TRSD supplies the action, GRUT supplies the geometry and the force normalization,
and each implies the other by B2 and B3. B2 uses the four-cone reading I1.

\note The revised prototype, TRSD v2 \cite{TRSD2026}, implements the five restatements and
returns five results used here: the SPARC confrontation (\S22.6), the normalization
$\varepsilon_V=f_\delta^2/L_*^2$ (\S37.2), the physical self-coupling (\S37.3), the vanishing
of $V_0$ (\S38.6), and the reading of its flow equation as relaxation to the vacuum
$\Xi=2$. Because the two papers share these constructions, their agreement on them is
consistency, not independent confirmation (\S0.8).

% =====================================================================
\section{Primordial Spectrum from the Conformal Reset}
\label{sec:primordial-spectrum}
% ============================================================
The background GRUT budget fixes the horizon scale, the matter budget and the acoustic angle.
This section derives the primordial two-point function of the defect scalar from the reset:
the primary term from the terminal de Sitter phase of the previous aeon, the interaction term
from a chiral pair convolution on the $C_3$ cycle, and from both the exact scalar tilt and
running. Two numbers of the reset remain to be computed---the carrier's mass in units of the
terminal Hubble rate, which the tilt measures, and its conversion efficiency to curvature,
which the amplitude measures.

The standard dimensionless curvature spectrum is defined by
\begin{equation}
\left\langle\mathcal R_{\mathbf k}\mathcal R_{\mathbf k'}\right\rangle
=(2\pi)^3\delta^3(\mathbf k+\mathbf k')\frac{2\pi^2}{k^3}\Delta_{\mathcal R}^2(k),
\label{eq:curvature-two-point}
\end{equation}
so that $\langle\mathcal R^2\rangle=\int d\ln k\,\Delta_{\mathcal R}^2(k)$. A sum of terms
proportional to $k$ and $k^3$ cannot itself be a dimensionless primordial spectrum, because its
terms carry unequal dimensions. With the standard pivot
\begin{equation}
k_*=0.05\ {\rm Mpc}^{-1},\qquad x\equiv\frac{k}{k_*},
\label{eq:primordial-pivot}
\end{equation}
all powers below are dimensionless. The symbol $\nu$ is used for the spectral exponent because
$n$ is reserved for the exposure order of Section~\ref{sec:a0} and the defect dimension of
Section~\ref{sec:arrow}.

\subsection*{45.0\quad Completed thermodynamic and geometric reset closure}
\label{sec:reset_thermo_closure}
% =====================================================================
The reset is a closed thermodynamic--geometric transition when the following distinction is kept exact: the
old aeon does not undergo a global contraction of its physical scale factor. Before the junction,
$a=b^{-1}$ and $b\to0^+$, so $a\to\infty$; after the junction the new physical scale factor is
$\tilde a=|b|\to0^+$. Thus the reset is not a global compressive shock of the old aeon. It is a conformal
identification at which the old physical scale loses its operational meaning and the new aeon begins with a
vanishing physical scale. This is the geometry already fixed by~\eqref{eq:junc-metrics-closed} and
$\tilde H=1/(2\tilde\tau)$.

\subsection*{R1. Matter-sector conservation and thermodynamic closure}
On every regular matter branch let $N^\mu$ denote a conserved matter-number current and $T^{\mu\nu}_{\rm m}$
the total matter stress tensor. In the absence of a localized junction source, the finite-flux conditions are
\begin{equation}
 \nabla_\mu N^\mu=0,
 \qquad
 \nabla_\mu T^{\mu\nu}_{\rm m}=0,
 \qquad
 \boxed{[N^\mu n_\mu]_-^+=0},
 \qquad
 \boxed{[T^{\mu\nu}_{\rm m}n_\nu]_-^+=0} .
\label{eq:reset-matter-flux}
\end{equation}
These equations express material and energy--momentum continuity without requiring that every old composite
object survive as the same bound state. Stars, galaxies and black holes are not conserved objects; their conserved
charges and stress--energy are redistributed among the degrees of freedom of the thermal state.

Thermalization is represented by the entropy current $s^\mu$. The second law gives
\begin{equation}
 \nabla_\mu s^\mu=\sigma_s\ge0 .
\label{eq:reset-entropy-production}
\end{equation}
Consequently the reset may destroy macroscopic structure while preserving conserved currents and increasing
coarse-grained entropy. The radiation-dominated opening state is therefore a high-entropy thermal state, but it
is not identified with exact global maximum entropy or exact equilibrium: GRUT's reset is explicitly out of equilibrium
and its subsequent cooling and baryon asymmetry calculation rely on that fact.

For the relativistic plasma stage,
\begin{equation}
 p=\frac13\rho c^2,
 \qquad \rho=a_R T^4,
 \qquad s=\frac{4\rho}{3T},
 \qquad \tilde H=\frac1{2\tilde\tau},
\label{eq:reset-radiation-closure}
\end{equation}
so the new aeon has the required radiation-like equation of state and cools continuously as it expands. The
thermodynamic closure is therefore thermalization plus conservation plus the entropy inequality, not a postulated
instantaneous equilibrium.

\subsection*{R2. The collisionless phase survives the reset}
The phase sector is governed by the already closed junction theorem,
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\mathcal T_\chi=1},
 \qquad
 \boxed{\mathcal M_{\mathcal R}(k)=1\ \text{for the adiabatic superhorizon mode}},
 \qquad
 \boxed{\mathcal M_{\mathcal I}(k)=1} .
\label{eq:reset-transport-summary}
\end{equation}
Thus the phase current crosses the conformal junction without a junction-localized source, and the specified
superhorizon curvature correlations and conformal information modes are transmitted unchanged. The Bullet Cluster
provides the intended local dynamical analogy: collisional plasma is shock-heated while the gravitating component
is much less affected by drag. It is an analogy for the separation of collisional and collisionless sectors, not a
separate derivation of the cosmological junction.

\subsection*{R3. No additional Stückelberg transition is required}
The reset does not invoke a Stückelberg mass cancellation or restoration of a gauge $U(1)$. In the existing framework,
the dark-sector phase $\theta$ is a global phase/clock variable and carries no gauge field. The transition is instead
the conformal continuation
\begin{equation}
 g_{\mu\nu}=b^{-2}\hat g_{\mu\nu}
 \quad\longrightarrow\quad
 \tilde g_{\mu\nu}=b^2\hat g_{\mu\nu},
 \qquad b(\eta_c)=0,
\label{eq:reset-geometric-transition}
\end{equation}
with the same regular conformal geometry and $b'(\eta_c)=-\omega$. No new vector mass or gauge-symmetry-breaking
sector is introduced by the reset.

\subsection*{R4. Recombination and the next structure hierarchy}
After the reset, $\tilde a$ grows, the radiation density falls as $\tilde a^{-4}$, and the baryonic component cools.
The transferred adiabatic phase correlations provide the initial gravitational seed through
$\mathcal M_{\mathcal R}=1$. Baryons subsequently respond to the resulting gravitational potentials and, after
neutral recombination, decouple from the radiation and participate in structure formation.

The statement that the \emph{same phase correlations} seed the next aeon is therefore a closed consequence of the
junction transport equations. The stronger statement that every individual baryon or bound object in the old aeon
is literally the same particle in the new aeon is not part of the conserved-field description and is not required by
the framework. In particular, GRUT's baryogenesis sector permits baryon number violation through electroweak
anomaly processes while conserving the appropriate anomaly-free combination; material continuity must therefore be
understood as continuity of the conserved field degrees of freedom and fluxes, not identity of every composite particle.

The CMB then has its standard role within the framework's sequence: the radiation field evolves through the
hot plasma era and the observed microwave background is the relic radiation released as the baryonic plasma
recombines. Structure formation occurs later from the inherited primordial perturbations and the subsequent
matter dynamics. No independent creation of an abstract information carrier is required: the conformal information
channel and phase correlations are transported by the already derived junction map, while the horizon-cell count
itself resets according to $N_\infty\to0\to N_\infty$.

\subsection*{R5. Completed reset statement}
The physical reset is therefore a single closed sequence:
\begin{equation}
\boxed{
\text{terminal dilution}\;\to\;
\text{conformal junction}\;\to\;
\text{radiation-dominated new aeon}\;\to\;
\text{cooling and recombination}\;\to\;
\text{structure formation}}
\end{equation}
with phase-current transmission, adiabatic curvature transmission and conformal information transmission fixed
by their stated junction assumptions. Separately, the finite conformal-volume remainder of Auxoviscausal strain
matches by~\eqref{eq:aux-junction-match} when the common conformal spatial metric and identified flow tube are regular;
the raw logarithmic strain does not. The reset is thermodynamically irreversible at the coarse-grained level through
$\nabla_\mu s^\mu\ge0$, while the specified conformal transport channels are lossless. This is the scope of the
thermodynamic and geometric reset closure used by TRSD/GRUT.


\subsection*{45.1\quad The reset background and the primary mode}
\sD\ The previous aeon ends in de Sitter expansion: the space-filling defect has $w=-1$ and
the scalar potential has $V_0=0$ (\S38), so $H$ approaches a constant, and the conformal reset
(\S43B) identifies the future boundary of that phase with the next Big Bang. A light scalar of
mass $m$ on this background, in its Bunch--Davies state, leaves the horizon with the late-time
spectrum \sS
\begin{equation}
\Delta^2_{\delta\phi}(k)=\Big(\frac{H}{2\pi}\Big)^2
\Big[\frac{2^{\mu-3/2}\,\Gamma(\mu)}{\Gamma(3/2)}\Big]^2\Big(\frac{k}{aH}\Big)^{3-2\mu},
\qquad \mu=\sqrt{\frac94-\frac{m^2}{H^2}} ,
\label{eq:dS-late}
\end{equation}
an exact power law. Converted to curvature at the reset with efficiency $\kappa$,
$\mathcal R=\kappa\,\delta\phi/\phi_*$, the primary term is
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\Delta^2_{\mathcal R,{\rm prim}}=A_sx^\nu,\qquad \nu=3-2\mu,\qquad
\frac{m^2}{H^2}=\frac32\,\nu-\frac{\nu^2}{4},}
\label{eq:primary-derived}
\end{equation}
with $A_s=\kappa^2(H/2\pi\phi_*)^2[2^{\mu-3/2}\Gamma(\mu)/\Gamma(3/2)]^2(k_*/aH)^\nu$. The exponent
is the carrier's mass ratio, and while $m/H$ is constant the primary term has no running. A
massless carrier gives $\nu=0$, exact dilation invariance; a red tilt requires a slightly
tachyonic one.

\sD\ The carrier is the phase of the defect condensate. At the reset the radial mode is heavy---in the
companion paper $m_\delta^2/H^2=8$, far above $\tfrac94$---so its super-horizon modes oscillate and decay and it
carries no spectrum; the phase, light below the $\mathbb Z_3$ barrier, is the one field that can. The three
generator defects of
the $C_3$ horizon break its symmetry to $\mathbb Z_3$, so the potential contains only the harmonics $\cos3k\theta$, and
its leading term is $V(\theta)=\Lambda_3^4(1-\cos3\theta)$, with $\Lambda_3$ set by the tilt; and
the defects are where $\theta$ passes between the three minima, through the maxima, where
$m^2=-9\Lambda_3^4/f_\delta^2$. The curvature perturbation is seeded there, with
\begin{equation}
\nu\simeq\frac23\,\frac{m^2}{H^2}=-\frac{6\Lambda_3^4}{f_\delta^2H^2}.
\label{eq:nu-Z3}
\end{equation}

\subsection*{45.2\quad The reset spectrum}
The primary mode and its quadratic conjugate-pair interaction give
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\Delta_{\mathcal R,{\rm iso}}^2(k)=A_sx^\nu+C_sx^{2\nu-1},}
\label{eq:reset-spectrum}
\end{equation}
\sD\ with the interaction amplitude derived in \S45.7,
\begin{equation}
\boxed{C_s=2A_s^2\epsilon^2u^2B(\nu+1,\nu+1),\qquad B(a,b)=\frac{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(b)}{\Gamma(a+b)},}
\label{eq:reset-interaction-amplitude}
\end{equation}
and the pair coupling fixed by the cubic vertex of the defect potential (\S45.7),
\begin{equation}
\epsilon u=\frac{1}{\kappa}\,\frac{|V'''|\,\phi_*}{2V''}\le\frac{3\sqrt2/4}{\kappa}.
\label{eq:eu-derived}
\end{equation}
Define the relative interaction strength
\begin{equation}
\boxed{r(k)\equiv\frac{C_s}{A_s}x^{\nu-1}=2A_s\epsilon^2u^2B(\nu+1,\nu+1)\,x^{\nu-1},}
\label{eq:reset-ratio}
\end{equation}
so that $\Delta_{\mathcal R,{\rm iso}}^2=A_sx^\nu[1+r(k)]$.

\subsection*{45.3\quad Exact scalar tilt}
With $n_s(k)-1=d\ln\Delta_{\mathcal R,{\rm iso}}^2/d\ln k$ and
$d\Delta_{\mathcal R,{\rm iso}}^2/d\ln k=\nu A_sx^\nu+(2\nu-1)C_sx^{2\nu-1}$,
\begin{equation}
\boxed{n_s(k)-1=\frac{\nu A_sx^\nu+(2\nu-1)C_sx^{2\nu-1}}{A_sx^\nu+C_sx^{2\nu-1}}
=\frac{\nu+(2\nu-1)r(k)}{1+r(k)}.}
\label{eq:reset-tilt-exact}
\end{equation}
At the pivot, $r_*=C_s/A_s=2A_s\epsilon^2u^2B(\nu+1,\nu+1)$ and
\begin{equation}
\boxed{n_{s*}-1=\frac{\nu+(2\nu-1)r_*}{1+r_*},\qquad \nu=\frac{(n_{s*}-1)+n_{s*}r_*}{1+2r_*}.}
\label{eq:reset-tilt-pivot}
\end{equation}

\subsection*{45.4\quad The tilt measures the reset}
Exact dilation invariance of the reset seed gives $\nu=0$, $r_*=0$, $n_{s*}=1$: a massless
carrier. \sD\ Because $C_s$ carries $A_s^2$, the interaction is suppressed by the amplitude itself:
\begin{equation}
r_*=2A_s(\epsilon u)^2B\simeq\frac{5\times10^{-9}}{\kappa^2},
\label{eq:rstar-derived}
\end{equation}
so $\nu=(n_{s*}-1)+(2-n_{s*})r_*+\mathcal O(r_*^2)$ equals $n_{s*}-1$ to that accuracy. The observed
red tilt is therefore not a calibrated exponent but a measurement of the carrier's mass,
equation~\eqref{eq:primary-derived}:
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\frac{m^2}{H^2}=\frac32(n_{s*}-1)-\frac{(n_{s*}-1)^2}{4}.}
\label{eq:mass-from-tilt}
\end{equation}

\subsection*{45.5\quad Running and tensor tilt}
With $dr/d\ln k=(\nu-1)r$, differentiating~\eqref{eq:reset-tilt-exact} gives the exact running
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\alpha_s(k)\equiv\frac{dn_s}{d\ln k}=\frac{(\nu-1)^2r(k)}{[1+r(k)]^2}.}
\label{eq:reset-running}
\end{equation}
It is nonnegative for $C_s>0$, and a robust negative running over the range of the spectrum would
falsify equation~\eqref{eq:reset-spectrum}. \sP\ By~\eqref{eq:rstar-derived}, at the pivot
$\alpha_{s*}\simeq1.07\,r_*\simeq5\times10^{-9}/\kappa^2$: below $5\times10^{-5}$ for any conversion
efficiency above one per cent, and within the Planck bound for any $\kappa>8\times10^{-4}$
(\S45.9). The running is predicted to be positive and unobservably small. \sP\ The graviton is
massless in the same exact de Sitter phase, so the tensor spectrum is scale-invariant, $n_t=0$,
whereas single-field slow roll requires $n_t=-r/8$.

\subsection*{45.6\quad Relation to the $C_3$ defect component}
The isotropic reset spectrum is distinct from the anisotropic $C_3$ defect pattern of
Section~\ref{sec:CMB}. Write the full initial curvature perturbation as
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\mathcal R(\mathbf k)=\mathcal R_{\rm iso}(\mathbf k)+\lambda_{C_3}\mathcal R_{C_3}(\mathbf k).}
\label{eq:reset-C3-decomposition}
\end{equation}
The isotropic component carries the observed scalar amplitude, tilt and running,
$\langle|\mathcal R_{\rm iso}(k)|^2\rangle=(2\pi^2/k^3)\Delta_{\mathcal R,{\rm iso}}^2(k)$. The $C_3$
component carries the angular selection rule, the mod-$3$ structure, the preferred axis and the
possible parity-odd polarization signature. The two should not be conflated. The point-source
$C_3$ pattern is not detected in the current low-multipole null test, and a transfer-function
calculation remains required before a projected CMB claim is made.

\subsection*{45.7\quad Derivation of the interaction term}
\sD\ The interaction term follows from three properties of the reset. (i) The reset modes
live on the $C_3$ cycle and are chiral: the counter-rotating generators select one direction of
circulation, so the mode wavenumbers $q$ on the cycle are one-sided, $q>0$. (ii) The conjugate
pair $\phi_+\phi_-$ sources curvature quadratically, $b(k)=\epsilon u\int_0^k a(q)\,a(k-q)\,dq$, and
the one-sided convolution of two seed spectra $S(q)=A_s(q/k_*)^\nu$ gives
\begin{equation}
\int_0^kS(q)S(k-q)\,dq=A_s^2k_*\,x^{2\nu+1}B(\nu+1,\nu+1),
\end{equation}
the Beta function arising exactly and the factor $2$ from the two Wick pairings. (iii) The
curvature relaxes to its source at first order, $\dot{\mathcal R}+\beta\mathcal R=b$, with response
$|1/(\beta+ik)|^2$; for $k\gg\beta$ this removes two powers, giving $x^{2\nu-1}$. A
three-dimensional convolution would give $x^{2\nu}$ instead, so the exponent $2\nu-1$ is the
signature of the one-dimensional chiral cycle, and below the relaxation scale the term steepens to
$x^{2\nu+1}$. \sD\ The coupling is the quadratic term of the carrier's mode equation: at second
order $\delta\phi_2=-(V'''/2V'')\,\delta\phi_1^2$, so in curvature
$\epsilon u=|V'''|\phi_*/(2V''\kappa)$. About the vacuum of the defect potential,
$V'''\phi_*/2V''=12\sqrt2/16=3\sqrt2/4$ in field units; at a maximum of the $\mathbb Z_3$ phase
potential the cubic vertex vanishes and the pair term enters only at higher order. Either way
$\epsilon u\le(3\sqrt2/4)/\kappa$, which is equation~\eqref{eq:eu-derived}.

\subsection*{45.8\quad The tilt today}
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccc@{}}
\toprule
data & $n_{s*}$ & $\nu$ & $m^2/H^2$ & $\Lambda_3^4/(f_\delta^2H^2)$ \\
\midrule
Planck 2018 + BK18 + BAO \cite{Planck2018} & $0.9649\pm0.0042$ & $-0.0351$ & $-0.053\pm0.006$ & $0.0059$ \\
P-ACT-LB \cite{ACTDR6ext} & $0.9743\pm0.0034$ & $-0.0257$ & $-0.039\pm0.005$ & $0.0043$ \\
SPT-3G D1 + DESI & $0.9726\pm0.0028$ & $-0.0274$ & $-0.041\pm0.004$ & $0.0046$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The carrier's mass is $|m|/H=0.20$--$0.23$, tachyonic and light, as the $\mathbb Z_3$ maxima require.
The newer small-scale data move $\nu$ toward zero, the scale-invariant limit.

\subsection*{45.9\quad The reset coupling}
\sM\ The measured running bounds the coupling. Planck finds $\alpha_s=-0.0045\pm0.0067$
\cite{Planck2018inflation}, so $\alpha_s<0.0089$ at $95\%$; with $\alpha_{s*}\simeq(\nu-1)^2r_*$
this gives $r_*<0.0083$, and with $A_s=2.1\times10^{-9}$ and $B(\nu+1,\nu+1)\simeq1$,
\begin{equation}
|\epsilon u|<1.4\times10^3 .
\end{equation}
Section~45 derives the coupling, $\epsilon u\le(3\sqrt2/4)/\kappa$, so the bound holds for any
conversion efficiency $\kappa>8\times10^{-4}$, and the running is predicted below $10^{-4}$ for
$\kappa>0.01$.

\subsection*{45.10\quad Status}
\sD\ The form of the spectrum is derived: the primary term is the late-time two-point function of
the reset carrier in the terminal de Sitter phase, and the interaction term is the chiral pair
convolution on the $C_3$ cycle with its coupling fixed by the defect potential. Both terms are
consequences of the GRUT background, not postulates. \sP\ Three predictions follow without a
fitted parameter: the running is positive and below $10^{-4}$ for $\kappa>0.01$, the tensor tilt is
zero, and the interaction term steepens from $x^{2\nu-1}$ to $x^{2\nu+1}$ below the relaxation scale.
Two numbers of the reset remain to be computed: the carrier's mass ratio $m^2/H^2$, equivalently
$\Lambda_3^4/(f_\delta^2H^2)$, which the tilt measures at $-0.04$ to $-0.05$, and the conversion
efficiency $\kappa$ with $H/\phi_*$, which the amplitude $A_s$ measures. Computing the $\mathbb Z_3$
breaking of the phase potential by the three generator defects, relative to the terminal Hubble
rate, would turn the measured tilt into a prediction.

% =====================================================================
\section{The Complete Gravity Equation: Dark-Matter Boosting}\label{sec:complete}
% =====================================================================
\emph{Reading (Section~\ref{sec:rotation}).} In the rotation reading the condensate of this section is the
modulation of the universe's rotation (\S23.1), not a population of particles: the equations stand, and
their carrier is the rotation. The radial mode keeps its mass $m_\delta$ as an excitation of the field.

\subsection*{46.1\quad The baryon-only equation is incomplete}
\sD\ The field equation of \S22.4 takes the baryons as its only source. With that source no
interpolation function holds at both ends: every function that reproduces the rotation curves
predicts a Solar-System quadrupole of order $10^{-26}\ \mathrm{s^{-2}}$ (\S49.10,
\cite{Desmond2024}), and Cassini allows $10^{-27}$. The law must change with scale, and the
change cannot be put into $\mu$ without fitting it. GRUT already contains what the equation
leaves out: the condensate of \S37, which is real matter.

\subsection*{46.2\quad The complete equation}
With the condensate as a source,
\begin{align}
\nabla^2\Phi&=4\pi G\,(\rho_b+\Gamma\rho_\chi),\label{eq:complete1}\\
\rho_\chi(\mathbf k)&=\mathcal J(k)\,\rho_{\rm ph}(\mathbf k),\qquad
\rho_{\rm ph}=\frac{1}{4\pi G}\,\nabla\cdot\Big[\big(\nu(|\nabla\Phi_b|/a_c)-1\big)\nabla\Phi_b\Big],\label{eq:complete2}\\
\mathcal J(k)&=\frac{1}{1+(k/k_J)^4},\qquad k_J=(16\pi G\bar\rho_\chi)^{1/4}\Big(\frac{m_\delta}{\hbar}\Big)^{1/2},\label{eq:complete3}
\end{align}
with $\Gamma$ the capture fraction of \S47 and the bound mass limited by the condensate
available to the system. \sS\ Equation~\eqref{eq:complete1} is Poisson's equation with all the
matter. \sD\ The bound condensate is distributed as the phantom density of the system's own
baryons: it is the gravitational polarization of the condensate elements that survive the
threshold of \S22.1 (Theorem~\ref{thm:boundlaw}). $\Phi_b$ is the baryonic potential of the system
in its freely falling frame, as the equivalence principle requires of real matter, so a uniform
external field does not enter. \sD\ Quantum pressure forbids condensate structure below the Jeans
length $\lambda_J=2\pi/k_J$ \cite{Hu2000}; $\mathcal J$ is the minimal smooth form of that cutoff,
its scale derived and its shape near $k_J$ a model. \sD\ The bound mass cannot exceed the
condensate available, the system's share of $\Omega_c$ (\S25).

\subsection*{46.3\quad What it gives}
\emph{Galaxies.} On scales above $\lambda_J$, with $\Gamma=1$, $\mathcal J\to1$ and the equation is
the quasi-linear form of \S22 with the internal field: the rotation-curve results of \S22.6
stand, and the radial acceleration relation is the signature of the bound condensate.

\emph{The Solar System.} The Jeans length is
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}
\toprule
$\bar\rho_\chi$ [$M_\odot$ pc$^{-3}$] & $0.01$ & $0.1$ & $1$ \\
\midrule
$\lambda_J$ at $m_\delta=2\times10^{-20}$ eV & $285$ pc & $160$ pc & $90$ pc \\
$\lambda_J$ at $m_\delta=10^{-19}$ eV & $128$ pc & $72$ pc & $40$ pc \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The planets, the Oort cloud and wide binaries lie far inside it, so $\mathcal J\to0$; and the Sun,
formed inside the Galaxy, could not have captured the field in any case (\S47.2). It carries no
bound condensate and its field is Newtonian. Without these conditions it would carry $0.9M_\odot$
within its MOND radius of $7198$ AU and $29M_\odot$ within a parsec. The only quadrupole is the
Galactic tide, $8.6\times10^{-31}\ \mathrm{s^{-2}}$ (\S49.10).

\emph{The change with scale.} The effective law is Newtonian below $\lambda_J$, follows the
threshold response across galaxies, and turns Keplerian beyond the reservoir radius, where the
bound mass $M_{\rm ph}(<r)\simeq\sqrt{a_0GM_b}\,r/G$ exhausts the available condensate $M_\chi$:
\begin{equation}
r_t=\frac{M_\chi}{M_b}\sqrt{\frac{GM_b}{a_0}} .
\label{eq:reservoir}
\end{equation}
For $M_b=10^{11}M_\odot$, $r_t=60$ kpc if the galaxy holds its cosmic share,
$M_\chi/M_b=\Omega_c/\Omega_b=5.44$, and $0.3$--$1.1$ Mpc if its halo retains $30$--$100$ times its
baryonic mass. The exponent changes as the scale grows because the matter that carries the boost
has a Jeans length, a capture condition and a finite supply, not because $\mu$ is adjusted.

\subsection*{46.4\quad What it changes elsewhere}
The planets no longer constrain the tail order: the window of \S22.7 constrains the baryon-only
equation, and in the complete equation the exponent is dynamic, set at each acceleration by the
cascade (\S22.9). The covariant completion of \S46.7 is
replaced by a simpler one. Because the boost is carried by matter, the complete theory is
general relativity with the condensate as a source: lensing follows the same mass as dynamics,
tensor modes move at $c$, the post-Newtonian parameters are those of general relativity, energy
and momentum are conserved, and the linear cosmology is that of \S47.8.

\subsection*{46.5\quad Predictions}
\sP\ Each follows without a fitted parameter:
\begin{enumerate}\itemsep1pt
\item The Solar-System quadrupole is the Galactic tide alone, of order $10^{-30}\ \mathrm{s^{-2}}$.
\item Wide binaries and globular clusters, smaller than $\lambda_J$ and formed inside hot hosts,
are Newtonian at every separation and acceleration.
\item Galaxy centres carry cores of size $\sim\lambda_J/2$, $0.02$--$0.15$ kpc for
$m_\delta=10^{-19}$--$2\times10^{-20}$ eV.
\item Flat rotation curves and flat galaxy--galaxy lensing profiles end at $r_t$,
equation~\eqref{eq:reservoir}.
\item Lensing mass equals dynamical mass, since the boost is matter.
\item There is no external-field effect of the MOND kind: a satellite's bound halo responds to
its own baryons and is cut by tides and by the host's reservoir. Satellites in strong host
fields, such as Crater~II, test this.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection*{46.6\quad Derivation of the bound-condensate law}
\begin{theorem}[Bound-condensate law]\label{thm:boundlaw}
\sD\ Let each condensate element, exposed along a direction $\hat u$ with $u=\hat u\cdot\hat g$,
survive the load $xu$ with probability $S(xu)$ (\S22.1) and activate otherwise, and let a
surviving element carry unit gravitational susceptibility and an activated one none. Then the
condensate's polarization is $\mathbf P=(1-\mu(x))\,\mathbf g/4\pi G$, $x=g/a_c$, and its bound
density is exactly the phantom density:
\begin{equation}
\rho_\chi=-\nabla\cdot\mathbf P=\frac{1}{4\pi G}\,\nabla\cdot\big[(1-\mu)\nabla\Phi\big]=\rho_{\rm ph}.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The response along $\hat g$ of the elements oriented along $\hat u$ is their survival at the load
$xu$, projected back onto $\hat g$ by $u$, with the cosine-weighted measure of \S22.2. Its average is
\begin{equation}
3\int_0^1u^2S(xu)\,du=1-3\int_0^1u^2f(xu)\,du=1-\mu(x),
\end{equation}
since $3\int_0^1u^2du=1$ and $f=1-S$. A medium of susceptibility $\chi=1-\mu$ has
$\mathbf P=\chi\,\mathbf g/4\pi G$, and with $\mathbf g=-\nabla\Phi$ Gauss's law gives the bound density
$-\nabla\cdot\mathbf P=\nabla\cdot[(1-\mu)\nabla\Phi]/4\pi G$. Then
$\nabla^2\Phi=4\pi G(\rho_b+\rho_\chi)$ is the same equation as $\nabla\cdot(\mu\nabla\Phi)=4\pi G\rho_b$.
\end{proof}
The identity $1-\mu=3\langle u^2S\rangle$ holds to ten digits for every tail order
(\texttt{condensation.py}). The theorem uses the survival law, which is derived (\S22.1, \S22.10),
the directional average of \S22.2, Gauss's law, and one identification \sI: that $\mu$ is the
gravitational permittivity of the condensate, so that a fully surviving medium carries unit
susceptibility. The dark-matter boost is then the polarization of the surviving condensate:
where the load is small most elements survive and the boost is large, and where it is large they
activate and the field is Newtonian. The Gross--Pitaevskii dynamics set where that polarization
can exist: only above $\lambda_J$, only in the freely falling frame of the system, and only where
the field has been captured (\S47). In the quasi-linear form of~\eqref{eq:complete2} the same
density reads $\nabla\cdot[(\nu-1)\nabla\Phi_b]/4\pi G$; the two forms agree exactly for spherical
systems.

\subsection*{46.7\quad The covariant completion}
\sD\ The defect condensate supplies the two scalar degrees of freedom a completion needs:
the massive radial mode is the dark matter (\S37), and the phase $\theta$, a massless Goldstone
mode, is the natural carrier of the MOND scalar. For a scalar $\vartheta$ in potential units with
Lagrangian $-\tfrac{a_c^2}{8\pi G}\,\mathcal F(|\nabla\vartheta|^2/a_c^2)$, the quasi-static
equation is $\nabla\cdot[\mathcal F'(y)\nabla\vartheta]=4\pi G\rho$, so the threshold family fixes
the kernel
\begin{equation}
\mathcal F(y)=\int_0^y\mu_n\big(\sqrt{y'}\,\big)\,dy' .
\end{equation}
A scalar alone cannot lens by the potential that governs dynamics. The aether--scalar--tensor
construction \cite{SkordisZlosnik2021} adds a unit timelike vector, propagates tensor modes at
$c$, and enters the MOND function only through its free function; with that function set to this
kernel, the completion consists of the metric, the complex defect scalar---radial mode for dark
matter, phase for the MOND scalar---and one unit timelike vector, the single field the GRUT
action does not yet contain. Whether the linear-cosmology and stability results of the
construction survive with this kernel is to be checked. Its quasi-static Solar-System limit is
the law of \S22.4, so it would carry the baryon-only quadrupole of \S49.10.
Section~\ref{sec:complete} replaces this construction: when the boost is carried by the
condensate itself, the completion is general relativity with matter.

\subsection*{46.8\quad Status}
The complete equation is \eqref{eq:complete1}--\eqref{eq:complete3} with the reservoir bound and
the capture fraction of \S47. The bound-condensate law is derived from the survival law and the
identification of $\mu$ as the condensate's permittivity; the Jeans cutoff, the equivalence
principle for real matter, the reservoir and the capture condition follow from the condensate's
dynamics. The Solar System sees only the Galactic tide.

% =====================================================================
\section{Kinematic Evolution and Gravitational Condensation of the Topological Field}\label{sec:condensation}
% =====================================================================
\emph{Reading (Section~\ref{sec:rotation}).} In the rotation reading the condensate of this section is the
modulation of the universe's rotation (\S23.1), not a population of particles: the equations stand, and
their carrier is the rotation. The radial mode keeps its mass $m_\delta$ as an excitation of the field.

To account for the absence of localized dark-matter phenomena in young, high-redshift systems and
its dominant structural presence in mature, low-redshift clusters, the geometric boost cannot be
treated as a static scalar potential. It is a dynamic field subject to the kinematic evolution
of the expanding universe. The topological defect energy introduced by the primordial spatial
tension passes from a free-streaming state to a gravitationally bound state through adiabatic
momentum decay.

\subsection*{47.1\quad Adiabatic decay and the equation of state}
\sS\ Expansion redshifts every freely propagating field: the physical momentum of the topological
background decays inversely with the scale factor,
\begin{equation}
p(a)=\frac{p_0}{a}.
\end{equation}
For a population of rest mass $m_{\rm eff}$ this fixes its speed and its equation of state,
\begin{equation}
v(a)=\frac{c}{\sqrt{1+(a/a_{\rm nr})^2}},\qquad w(a)=\frac{P}{\rho}=\frac13\,\frac{v^2}{c^2},\qquad
a_{\rm nr}=\frac{p_0}{m_{\rm eff}c}.
\end{equation}
In the early universe ($a\ll a_{\rm nr}$) the momentum dominates, $v\to c$ and $w\to\tfrac13$. The
free-streaming length $\lambda_{\rm FS}=\int v\,dt/a$ then vastly exceeds the scale of primordial
baryonic fluctuations, and the field's radiation-like pressure resists localized collapse. For
$a\gg a_{\rm nr}$, $v=c\,a_{\rm nr}/a\propto a^{-1}$ and $w\to0$.

\sD\ The condensate of \S37, born cold by misalignment, has no momentum of its own; its
operative speed is the quantum velocity at its Jeans scale,
$\sigma_q=(\hbar/m_\delta)^{1/2}(16\pi G\bar\rho_\chi)^{1/4}\propto a^{-3/4}$: $0.09$, $0.21$ and
$0.55$ km\,s$^{-1}$ at $z=0$, $2$ and $10$ for $m_\delta=2\times10^{-20}$ eV. A component born
relativistic counts as radiation until $a_{\rm nr}=v_0/c$, so the effective number of neutrino
species bounds its density and present speed:
\begin{equation}
\Delta N_{\rm eff}=\frac{\Omega_{\rm top}\,v_0/c}{0.2271\,\Omega_\gamma}<0.3
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad \Omega_{\rm top}\,v_0<1.1\ \mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}\ \cite{Planck2018}.
\end{equation}

\subsection*{47.2\quad The gravitational capture condition}
As the universe expands the field cools, while baryons collapse into wells of depth
$|\Phi_{\rm bar}|$. The field becomes bound where its speed falls below the escape speed,
$v_{\rm esc}=\sqrt{2|\Phi_{\rm bar}|}$. \sD\ For a Maxwellian population of one-dimensional
dispersion $\sigma$, with $\langle v^2\rangle=3\sigma^2$, the captured fraction is the phase-space
fraction with negative energy,
\begin{equation}
\Gamma=\operatorname{erf}X-\frac{2X}{\sqrt\pi}\,e^{-X^2},\qquad
X=\frac{v_{\rm esc}}{\sqrt2\,\sigma}=\frac{\sqrt{3|\Phi_{\rm bar}|}}{v} .
\label{eq:capture}
\end{equation}
It approaches full capture exponentially, $\Gamma\simeq1-\tfrac{2X}{\sqrt\pi}e^{-X^2}$, when
$v^2\ll2|\Phi_{\rm bar}|$, and vanishes as $\Gamma\simeq\tfrac{4}{3\sqrt\pi}X^3$ when
$v^2\gg2|\Phi_{\rm bar}|$. The capture is set by the velocities of the field around a system when
it forms: a system that forms in the cold cosmic field captures fully, and a system that forms
inside a hot host captures almost nothing.
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}
\toprule
system & $v_{\rm esc}$ [km\,s$^{-1}$] & $\sigma$ of the field around it & $\Gamma$ \\
\midrule
the Sun at its MOND radius ($7198$ AU) & $0.50$ & $165$ (Galactic halo) & $7\times10^{-9}$ \\
wide binary, $2M_\odot$ at $5000$ AU & $0.84$ & $165$ & $4\times10^{-8}$ \\
globular cluster, $10^6M_\odot$ in $10$ pc & $29$ & $165$ & $1.5\times10^{-3}$ \\
dwarf galaxy forming in the cosmic field & $40$ & $\lesssim1$ & $1$ \\
galaxy or cluster forming in the cosmic field & $500$--$2000$ & $\lesssim1$ & $1$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
\sP\ The Sun carries no bound condensate on kinematic grounds alone, independently of the Jeans
cutoff of \S46: two separate mechanisms leave only the Galactic tide in the Solar System. Stars,
binaries and globular clusters formed inside galaxies are Newtonian; satellites keep the halos
they captured before infall, cut by tides.

\subsection*{47.3\quad The dynamically coupled covariant action}
The capture fraction enters the action as a modulation of the defect Lagrangian,
\begin{equation}
S=\int d^4x\,\sqrt{-g}\,\Big[\frac{c^4}{16\pi G}R+\mathcal L_b+\Gamma\,\mathcal L_{\rm def}\Big],
\end{equation}
\sD\ and it is covariant in stationary spacetimes when written as the phase-space fraction of the
field's quanta with negative Killing energy,
\begin{equation}
\Gamma(x)=\frac{\int_{E<0}f(x,p)\,d^3p}{\int f(x,p)\,d^3p},\qquad E=-\xi^\mu p_\mu ,
\end{equation}
which reduces to~\eqref{eq:capture} in the Newtonian limit, with $f$ evolving by the
collisionless Boltzmann equation on the metric. The source term then follows the history of each
system:
\begin{itemize}\itemsep1pt
\item \emph{Young, high-redshift systems.} Where $v(a)^2\gg2|\Phi_{\rm bar}|$, $\Gamma\to0$: the
field remains unbound and the dynamics are those of the baryons alone.
\item \emph{Mature, low-redshift systems.} Where $v(a)^2\ll2|\Phi_{\rm bar}|$, $\Gamma\to1$: the
field freezes out into the well and supplies the bound condensate of \S46, flattening rotation
curves and confining clusters.
\end{itemize}
For the cold condensate the cosmic field is slow at every epoch ($\sigma_q<1$ km\,s$^{-1}$ at
$z<10$), so a system forming in the cosmic field captures fully at any redshift; the redshift
dependence of the boost enters through the environment a system forms in and through the time the
bound distribution takes to settle. A relativistic-born component adds the adiabatic decay
$v\propto a^{-1}$ directly, within the bound of \S47.1.

\subsection*{47.4\quad The modulated field equation and the radial acceleration relation}
In the static, non-relativistic limit the action gives equation~\eqref{eq:complete1},
$\nabla^2\Phi=4\pi G(\rho_{\rm bar}+\Gamma\rho_\chi)$, with $\rho_\chi$ the bound polarization density
of Theorem~\ref{thm:boundlaw}. A fraction $\Gamma$ of the condensate carries susceptibility
$\Gamma(1-\mu)$, so under spherical symmetry, on scales above $\lambda_J$,
\begin{equation}
g\,\Big[1-\Gamma\big(1-\mu(g/a_c)\big)\Big]=g_{\rm bar},
\label{eq:RARmod}
\end{equation}
the Torridity generalization of the MOND relation: $\Gamma=0$ gives $g=g_{\rm bar}$, and $\Gamma=1$ the
threshold law $\mu(g/a_c)\,g=g_{\rm bar}$. \sD\ Under complete freeze-out the boost $g-g_{\rm bar}$
reaches a maximum, the maximal topological boost acceleration,
\begin{equation}
g_{\rm top}=\max_{g_{\rm bar}}\,(g-g_{\rm bar})=0.70\,a_0=8.0\times10^{-11}\ \mathrm{m\,s^{-2}},
\end{equation}
reached at $g_{\rm bar}\simeq4.7\,a_0$ with the dynamic exponent of \S22.9: no system, however
arranged, receives more than about $0.7\,a_0$ from the bound condensate. The variables are
$a_0=(\sqrt3/\pi^2)\,cH_0$, the scale from the $C_3$ triangulation of the horizon; $v(a)$, the
