deficit $\ell_P^2/r^2$ that is the solid angle of one Planck cell:
\begin{equation}
n_{\max}=\log_2N_{\rm full}=\log_22N=408.47,\qquad E_{\rm cell}=\frac{\MH c^2}{2N}=\frac{\hbar H}{8\pi}=\tfrac14k_B\TGH .
\label{eq:ladder-floor}
\end{equation}
The Hubble quantum $\hbar H$ lies at $\log_2(N/4\pi)=403.82$. Read with the horizon temperature, the top of the
ladder is $\MH c^2=\tfrac12Nk_B\TGH$, the holographic equipartition of Padmanabhan \cite{Padmanabhan2012}; here it
follows from the closure.

\subsection*{50.3\quad Why halving: three conservation laws at one step}
\sS\ Momentum conservation fixes the daughters' energies only in a two-body split, and when the daughters are
identical and massless each carries exactly half the parent's energy: $\pi^0\to\gamma\gamma$ steps from rung
$267.70$ to $268.70$, and para-positronium and $H\to\gamma\gamma$ do the same. \sD\ In the law this split is the
$\mathbb Z_2$ cut: the parent's defect divides into two equal cones moving apart, each with half the defect, half
the cells, and one new bit, which half it is. Energy is conserved because the defect total is topological,
information because the cell count is the same fraction~\eqref{eq:shares}, and momentum because the two halves
are equal and opposite. The ladder's step is where the three meet.

\subsection*{50.4\quad The fixed points}
\sD\ For any mass the gravitational and quantum lengths are reciprocal, $r_s\lambda_C=2\ell_P^2$. Read downward
the ladder halves $r_s$ from $r$; read upward from $\hbar H$ it halves $\lambda_C$ from $r$; the two readings cross
at the Planck energy, rung $201.41=\tfrac12[\log_2(N/4\pi)-1]$, for every $H$, since $m_P^2=2\MH\,\hbar H/c^2$. The
dark-energy scale of the capacity relation~\eqref{eq:rhoL-capacity}, $E_\Lambda=(\rho_\Lambda\hbar^3c^3)^{1/4}=2.23$ meV,
sits at rung $303.52=n_P+\tfrac14\log_2(4N_\infty/3)$, three quarters of the way down, at the geometric mean of the
Planck and Hubble energies \cite{CKN1999}. \sI\ Matter sits near two thirds. The Eddington--Weinberg relation
$\hbar^2H\approx Gcm^3$ \cite{Weinberg1972} is the holographic matching $(r/\lambda_C)^3=(r/\ell_P)^2$: the horizon volume
holds as many Compton cells as its area holds Planck cells \cite{MenaMarugan2002}. With unit cells the matched mass is
$59.8$ MeV, at rung $268.88$, two thirds of the way down; with the cells of $N$ it is $110.3$ MeV; the nucleon lies
$3.97$ rungs above. The global count leaves its cell coefficient open; the per-particle footprint balance of \S50.8 fixes its own
coefficient and places the matched mass at $47.5$ MeV.

\subsection*{50.5\quad What the law says about masses}
\sP\ Exact integer steps occur only along chains of symmetric splits; masses formed by other physics carry no
preference for rungs. The data agree. For $23$ particle and atomic energies the offsets from the nearest rung
average $0.247$ rung (uniform: $0.250$; Rayleigh $p=0.18$), and for $18$ planets, moons and dwarf planets $0.237$
($p=0.92$). Near-exact single matches occur at the rate chance gives and move with $H_0$, every rung shifting by
$8.8\%$ between $67.12$ (the withdrawn $5/2$ budget) and $73.04$: at $67.12$ the closest are the Moon ($0.3\%$), the $\tau$ ($1.2\%$) and Ganymede
($1.2\%$); at $73.04$ they are Titan ($0.0\%$), Eris ($1.3\%$) and the 21-cm line ($2.5\%$).

\subsection*{50.6\quad The limits}
\sD\ The ceiling is the closed horizon's total, $\MH c^2\propto r$, and the floor is one cell, $\propto1/r$. Both move
with the horizon, one rung each per doubling of $r$, while the Planck crossing stays fixed. The depth grows as
\begin{equation}
\frac{dn_{\max}}{dt}=\frac{2H(1+q)}{\ln2}=1.39\,H_0\quad\text{today},
\end{equation}
and stops as $q\to-1$, where the bulk reaches holographic equipartition \cite{Padmanabhan2012}. The final depth is
$\log_22N_\infty=409.03$ against $408.47$ now: the ladder is $99.86\%$ complete, with $0.56$ rungs to come, $0.28$ at
each end. \sD\ Beyond our horizon the law holds for every observer's horizon, each closed and each carrying its own
Hubble mass; nothing outside changes the total inside, which changes only as the horizon grows. A conformal gluing
preserves topology, so across the reset $\chi=2$ carries over and each aeon opens with the same total defect,
$4\pi$. \sP\ Register rows 63--64.
\subsection*{50.7\quad The reference mass and the radius of $S^3$}
\sD\ The horizon condition, $2G\MH/c^2=r$ (\S8), is $\Omega_{\rm tot}=1$ exactly; with
\eqref{eq:S3radius} it gives $R\to\infty$. The spatial section is flat, and the $S^3$ of
\S1.8--1.9 is the group manifold $SU(2)$, not space; the closed option of \S4.6 survives only as a
correction beyond the horizon condition, bounded by $R>18r$. For the reference mass, the scale
duality and the Lyman-$\alpha$ bound give $M_*\le7.5\times10^8M_\odot$ (\S37), and the relic
density, $\Omega\simeq0.1\,(f_\delta\theta_i/10^{17}\,{\rm GeV})^2(m_\delta/10^{-22}\,{\rm
eV})^{1/2}$ \cite{HOTW2017} with $f_\delta=4.3\times10^{18}$ GeV, requires the initial
displacement $\theta_i\simeq1.0\times10^{-2}\,(m_\delta/2\times10^{-20}\,{\rm eV})^{-1/4}$. $M_*$ is
fixed once the reset (\S45) supplies $\theta_i$; a displacement of order one would need
$m_\delta\sim2\times10^{-28}$ eV, which the Lyman-$\alpha$ forest excludes.

\subsection*{50.8\quad Why matter sits at two thirds: the footprint balance}
\sI\ A particle has two holographic sizes: its footprint on the horizon, the patch its defect occupies,
$\delta(m)\,r^2=4\pi r_sr$ by~\eqref{eq:defect-rs}, and its own quantum boundary, the Compton sphere of area
$4\pi\lambda_C^2$. Counted by the rule of the closure law, one bit per Planck area, the two balance when
\begin{equation}
\boxed{r_s\,r=\lambda_C^2,\qquad m_*^3=\frac{\hbar^2H}{2Gc},\qquad \frac{Gm_*}{\lambda_C^2}=\frac{cH}{2}:}
\label{eq:footprint}
\end{equation}
the particle's footprint is its own surface, and gravity at its Compton radius is half the Hubble acceleration.
\sD\ Since $\hbar^2H/Gc=m_P^2\,\hbar H/c^2$, the balance mass lies one third of the way down from the Planck mass
to the Hubble quantum, the two-thirds point of the ladder. At the resonance budget, $H_0=67.35$, $m_*=47.5$ MeV. Matter sits
near it: the muon at $2.23\,m_*$, the pions at $2.84$--$2.94\,m_*$, the nucleon at $19.76\,m_*$, the electron at
$0.011\,m_*$. Here it follows from the closure law, with its coefficient fixed; earlier heuristic arguments reached the same
scaling without a fixed coefficient---a gravitational cross-section equal to the Compton area \cite{AlfonsoFaus2008},
a Compton-surface gravity of the order of the Hubble acceleration \cite{Funkhouser2005}---as did the holographic count
of \S50.4 \cite{MenaMarugan2002}.

\sD\ The balance drifts as $H^{1/3}$ while the nucleon mass does not, so the nucleon holds a fixed ratio to
$m_*$ at one epoch only, as Dicke observed for the large-number coincidences \cite{Dicke1961}. Deriving the ratio
is deriving the epoch. Here the epoch is selected at the maximum-power point (\S36.6), whose expansion rate the
capacity relation~\eqref{eq:rhoL-capacity} ties to the final horizon; an exact nucleon ratio at that epoch would
fix $N_\infty$, the boundary datum that no internal condition determines (\S44.6). The size of the universe would
follow from the mass of the nucleon.

The factor is fixed by the framework's own expansion rate. At the resonance budget, $H_0=67.35\pm0.06$, with $m_*\propto H^{1/3}$,
\begin{equation}
K\equiv\frac{m_p}{m_*}=19.759\pm0.006,\qquad m_*=47.49\pm0.01\ \mathrm{MeV}.
\label{eq:nucleon-K}
\end{equation}
The nearest factor built from the framework's constants is $\mathrm{vol}(S^3)=2\pi^2=19.739$, the $SU(2)$ group manifold of
\S1.8--1.9, $0.10\%$ below, which would require $H_0=67.56$: $3.3\sigma$ from the framework's own $H_0$ ($0.4\sigma$ with Planck's).
It was found by search after the ratio was known, and among $1071$ factors $2^a3^b\pi^{c/2}$ one lies this close for $31\%$ of
random ratios; the framework's own expansion rate disfavours it. (At the withdrawn $5/2$ budget the factor was $19.782$.)

\sS\ No geometric number can supply $K$. The nucleon mass is set by dimensional transmutation, $m_p=C\,\Lambda_{\rm QCD}$
with $C$ computed in lattice QCD and $\Lambda_{\rm QCD}\propto\mu\,e^{-2\pi/b_0\alpha_s(\mu)}$, so $K$ is exponential in
$1/\alpha_s$: at the $Z$ mass, with $b_0=23/3$, $d\ln K/d\alpha_s=2\pi/b_0\alpha_s^2=59$, and fixing $K$ to its $0.035\%$
would take $\alpha_s$ to $6\times10^{-6}$.

\sD\ The relation is therefore an exact identity,
\begin{equation}
\boxed{m_p^3=K^3\,\frac{\hbar^2H}{2Gc},}
\label{eq:EW-identity}
\end{equation}
whose geometric part is the footprint balance and its $\tfrac12$, whose remaining factor is the strong coupling,
and whose expansion rate is the budget's at the selected epoch. The Eddington--Weinberg coincidence is therefore not a
second unexplained number: with the epoch selected (\S36.6) and $H$ set by the final horizon, it is the boundary
datum $N_\infty$ written in units of the QCD scale. Stating the nucleon's position and stating the size of the
universe are one and the same datum.

\subsection*{50.9\quad The strong coupling: two rules tested}
\sH\ Two rules carry the geometry of the electric coupling (\S43F) over to the strong one, each fixed before any
comparison. The volume rule takes $1/\alpha$ as the volumes of the electroweak group and its $\mathbb Z_2$ quotients and
replaces them by those of $SU(3)$ and its centre $\mathbb Z_3$, the $C_3$ of the horizon. The closure rule notes that the
centres of $SU(2)$ and $SU(3)$ are the horizon's two closure symmetries, the $\mathbb Z_2$ of the deficit $\pi$ and the
$\mathbb Z_3$ of $C_3$, and sets $\alpha_3=\alpha_2$ at the defect scale $f_\delta=M_P/\sqrt8$, where both close.

The measured couplings at $M_Z$, run at two loops in the Standard Model with $\alpha_s(M_Z)=0.1180\pm0.0009$, decide
both. The volume rule, $1/\alpha_s=\mathrm{vol}(SU(3))+\mathrm{vol}(SU(3)/\mathbb Z_3)=\tfrac43\sqrt3\,\pi^5=706.7$, holds only
near $10^{274}$ GeV. The closure rule predicts $\alpha_s(M_Z)=0.191$ at $f_\delta$ and $0.219$ at $M_P$, $81\sigma$ and
$112\sigma$ high, because the measured $\alpha_2$ and $\alpha_3$ cross at $2.9\times10^{16}$ GeV, a factor $148$ below
$f_\delta$. Both rules are excluded. The crossing lies near $\alpha f_\delta=3.15\times10^{16}$ GeV, where the closure rule
would give $\alpha_s(M_Z)=0.1188$, $0.9\sigma$ from the measurement; that scale was found after the crossing was known,
and among thirty natural scales built from $f_\delta$, $M_P$, $\alpha$ and $\pi$ one lands in the $1\sigma$ window for
$36\%$ of random crossings. It is not evidence.

\sD\ The strong coupling is therefore an input of the present framework, as the electroweak scale is (\S43D), and
through~\eqref{eq:EW-identity} it carries the nucleon's position. \sD\ It is an input by Bridge theorem B4 (\S44.6): it is
in neither axiom, and with massless quarks it is not a pure number at all but the strong scale $\Lambda_{\rm QCD}$ written in units of another
scale, so a derivation needs that scale in Planck units---which~\eqref{eq:EW-identity} supplies only through $K$ and the boundary datum. The
strong coupling and $N_\infty$ are therefore one datum only if $K$ is derived; with $K$ measured they are two.

\subsection*{50.10\quad The nucleon as a closed $C_3$}
\sD\ The nucleon is the horizon's closed $C_3$ at the strong scale. Its quarks are as many as the order of $C_3$, as the
anomaly argument requires, and they form a colour singlet because their $\mathbb Z_3$ phases, $e^{2\pi i/3}$ each, sum to
zero, as the windings of the phase on the closed horizon do (Section~\ref{sec:rotation}); the smallest closed universe of
$2{+}1$ gravity is likewise three particles \cite{Brill2003}. \sS\ The three flux tubes meet at a junction, at $120^\circ$,
where their equal tensions cancel: the configuration lattice QCD finds for the static three-quark potential
\cite{Takahashi2001}, and the force balance of the horizon's three defects.

\sD\ The closure also fixes the charge. The centres of $SU(3)$ and $SU(2)$, $\mathbb Z_3$ and $\mathbb Z_2$, are the
horizon's two closure symmetries, and together they form the $\mathbb Z_6$, the largest quotient the Standard Model's
charges allow, $(SU(3)\times SU(2)\times U(1))/\mathbb Z_6$ \cite{Tong2017}. A closed $C_3$ cancels the $\mathbb Z_3$ part,
so every colour singlet carries integer charge: the nucleon's $\tfrac23+\tfrac23-\tfrac13=1$ is its closure.

\sD\ The correspondence fixes ratios and leaves one scale. At the horizon the closure fixes every ratio and leaves one
scale, set by $G$ and the boundary datum $N$ (\S44.6); at the nucleon it fixes the ratios of the closed $C_3$ and leaves
one scale, the strong tension, which carries $\alpha_s$ through dimensional transmutation. The strong coupling is the
nucleon's scale datum, as $N$ is the horizon's.

The closed $C_3$ offers one ratio without a parameter: if the nucleon closes as the horizon does, its radius in its
own Compton units is the full closure over the $C_3$ total, $4\pi/\pi=4$. The measured charge radius \cite{CODATA2022}
gives $r_p/\bar\lambda_p=3.9977\pm0.0030$, $0.8\sigma$ from $4$, and muonic hydrogen \cite{Antognini2013} gives
$3.9983\pm0.0019$, $0.9\sigma$. The near-equality $r_p\approx4\bar\lambda_p$ was known before this reading, and among the
$288$ simple numbers between $2$ and $8$ one lies within $1\sigma$ of a random ratio $27\%$ of the time: the radius is
consistent with the closure and is not evidence for it.

\section{Status of the Framework}\label{sec:status}

\subsection*{51.1\quad Status}
Nothing in this section is left to calculate. Derived: the two-dimensional law and its reduction length;
the Tully--Fisher law and its zero point ($-0.7\sigma$); the binding condition; the Solar-System quadrupole
($-0.9\sigma$, and $16\sigma$ against the total-field alternative); the dust of \eqref{eq:rot-constraint} and its
zero sound speed; the field's magnetic and chiral currents; the birefringence formula. Identified: the deficit-$\pi$ cone of each
system (\sH), the dust as the budget's $\Omega_c$, and the two-dimensional field as the cone's section.
Decided by observation: wide binaries, Newtonian here; a confirmed low-acceleration boost in them falsifies
the reading. Derived: the dark sector as the spin's quanta in two states, its equation of state $w=-1+\Theta$, and the
dilation $\mathcal S=1/\cos_n\theta$ as what lensing infers as dark matter (\S53); the dilation on the unbound web, which would set the
expansion measured inside the local void, is a hypothesis (\S53.9).

\subsection*{51.2\quad The identifications that remain}
Beyond the axioms, every other step of the paper is derived. It rests on the correspondences below; for
each, what a derivation would need is stated. Release 1.14.0 worked all seven; each item ends with its status.
\begin{itemize}\itemsep1pt
\item I5: that each dark sector's density is the power per mode of its harmonic---the assignment of the sectors
to the axial quadrupole and the triangular octupole is derived (\S25); a dynamical link between power and
density is needed; the CMB spectra disfavour $5/2$ at $4.6\sigma$, short of the $5\sigma$ removal threshold, and prefer $2.61\pm0.02$ (\S25.9); with the measured ratio the dark ratio is boundary data, the amount of the
rotation's dust being an integration constant, and the budget lands on Planck's densities (\S25.10); the resonance of the present (\S36.7) gives
$\Omega_\Lambda=27/4\pi^2$ and the dark ratio $2.580$ ($2.568$ without the neutrinos); in the rotation reading the dust's amount is an integration constant of the constraint
(\S23.1), which no field equation fixes. \emph{Status: withdrawn.} The integration constant decides when the resonance falls, not the
budget there: with the rotation at the spin (\S36.14) and the present at the resonance (H3), $\Omega_\Lambda/\Omega_c=2.580$ follows with no
link between power and density, $1.3\sigma$ from the CMB's value, and the budget lies within $1\sigma$ of Planck's densities (\S25.11). $5/2$
is kept in \S52.6.
\item The form $\Omega_b/\Omega_c=\alpha_{\rm TRSD}\,n$ and the reset's asymmetry---need the branching of the reset (\S25.8).
\emph{Status:} the exponent is read at the horizon's load, a pure number because the acceleration scale tracks the horizon (\S36.15), and
the ratio passes the CMB spectra ($+0.3\sigma$); the amount of the asymmetry is fixed by the budget, $\eta=6.16\times10^{-10}$ at the
resonance budget, $+0.7\sigma$ in $\omega_b$ and $+0.4\sigma$ from deuterium burning, and Sakharov's conditions hold without a new coupling
(\S25.8). Identified: TRSD's product form. Open: the branching that realizes the amount, on which no prediction waits.
\item I6: that the $\sqrt m$ vector obeys the seed condition in the cycle's Lorentzian form (\S42), and
$3\delta_\ell=Q$---need a dynamics of generation space that makes the mass vector null; the $2\times10^{-7}$
correction is part of the target. \emph{Status:} the null condition is the vacuum of the axiom's potential. With each generation's
amplitude the rise of its generator's invariant when the pair is added, $Q=(\Xi+2)/3\Xi$, so $Q=\tfrac23$ at $\Xi=2$, the director circle
of the generators, from which they subtend the seed's right angle; the charged leptons measure $\Xi_\ell=2.000013\pm0.000031$ (\S42.8).
Identified: the dressed amplitude~\eqref{eq:dressed}. Open: $3\delta_\ell=Q$ and its $2\times10^{-7}$ correction.
\item The fine-structure sum, and its correction as $\alpha$ times the generator tension $f_{\rm gen}^2/F=\tfrac1{24}$ dressed
thermally (\S43A/C)---need a calculation that returns the electroweak volume sum as an inverse coupling, with
its first correction. \emph{Status:} the thermal dressing is the Boltzmann weight of one quantum of the spin, the quantum the rotation
carries, so both factors of the correction are quantities of the framework (\S43F). Identified: the volume sum as the inverse coupling at
zero momentum. Open: the calculation that returns it; the weight $1$ against $3/\pi$ is decided by $2.6\times10^{-8}$ in $1/\alpha$.
\item First starlight (\S48.8)---a hypothesis, tested by the register. \emph{Status:} with the present selected by the resonance, first
starlight no longer selects it; it relates two physical epochs, $t_\star=\alpha\,t_{\rm res}$, at $100.6$ Myr, $z=29.6$, $46.4$ MHz, and needs
no observer. It is not derived: the onset of cosmic dawn depends on the primordial amplitude and the first stars, which the framework does
not compute. No starlight before it is known; the claimed $z\simeq32$ galaxy is a Y dwarf (register row 76). Counted in cycles, the onset is a ratio of received to emitted cycles,~\eqref{eq:dawn-cycles}, and $\alpha$ cancels against the birefringence,
$H_\infty t_\star=(\pi/3)\beta$: the measured twist places the onset at $42.7^{+5.6}_{-6.0}$ MHz with no clock and no Hubble constant
(register row 78).
\item The strong coupling $\alpha_s$: with it the nucleon's position above the footprint balance follows,
$K=19.759\pm0.006$ (\S50.8), and with the selected epoch the boundary datum $N_\infty$; no geometric factor
supplies it ($2\pi^2$ is a post-hoc target, consistent with the CMB's $H_0$), and the volume and closure rules for $\alpha_s$ are excluded (\S50.9). It is the nucleon's scale datum, as $N$ is
the horizon's (\S50.10). \emph{Status:} an input by Bridge theorem B4, which now lists it (\S44.6): it is in neither axiom, and with
massless quarks it is the strong scale written in units of another scale. At the resonance budget $K=19.759\pm0.006$, and $2\pi^2$ lies
$3.3\sigma$ below with the framework's own $H_0$ ($0.4\sigma$ with Planck's): the post-hoc target is disfavoured.
\item The spinning-arc principle (\S36.9), $\omega R=c$ with a fixed spin: the founding picture of the framework, stated as
a hypothesis; from it the rotation's rate $H_\infty$ follows, in place of the equilibrium premise of \S36.8. For
the final horizon it is now derived (\S36.10--36.12): the arcs, the rate, the light-speed rim, the constancy, the charge and the
quantum of the spin. What remains is I8, that the internal phase turns with the spin, $X_0=\Lambda/3$---a pure number $k$, tested by
the birefringence ($k=0.88\pm0.18$) and, through the resonance H3, by $\Omega_\Lambda$ ($k=0.9994\pm0.0053$) (\S36.13). \emph{Status: derived.}
Each quantum of the rotation carries Killing energy $\hbar\Omega_0$ across the final horizon, whose entropy steps by $2\pi$, so
$\Omega_0=jH_\infty$ with $j$ an integer, and the two measurements fix $j=1$: the rotation's quantum is one quantum of the spin (\S36.14).
The galactic scale tracks the horizon, not the spin, and the high-redshift rotation curves agree that it evolves (\S36.15).
\end{itemize}
The paper assumes, beyond its primitives: the identifications I1--I4, I6 in the form~\eqref{eq:dressed}, and I7; the
product form of the baryon ratio and the fine-structure identification; and the hypotheses H1--H4, of which H3 is the opening principle's
statement of the present and carries the dark ratio, the closed-form birefringence and the phase turned since the beginning, and H4 places
the same present in Planck cells (\S48.12), fixing the boundary datum $N$ by $\alpha$. Its inputs are
those of B4 (\S44.6).
Two readings carry no prediction and are not assumptions of the paper: $\mu$ as a permittivity (\S46.6), since
the phantom density follows from the field equation itself, and the cone of every baryonic system (\S49.6),
since the two-dimensional law is the derived deep limit of \S22. The dimensionful anchors and the cell count
are not among them either: no dimensionful constant follows from
dimensionless geometry, and $N$ is the boundary datum that no internal condition fixes.

% =====================================================================
\section{Superseded and Excluded Results}\label{sec:superseded}
This section keeps in full the results that later work superseded and the mechanism it excluded, each with
what replaced it; nothing in the sections above rests on them. The frozen-phase birefringence bound (formerly
\S46.13) holds the phase frozen, as the condensate reading does; the rotation reading's angle replaces it
(\S49.12--49.13). The global frame dragging (formerly \S50.3, \S50.4 and \S50.6), first
proposed as the carrier of the extra gravity, is excluded: on the closed horizon the windings of the phase sum to
zero (Section~\ref{sec:rotation}). Its equations are kept as proposed, with their units made consistent. The equal-frequency
Lissajous count of the geometric origin, withdrawn in release 1.13.0, is kept in \S52.5, and the defect power ratio as the dark ratio,
withdrawn in release 1.14.0, in \S52.6.

\subsection*{52.1\quad The birefringence angle}
\emph{Reading note.} This computation holds the phase frozen by a tilt-fixed mass, as the condensate
reading does. In the rotation reading the phase turns at the rate fixed by the $C_3$ eigenvalue, and the
angle is computed in \S49.12--49.13: $\beta=0.314^\circ$.

\sD\ If the defect phase coupled to electromagnetism through an anomaly,
\begin{equation*}
\mathcal L\supset\frac{\kappa\alpha}{4\pi}\,\theta\,F_{\mu\nu}\tilde F^{\mu\nu},
\end{equation*}
the plane of polarization of light from last scattering would rotate by
\begin{equation}
\beta=\frac{\kappa\alpha}{2\pi}\,\Delta\theta ,
\label{eq:beta}
\end{equation}
with $\Delta\theta$ the phase advance between last scattering and today. Three parts of this
statement are not derived, and the observation is not settled. (i) The coupling: the condensate is
exactly neutral (\S31.1), so a $\theta F\tilde F$ term arises only if $\theta$ is a chiral phase of
charged-fermion masses, which the action does not contain. (ii) Granted that, the coefficient
follows from the charges of \S31.4: rotating away a mass phase $\theta$ of a Dirac fermion of charge
$Q$ generates $(\alpha Q^2/4\pi)\,\theta F\tilde F$, so $\kappa=\sum_fN_cQ_f^2$ over the fermions
carrying the phase, $3$ for the charged leptons and $8$ for every charged fermion. (iii) The phase
advance had been taken as one $C_3$ step. (iv) The measurements---$0.342^\circ\pm0.094^\circ$
(Planck + WMAP \cite{EskiltKomatsu2022}), $0.215^\circ\pm0.074^\circ$ (ACT DR6
\cite{DiegoPalazuelos2025}) and jointly $0.277^\circ\pm0.057^\circ$ (ACT + Planck
\cite{Eskilt2026})---are not yet established as cosmological: the ACT team reports its angle as an
unexplained instrumental discrepancy, and the joint analysis states that unresolved systematics
must be understood first.

\emph{The phase advance, computed.} \sD\ The phase is not free to advance a step: its mass is
fixed by the tilt. With the carrier of \S45 read as the phase near a $\mathbb Z_3$ maximum (\sI),
$V(\theta)=\Lambda_3^4(1-\cos3\theta)$ gives $m_\theta^2=9\Lambda_3^4/f_\delta^2=|m^2|$, and \S45.8
measures $|m^2|/H_\infty^2=0.039$--$0.053$ at the terminal rate $H_\infty=H_0\sqrt{\Omega_\Lambda}$ of
the previous aeon, whose potential and budget are those of this one. Hence
\begin{equation}
\frac{m_\theta}{H_0}=\Big(\frac{|m^2|}{H_\infty^2}\,\Omega_\Lambda\Big)^{1/2}=0.16\text{--}0.19,\qquad
\frac{\Lambda_3^4}{\rho_\Lambda}=\frac{\pi}{27}\,\frac{|m^2|}{H_\infty^2}=0.005 .
\end{equation}
A field this light is held by Hubble friction. Integrating
$\ddot\theta+3H\dot\theta+\tfrac13m_\theta^2\sin3\theta=0$ from $z=1090$ to today in the budget's
expansion history, from rest at every initial phase between a minimum and a maximum, the largest
advance and the angles it allows are
\begin{equation}
|\Delta\theta|_{\max}=(1.2\text{--}1.6)\times10^{-3}\ \mathrm{rad},\qquad
|\beta|<3.3\times10^{-4}\,{}^\circ\ (\kappa=3),\qquad |\beta|<9\times10^{-4}\,{}^\circ\ (\kappa=8).
\end{equation}
\sP\ GRUT therefore predicts no measurable isotropic birefringence from the defect phase,
$|\beta|<10^{-3}\,{}^\circ$. If the measured angle is confirmed as cosmological it lies $4.9\sigma$
from this and needs a source outside the defect phase; if it is instrumental, the prediction
stands. The phase holds at most $2\Lambda_3^4\simeq1\%$ of the dark-energy density and thaws only on
a time of order $10^2/H_0$, so $w=-1$ is unaffected.

\subsection*{52.2\quad From modulation to orbit}
A phase that winds at a modulated rate has a momentum density, and that momentum density sources the
frame-dragging field. The chain is
\begin{equation}
\begin{gathered}
\text{baryons}\;\to\;\text{modulated phase frequency}\;\to\;\text{momentum density}\\
\to\;\text{frame dragging}\;\to\;\text{orbital velocity}.
\end{gathered}
\end{equation}
The phase stress tensor is
$T_{\mu\nu}=\chi_0^2\big[\partial_\mu\theta_{\rm phase}\partial_\nu\theta_{\rm phase}-\tfrac12g_{\mu\nu}(\partial\theta_{\rm phase})^2\big]$
with $\chi_0^2=c^4/4G$, and its momentum density is the mixed component, in orthonormal components with
$\varrho=R_H\sin\chi$ the distance from the axis,
\begin{equation}
T_{\hat t\hat\varphi}=\frac{c^4}{4G}\,\frac{n\,[\omega_0+\delta\omega_b(\chi)]}{c\varrho} .
\end{equation}
It drives the dimensionless frame field $h\equiv h_{\hat t\hat\varphi}$ through
$\nabla^2h=-(16\pi G/c^4)\,T_{\hat t\hat\varphi}$, that is $\nabla^2h=-4\pi n[\omega_0+\delta\omega_b(\chi)]/c\varrho$, and the
frame field acts on orbits through
\begin{equation}
\Omega_{\rm frame}=-\frac{c\,h}{\varrho},\qquad a_{\rm drag}=2\Omega_{\rm frame}v=-\frac{2c\,h}{\varrho}\,v,\qquad
\frac{v^2}{r}=g_b+a_{\rm drag}.
\end{equation}
The gravity was already there, from the global winding. The baryons tune it: they make it locally
coherent in a way that produces flat rotation curves.

\subsection*{52.3\quad The compact space is essential}
The geometry is closed, so whatever sources the frame dragging must have a compensating background:
$\int_{S^3}\nabla^2h\,dV=0$. With $\Lambda_{\rm loc}=4\pi n[\omega_0+\delta\omega_b(\chi)]/c\varrho$, the source is
\begin{equation}
\Lambda_{\rm eff}=\Lambda_{\rm loc}-\langle\Lambda_{\rm loc}\rangle_{S^3},\qquad\int_{S^3}\Lambda_{\rm eff}\,dV=0 .
\end{equation}
The background is not arbitrary: it is the average of the modulation over the closed space,
\begin{equation}
\rho_{\rm bg}=\frac{\int_0^\pi\sin^2\chi\;\Lambda_{\rm loc}(\chi)\,d\chi}{\int_0^\pi\sin^2\chi\,d\chi}
=\frac{8n}{cR_H}\int_0^\pi\sin\chi\,[\omega_0+\delta\omega_b(\chi)]\,d\chi .
\end{equation}
This is what makes the equation solvable; without it there is no solution at all. The solution is
\begin{equation}
h(\chi)=\int_{S^3}G_{S^3}(\chi,\chi')\,\Lambda_{\rm eff}(\chi')\,dV',\qquad
\nabla^2G_{S^3}=-\delta_{S^3}+\frac1V,\qquad h=h_{\rm loc}-\langle h_{\rm loc}\rangle,
\end{equation}
the additive constant being fixed by the chosen frame.

\subsection*{52.4\quad The completed system}
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
&ds^2=-c^2dt^2+R_H^2\,[d\chi^2+\sin^2\chi\,d\Omega_2^2],\\
&\theta_{\rm phase}=[\omega_0+\delta\omega_b(\rho_b)]\,t+n\varphi,\qquad
\delta\omega_b=\Gamma_b(\rho_b)\left[\frac{v_{\rm flat}(\rho_b)}{R_H\sin\chi}-\omega_0\right],\\
&T_{\hat t\hat\varphi}=\frac{c^4}{4G}\,\frac{n\,\omega(\chi)}{c\varrho},\qquad
\nabla^2h=-\frac{16\pi G}{c^4}T_{\hat t\hat\varphi}+\rho_{\rm bg},\qquad
\int_{S^3}\Big(-\frac{16\pi G}{c^4}T_{\hat t\hat\varphi}+\rho_{\rm bg}\Big)dV=0,\\
&\Omega_{\rm frame}=-\frac{c\,h}{\varrho},\qquad a_{\rm drag}=-\frac{2c\,h}{\varrho}\,v,\qquad v(\chi)=\varrho\,\omega(\chi),\quad\varrho=R_H\sin\chi.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}

\subsection*{52.5\quad The equal-frequency Lissajous count}
The geometric origin of release 1.11.0 stated: ``The threefold count is the Lissajous figure of the two counter-rotating arcs: two
equal-frequency perpendicular motions a quarter turn apart trace on the sphere a closed figure with three nodes at $2\pi/3$,
where the counter-rotation leaves no net tangential motion and the curvature concentrates.'' \sD\ They do not. In the plane the two
motions, $\cos\omega t$ and $\sin\omega t$, trace a circle, with no node. On the sphere, two semicircles turning at one rate in
opposite senses about perpendicular axes cross, if started together, on a great circle, $(\cos u,\sin u,\cos u)/\sqrt{1+\cos^2u}$,
and, if started a quarter turn apart, on an arc; neither has a node. A point carried by both turns traces a figure-eight with at
most one node. At equal rates no figure has three nodes; three require a relative rate of three, which the fold supplies against
the conjugate arc (\S1.11). The claim is withdrawn, as the companion paper
had withdrawn the same chain \cite{TRSD2026}.

\subsection*{52.6\quad The defect power ratio as the dark ratio}
Releases up to 1.13.0 identified the two dark densities with the power per mode of their harmonics in the three-defect pattern (I5,
\S25.1--25.3, kept there in full): $\Omega_\Lambda/\Omega_c=C_3/C_2=5/2$, from $C_\ell\propto1+2P_\ell(-\tfrac12)$ (\S39.3). The budget built on it
gave $\Omega_m=0.3219$ and $H_0=67.12$. \sM\ The CMB spectra disfavour it at $4.6\sigma$ (\S25.9):
it set the two material variances of that budget, the physical baryon density ($+2.5\sigma$) and DESI's matter density ($+2.8\sigma$).
\sD\ It is withdrawn because it is not needed: the dynamics leaves the dark amount an integration constant (\S23.1), and the resonance fixes
the ratio at the present, $2.580$ (\S25.11). The assignment of the sectors to the axial quadrupole and the triangular octupole stands; the
power ratio $5/2$ remains the single-triple limit of the defect pattern on the sky, where the selection rule, not the ratio, is the test
(\S39.2--39.6).

\subsection*{52.7\quad The cross assignment of mergers and the returning-merger dark core}
Two readings of mergers proposed for release 1.19.0 are not adopted. \emph{The cross assignment:} that the lensing mass at a galaxy peak is its stars plus the \emph{other}
cluster's condensate. It contradicts \S53.6, since the excited geometry and the galaxies share geodesics, and the synthetic Bullet-like maps exclude it at $T=99.1$ against the
own assignment (\S47.9); the true cross term is the superposition tail of \S47.9. \emph{The returning-merger dark core as a prediction:} that after a first passage the excess
returns to the gas, as in Abell~520. It contradicts the frozen field, $t_{\rm rel}\simeq31$ Gyr against passages of $0.2$--$1$ Gyr (equation~\eqref{eq:trel}); the settled share
that could follow the gas is $f(\Delta t)\le0.06$ after 2 Gyr. A galaxy-free peak on the gas is kept as the falsifier of register row 91.

% =====================================================================
\section{The Dark Sector as One Geometry}\label{sec:onegeometry}
% =====================================================================
 Dark energy is spacetime, the geometry itself; what is called
dark matter is the same spacetime in its excited state, bound to baryonic structure; and what lensing infers as dark matter is the
dilation---the excess of the inferred mass over the baryonic mass, smallest at the centre, growing toward the edge, and varying from system
to system. There is spacetime and there are baryons: dark matter is not a separate entry. Each statement is derived below from the
capacity of the horizon (\S48.10), the rotation held at its rate (\S23.1) and the gravity law (\S22.9).

\subsection*{53.1\quad Spacetime, unexcited and excited}
\sD\ The spin's quantum is $\hbar\omega$ with $\omega=H_\infty$ (\S36.11). The final horizon holds $L_\infty/\hbar=N_\infty/4\pi$ of them,
and their energy is the final horizon's own, $(N_\infty/4\pi)\,\hbar H_\infty=c^5/2GH_\infty$, the Hubble mass of the final horizon
(\S36.11, eq.~\eqref{eq:spin-charge}). Spread over the final horizon's volume $\tfrac{4\pi}{3}R_\infty^3$, this is exactly the vacuum:
\begin{equation}
n_*\,\hbar H_\infty=\rho_\Lambda c^2,\qquad
n_*=\frac{N_\infty/4\pi}{\tfrac{4\pi}{3}R_\infty^3}=\frac{3}{8\pi\,\ell_P^2\,R_\infty},
\label{eq:nstar}
\end{equation}
three quanta per area quantum $8\pi\ell_P^2$ (\S36.11) per final-horizon radius. Dark energy is spacetime \emph{unexcited}: the spin's quanta
held by the horizon, uniform, constant by the Bianchi identity (\S36.10), $w=-1$. The rotation carries quanta of the same kind---each quantum
of the rotation is one quantum of the spin (\S36.14)---and that is spacetime \emph{excited}: dust of number density $n\propto a^{-3}$,
conserved by the rotation's Noether current (\S23.1), with $w=0$ and zero sound speed, which clusters and binds to baryonic structure. Both
sit in one stress tensor,
\begin{equation}
\boxed{T^{\rm dark}_{\mu\nu}=\hbar H_\infty\big(n\,u_\mu u_\nu-n_*\,g_{\mu\nu}\big)=2X_0\big(\lambda\,u_\mu u_\nu-\lambda_*\,g_{\mu\nu}\big),
\qquad \lambda_*=\frac{3c^4}{16\pi G},}
\label{eq:dark-one}
\end{equation}
with $\lambda=n\hbar c^2/2H_\infty$ the multiplier of~\eqref{eq:rot-constraint}, $u_\mu=\partial_\mu\theta/\sqrt{X_0}$ and $X_0=\Lambda/3$ (I8, derived in
\S36.14). One scale, the spin $X_0=\Lambda/3$; one kind of quantum, $\hbar H_\infty$; two states, unexcited and excited. The vacuum's tension
$\lambda_*=(3/16\pi)\,c^4/G$ is a fixed fraction of the force $F=c^4/G$ of \S14, $7.22\times10^{42}$~N; the rotation's is
$2.80\times10^{42}$~N today, in the ratio $1/2.580$ of the resonance budget (\S25.11). The inventory is therefore spacetime---$0.684$ of the
critical density unexcited and $0.265$ excited, $0.949$ together---and the baryons, $0.050$, with the neutrinos and the radiation.

\subsection*{53.2\quad The equation of state of the whole}
\sD\ From~\eqref{eq:dark-one} the dark sector's equation of state is
\begin{equation}
\boxed{w_{\rm dark}=-1+\Theta,\qquad \Theta=\frac{n}{n+n_*}=\frac{\lambda}{\lambda+\lambda_*}=\frac{\rho_\chi}{\rho_\chi+\rho_\Lambda},}
\label{eq:wdark}
\end{equation}
the excited fraction of spacetime's quanta. This is the proposal's $w=-1+\Theta$, with $\Theta$ derived rather than posited. Over cosmic
time $n/n_*=(\Omega_c/\Omega_\Lambda)\,a^{-3}$, so
\begin{equation}
\Theta(a)=\frac{1}{1+(a/a_{\rm eq})^3},\qquad a_{\rm eq}=\Big(\frac{\Omega_c}{\Omega_\Lambda}\Big)^{1/3}:
\label{eq:Theta-a}
\end{equation}
a step in $\ln a$ from $1$ to $0$, centred on dark-sector equality. At the resonance budget, $\Omega_\Lambda/\Omega_c=2.580$, equality falls at
$z=0.372$, $\Theta=0.9$ at $z=1.85$, the dark sector alone stops decelerating ($w_{\rm dark}=-\tfrac13$) at $z=0.728$, and today
$\Theta_0=0.279$, $w_{\rm dark,0}=-0.721$.

Locally $\Theta(\mathbf x)=n(\mathbf x)/[n(\mathbf x)+n_*]$. Inside haloes, where the bound density is $10^2$--$10^5$ times $\rho_\Lambda$, $\Theta\simeq1$
and the dark sector is dust; in voids at $\delta=-0.8$, $\Theta=0.07$ and $w=-0.93$. The threshold is the vacuum's own: $\Theta=\tfrac12$ where
the excited quanta equal the unexcited ones, $\rho_\chi=\rho_\Lambda$. Where the bound share follows the baryons at the cosmic ratio, that is a
baryon density $\rho_b=(\Omega_b/\Omega_c)\,\rho_\Lambda$, $n_b=0.65\ \mathrm{m^{-3}}$, $2.58$ times the mean: the proposal's threshold in
$\rho_b$, located. Binding is a separate surface: a region is bound inside the zero-acceleration radius, where the mean density is
$2\rho_\Lambda$, the balance radius of \S43B.3 (register row 18), at matter overdensity $2\Omega_\Lambda/\Omega_m=4.33$; there $\Theta\ge0.63$.

\subsection*{53.3\quad Excited once, gathered since}
\sD\ The excitation is not made where baryons are dense. The two states are separately conserved: $n_*$ by the Bianchi identity and the
capacity relation (\S36.10, \S48.10), $n$ by the rotation's Noether current (\S23.1); an exchange needs a term the action does not contain. Nor
could the vacuum in a halo's own volume supply it: the mean density inside $r_{200}$ is $292\,\rho_\Lambda$, so the vacuum there holds $0.34\%$
of the halo's mass, and the dark density near the Sun, $0.4\ \mathrm{GeV\,cm^{-3}}$, is $1.2\times10^5\,\rho_\Lambda$. The excitation was set
once, at the beginning, as the integration constant of \S23.1, and gravity has gathered it onto baryonic structure since.

\sD\ Because each state is separately conserved, the expansion is that of the resonance budget,
$H^2=H_0^2(\Omega_\Lambda+\Omega_ma^{-3}+\Omega_ra^{-4})$: the decomposition moves no distance, growth rate or parameter of Appendix~D.2; what the dilation does to growth in the unbound web is
\S53.9. \sS\ Gravity
measures only the total dark stress-energy \cite{Kunz2009}, and a single Lagrange-multiplier field that unifies the two components was
proposed in \cite{LimSawickiVikman2010}; here the multiplier carries no potential, and the vacuum is the horizon's, tied to the dust by the one
constant $X_0=\Lambda/3$. \sM\ Fits that allow an exchange find at most a mild one: with DESI DR2 the simplest coupling is preferred at
slightly more than $1\sigma$, with no strong evidence for it \cite{PanIDE2025}; a non-parametric reconstruction finds a coupling that reverses
sign with time, at about $2\sigma$ at either end, and fits the data as well as evolving dark energy does \cite{LiZhang2025}. The prediction of
no exchange therefore stands or falls with the constant $w=-1$ of the vacuum.

\subsection*{53.4\quad The dilation}
\sD\ Lensing and dynamics infer mass with the flat relation between field and source, Poisson's equation: a field $g$ at radius $r$ is
read as the mass $gr^2/G$. The proposal's dilation is the ratio of the inferred mass to the baryonic mass, and the excess is what is called
dark matter:
\begin{equation}
\mathcal S(r)\equiv\frac{M_{\rm inf}(<r)}{M_b(<r)}=\frac{g}{g_{\rm bar}},\qquad M_{\rm inf}-M_b=(\mathcal S-1)\,M_b .
\end{equation}
The geometry does not respond flatly. In a relaxed system its response is the gravity law, $\mu\,g=g_{\rm bar}$ exactly in spherical symmetry
(\S22.4), and in the form whose exponent runs with the acceleration (eq.~\eqref{eq:torridity-dynamic}) the response is a cosine:
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\mathcal S=\frac{1}{\mu}=\frac{1}{\cos_n\theta},\qquad \tan\theta=\frac{a_0}{g},\qquad \cos_n\theta\equiv\big(1+\tan^n\theta\big)^{-1/n},\qquad
g^2=g_{\rm bar}\,\big(g^n+a_0^n\big)^{1/n},}
\label{eq:dilation}
\end{equation}
exact at every acceleration, with $n=n(z)$ the dynamic exponent of \S22.9. Take the local field $g$ and the horizon's acceleration $a_0$ (\S49.7)
as the two components of one field, tilted by $\theta$ from the local direction. Projecting the measured field through the tilt gives the
baryons' field, $g_{\rm bar}=g\cos_n\theta$; the flat inference takes no tilt and reads the whole measured field as the baryons', so the mass it
infers is dilated by the secant. The last identity says the measured field is the geometric mean of the baryons' field and the whole field. In the Euclidean plane, $n=2$, the cosine is the ordinary one, $\mathcal S=\sqrt{1+a_0^2/g^2}=1/\cos\theta$ exactly: the proposal's form,
whose values $2$, $3.86$ and $11.5$ at $\theta=60^\circ$, $75^\circ$ and $85^\circ$ are the dilation where the field has fallen to $0.58$, $0.27$ and
$0.087\,a_0$. (The proposal's first table attached these angles to the heights $R/2$, $0.87R$ and $0.98R$ of a sliced sphere, where $1/\cos$ is
$1.15$, $2.03$ and $5.03$.) The cascade sets the exponent between $1.01$ and $1.10$ across galaxies (\S22.9), close to the $\ell^1$ plane,
$\mathcal S\simeq1+a_0/g$; at those three tilts the dilation is $2.67$, $4.67$ and $12.4$. A fixed exponent near the Euclidean one is too sharp
at the transition: the rotation curves put $2\pi/3$ at $\Delta\chi^2/S^2=397$ (\S22.6).

\emph{Smallest at the centre, growing toward the edge.} Where $g\gg a_0$, $\theta\to0$ and $\mathcal S\to1$: Newton, with nothing inferred beyond
the baryons. Where $g\ll a_0$, around an isolated mass whose field is $\sqrt{GM_ba_0}/r$,
\begin{equation}
\mathcal S\simeq\frac{a_0}{g}=\frac{r}{\ell},\qquad \ell=\sqrt{\frac{GM_b}{a_0}},
\label{eq:dilation-deep}
\end{equation}
the radius in units of the coupling length of \S49.7. Around $10^{11}M_\odot$ of baryons, $\ell=11.0$ kpc and the dilation is $2$ at $16$ kpc, $4$ at
$39$ kpc and $11$ at $116$ kpc---the range the proposal gave---and $91$ at $1$ Mpc. The boost of \S22.9, $3.67$, $1.58$, $1.067$ and $1.019$ at
$g_{\rm bar}=0.1$, $1$, $10$ and $30\,a_0$, is the dilation.

\emph{The tilt is the horizon's.} $a_0=(\sqrt3/\pi^2)\,cH$ (\S22.5), and at the present $(2/3\pi)\,c^2\sqrt{\Lambda/3}$ (\S36.13). Without a horizon
$a_0=0$, $\theta=0$ and $\mathcal S=1$ at every radius: baryons alone, nothing to call dark matter. The dilation is the effect of dark energy,
the geometry, on the lensing of the baryons---the proposal's statement, derived.

\emph{It varies.} Through each system's field, $\theta$ rising outward; through the exponent, $n(z)$; through capture,
$\mathcal S=1/[1-\Gamma(1-\mu)]$ (eq.~\eqref{eq:mond-complete}), which is $1$ for the Solar System and wide binaries, which have captured none
(\S47.2); and through the relaxation state (\S47.6): a relaxed galaxy carries the dilation of the threshold law, an unrelaxed cluster its
gathered share (\S53.6). \sM\ Weak lensing extends the radial acceleration relation by two decades of baryonic acceleration, in agreement with the
MOND-type law, yet early- and late-type galaxies of the same stellar mass differ by at least $6\sigma$ \cite{Brouwer2021}, which no
function of the acceleration alone can give; clusters depart from the galactic relation \cite{Eckert2022}, one CLASH analysis finding the
deep slope at a scale seventeen times higher \cite{Tian2020}. \sP\ The dilation differs between systems at the same acceleration as their
capture and relaxation differ (register row 82).

\emph{What carries it.} \sD\ In general relativity light bends only by energy and momentum, and spacetime's excitation carries them, as a
gravitational wave does, without any matter. The dilation looks like mass and is no matter: in a relaxed system the excited geometry is
distributed exactly as the phantom density of the gravity law (Theorem~\ref{thm:boundlaw}),
$\rho_\chi=\rho_{\rm ph}=(4\pi G)^{-1}\nabla\cdot[(1-\mu)\nabla\Phi]$---the mass the flat inference sees and no matter supplies. Because the
completion lenses by the dynamical potential (\S46.4), the dilation of lensing and the dilation of the rotation curves are one.

\emph{How far it grows.} \sO\ If the dilation draws only on the excitation bound to a system, it stops where that is spent, at the reservoir
radius~\eqref{eq:reservoir}: $59$ kpc around $10^{11}M_\odot$ holding its cosmic share. \sM\ Weak lensing finds the circular velocities of
isolated galaxies flat with no clear decline to $1$ Mpc \cite{Mistele2024}, a dilation near $90$ there. Either an isolated galaxy binds $30$--$100$
times its baryons in excited spacetime, or the dilation is not drawn from the bound store; the data so far favour a dilation that keeps
growing (register row 28).

\subsection*{53.5\quad The dilation on the sky}
\sD\ Lensing sees the dilation through a second one of the same form: the slant of each shell to the line of sight. For a spherical
distribution
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\Sigma(b)=2\int_b^\infty\rho(r)\,S_r(b)\,dr,\qquad S_r(b)=\frac{r}{\sqrt{r^2-b^2}}=\frac{1}{\cos\psi},}
\label{eq:abel-dilation}
\end{equation}
with $\psi$ the angle between the line of sight and the normal of the shell of radius $r$ where it crosses: each crossing piles the shell's
surface into a smaller patch of sky, $1$ at the centre of the shell's disc and rising to the limb. For a thin shell of surface density $\sigma$
this is the proposal's $\kappa=(\Sigma_{\rm surf}/\Sigma_{\rm crit})\,S$ for each crossing, and $\kappa(b)=2\sigma S_R(b)/\Sigma_{\rm crit}$ for the
two; a shell of thickness $\Delta$ caps the rise near $S\simeq\sqrt{2R/\Delta}$, the tangent chord $\sqrt{2R\Delta}$ over the thickness. Over the
sky each crossing's mean is $2$, so a closed shell's mean surface density is $4\sigma$: Cauchy's ratio of a convex surface to its shadow,
$4\pi R^2/\pi R^2$, which the proposal's first form took as the mean dilation; the deficit-$\pi$ cone, half-apex $30^\circ$, has the uniform slant
$1/\sin30^\circ=2$ and, with its base, the ratio $3$. Sliced in parallel planes a sphere's slant averages $\pi/2$ over the heights, and its zones
are $4/\pi$ of their cylinders. The four is the framework's own: a ball is the union of cones of height $r$ from its centre to its surface,
$V_s=\tfrac13rA_s$, and the cone of \S3.12 stands on the shadow, $V_c=\tfrac13r\,\pi r^2$, so the four-cone closure $V_s=4V_c$, $\MH=4M_{\rm cone}$, is
Cauchy's ratio. Exchanging the order of integration in~\eqref{eq:abel-dilation},
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\int_0^\infty\Sigma(b)\,2\pi b\,db=\int_0^\infty4\pi r^2\rho(r)\,dr=M,}
\label{eq:proj-theorem}
\end{equation}
since $\int_0^r2\pi b\cdot2S_r(b)\,db=4\pi r^2$: the sky's slant redistributes toward the limbs and keeps the total, so the excess lensing
measures is the field's dilation, carried onto the sky intact. In an aperture the cylinder takes in the outer shells, by $\pi/2$ for an
isothermal profile (eq.~\eqref{eq:dwarf-profile}) and $1.3$--$1.9$ for an NFW halo between $r_s/2$ and $4r_s$. Nor is the excess one number: it is
$6.35$ for the universe as a whole, $1+\Omega_c/\Omega_b$, reached at $R_{200}$ in clusters, and growing without a fixed mean in galaxies.

At edges the sky's slant sharpens what the field has made. The excited geometry is geodesic and forms the caustics of cold matter; at the
splashback radius, where the infalling streams turn round, its density drops sharply \cite{MoreDiemerKravtsov2015}, and in projection the slant
turns that edge into the steepening measured around clusters \cite{Chang2018}. \sP\ The splashback of cold matter, with no self-interaction
to blur it (register row 81).

\subsection*{53.6\quad The Bullet Cluster}
\sM\ The lensing peaks of 1E\,0657$-$56 lie within $1\sigma$ of the galaxies' light and $8\sigma$ from the gas \cite{Clowe2006}. The gas is the
dominant baryonic component in every aperture, $29$--$48$ times the stellar mass at the gas peaks; at the subcluster the visible mass at the
gas peak is $1.8$ times that at the brightest galaxy, yet $\kappa=0.20\pm0.05$ at the galaxy and $0.02\pm0.06$ at the gas. \sD\ A dilation
computed from the baryons present would follow the gas. The dilation follows the excited geometry that carries it, and that is collisionless:
with $X=X_0$ constant its flow is geodesic, $u^\nu\nabla_\nu u_\mu=\nabla_\mu(\partial\theta\cdot\partial\theta)/2X_0=0$; it is pressureless (\S23.1),
and the constraint contains no interaction, so it scatters only gravitationally, $\sigma/m=0$. A cluster, about four crossing times old,
holds its infall configuration (\S47.6), so a merger is the passage of two excited geometries through each other with their galaxies,
while the gas is shocked and held back. The galaxies sit where the dilation is large and the gas where it is small, as the proposal had it. A
system that holds its cosmic share is dilated by
\begin{equation}
\frac{M_{\rm tot}}{M_b}=1+\frac{\Omega_c}{\Omega_b}=6.35,\qquad f_b=0.158,
\label{eq:cluster-share}
\end{equation}
with the derived baryon ratio (\S25.7). \sM\ In the X-COP clusters the baryon fraction reaches the cosmic value at $R_{200}$ \cite{Eckert2022},
and in the Bullet Cluster the dark-plus-galaxy convergence is about seven times the gas's \cite{Clowe2006}. \sP\ Zero self-interaction:
eleven double-radio-relic mergers give $\sigma/m<0.63\ \mathrm{cm^2\,g^{-1}}$ ($95\%$) \cite{Jee2026}; zero offsets between the excess and the
galaxies in mergers. \sD\ The collision is worked in \S47.9: each excited geometry stays on its own galaxies and its mass is the cosmic share of
its own pre-merger baryons, the gas it has since lost included; the Bullet Cluster separates GRUT and particle dark matter from a law computed
on the baryons present, and does not separate them from each other. Register rows 79--80 and 89--91.

\subsection*{53.7\quad The dilation field}
\sD\ The proposal's second form writes the dilation as a field, $S=e^{-\psi}$, with $S\simeq1-2\Phi/c^2$ in the weak field. That is the
refractive index of the geometry. With $\Phi=\Psi$ (\S46.4) light obeys Fermat's principle in the optical metric $d\ell_{\rm opt}=S\,d\ell$: $S$
dilates the measured path, delays the light by $\int(S-1)\,d\ell/c$ (the Shapiro delay) and bends it by $\nabla_\perp\!\int\ln S\,d\ell$. It is the
conformal factor of the optical metric, not of spacetime, as the proposal says; what it records is spacetime's dilation along the light path: in the
weak field time is dilated by $1-\Phi/c^2$ and space by $1-\Phi/c^2$, and light, crossing both, is slowed by their product,
$S\simeq1-2\Phi/c^2$---half from time and half from space, the two halves that double the bending of light over Newton's. Hence the names:
$S$ is the dilation field, and $\mathcal S$, its gradient against the baryons', the dilation, $S$ for the scale a dilation changes. Its value differs from one by $2|\Phi|/c^2$: a few parts per
million in galaxies and $10^{-5}$--$10^{-4}$ in clusters. Even the logarithmic potential of the deep regime adds only $2v_{\rm flat}^2/c^2\approx10^{-6}$
per $e$-fold of radius. Its gradient carries the field, $\nabla\ln S=2\mathbf g/c^2$, and the mass lensing infers inside $r$ is
$c^2r^2|\partial_r\ln S|/2G$. So the dilation of \S53.4 is a ratio of gradients,
\begin{equation}
\mathcal S=\frac{\partial_r\ln S}{\partial_r\ln S_b},
\label{eq:dilation-gradient}
\end{equation}
where $S_b$ is the index the baryons alone would make. The excess that lensing measures is the part of the path's dilation that the baryons do
not make.

\emph{The field equation.} \sD\ In these variables the gravity law of \S22.4 reads
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\nabla\cdot\Big[\mu\Big(\frac{c^2|\nabla\ln S|}{2a_c}\Big)\,\nabla\ln S\Big]=-\frac{8\pi G}{c^2}\,\rho_b,\qquad
R_{a_0}\,|\nabla\ln S|=\frac{g}{a_0},\qquad R_{a_0}=\frac{c^2}{2a_0},}
\label{eq:dilation-field}
\end{equation}
with $\mu\to1-\Gamma(1-\mu)$ under capture (eq.~\eqref{eq:mond-complete}). $R_{a_0}=3.91\times10^{26}$ m is the radius of the horizon whose surface
gravity is $a_0$ (\S49.7). Where the path's dilation changes by more than one $e$-fold per $R_{a_0}$, the geometry is stiff and the equation is
Poisson's: the proposal's $(\nabla^2-\mu_{\rm Y}^2)\ln S=-(8\pi G/c^2)\rho$ at $S\simeq1$, with its screening scale written $\mu_{\rm Y}$. Where it changes more slowly, the geometry softens and
dilates further. The proposal's self-coupling, the $S$ on the right of its equation, therefore acts through the gradient. The value of $\ln S$
is a few parts per million throughout a galaxy and cannot switch anything, while its gradient in units of $1/R_{a_0}$ runs over decades.

Outside a source the proposal's solution, $S=\exp[(2GM_S/c^2)\,e^{-\mu_{\rm Y}r}/r]$, is exact for a point source; an extended source multiplies
$M_S$ by its Yukawa form factor, which is $1$ for $\mu_{\rm Y}R\ll1$. Inside, Gauss fixes the gradient, $\partial_r\ln S=-2GM_S(<r)/c^2r^2$, while the value
includes the shells outside $r$. So $\ln S=2GM_S(<r)/c^2r$ holds at the surface and beyond, not within. \sD\ The screening scale has one value
available in the rotation reading, whose quanta carry no other scale (\S36.14): the horizon's, $\mu_{\rm Y}=H_\infty/c=1/R_\infty$. The dilation is cut off at $R_\infty=5.4$ Gpc, and inside $1$ Mpc the
screening changes $\kappa=\Sigma_{\rm eff}/\Sigma_{\rm crit}$ by less than $10^{-7}$. \sM\ Lensing profiles flat to $1$ Mpc \cite{Mistele2024} rule out any
screening length below the Mpc scale. \sP\ The lensing excess has no exponential cut inside the horizon (register row 83).

\emph{The switch.} \sD\ The proposal's coupling, $\alpha(\rho)=\alpha_0(\rho/\rho_c)^n/[1+(\rho/\rho_c)^n]$, turns the dilation on in dense systems and
off in diffuse ones. The framework's switch is the capture fraction of \S47.2. A system of radius $R$ in a field of dispersion $\sigma$ binds the
fraction $\Gamma=\operatorname{erf}X-(2X/\sqrt\pi)\,e^{-X^2}$ of the excited geometry streaming past it, with $X^2=\rho/\rho_c$. As a function of its mean
density this is a switch of the same kind,
\begin{equation}
\Gamma\simeq\frac{4}{3\sqrt\pi}\Big(\frac{\rho}{\rho_c}\Big)^{3/2}\ \ (\rho\ll\rho_c),\qquad \Gamma\to1\ \ (\rho\gg\rho_c),\qquad
\rho_c=\frac{3\sigma^2}{4\pi GR^2},
\label{eq:capture-switch}
\end{equation}
with $n=3/2$ and $\alpha_0=1$ derived. $\rho_c$ is not a constant of nature: it is the density at which the system's escape speed matches the
speed of the geometry streaming past it. A galaxy forming in the cold cosmic field has $\rho_c\simeq0$ and captures fully; the Sun, at its MOND
radius in the Galaxy's field, has $\rho/\rho_c=4.5\times10^{-6}$ and $\Gamma=7\times10^{-9}$ (\S47.2). The switch gates the excess, not the
baryons. The lensing density is $\rho_b+\Gamma\rho_{\rm ph}$ (eq.~\eqref{eq:complete1}), so a system that has captured nothing lenses as its
baryons, $S=S_b$, never as nothing.

\emph{The second form's numbers.} \sD\ In its galaxy estimate the exponential is $1+2G\alpha_0M/c^2R=1+O(10^{-6})$. So
$\langle\alpha S\rangle_{\rm gal}=\alpha_0$, and the whole excess would be $\alpha_0$. A constant $\alpha_0$ at high density is shared by the laboratory, the
Sun and the stars, and is absorbed into the measured $G$. A coupling that rises with density puts the excess where densities are highest, in
galaxy centres and the Solar System, and removes it where it is largest, in the outskirts and in low-surface-brightness galaxies
\cite{McGaugh2016,Lelli2016}. $\alpha\to0$ would switch off the gas's own gravity, which general relativity does not allow. And a shock
compresses gas rather than thinning it. What the second form's \S7 asks of $\alpha$---galaxies lensing, gas not---the collisionless excited
geometry gives (\S53.6).

\subsection*{53.8\quad Scales and the background}
\sD\ The proposal's last part sets three conditions. Each scale is the coarse-grained output of the scales below it and the fixed input of
those above. An intermediate scale $B$ is fixed jointly by two outer scales $A\cong C$, as neither their product nor their union. And the
background is the solution of its own consistency condition. The framework meets each.

The background's consistency condition is the closure law~\eqref{eq:closure}. Every energy inside the horizon is a defect on it, and summing
them returns the horizon, $\sum_ir_{s,i}=r$: coarse-graining the contents gives the background, $\langle g^{(k-dk)}\rangle=g^{(k+dk)}|_{\rm bg}$. The
background in turn sets, through $a_0$, the dilation of everything inside it (\S53.4). It is above and below at once, with no external stage.

The rule that does not change with scale is the spinning-arc rule, $\omega R=c$. The final horizon, every black hole and every particle's sphere
obey it (\S36.9--36.12), so it is the fixed point of the coarse-graining. The ladder of \S50 runs from the Hubble mass down to the Hubble
quantum, and its two readings cross at the Planck energy, its fixed point (\S50.4).

The intermediate scale needs both outer ones, as exact geometric means. In each, the two outer quantities are of one kind and the middle is
their midpoint on the logarithmic scale $k$:
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\ell_P^2=\tfrac12\,r_s\lambda_C,\qquad \ell^2=\frac{GM}{a_0}=r_s\,R_{a_0},\qquad g^2=g_{\rm bar}\,a_0\ \ (\text{deep regime}).}
\label{eq:geometric-means}
\end{equation}
At the bottom, the Planck length lies between the gravitational and quantum lengths of every mass (\S50.4). At the galactic scale, the coupling
length lies between the system's own horizon, its Schwarzschild radius, and the horizon whose surface gravity is $a_0$: $7{,}200$ AU for the
Sun, between $3$ km and $13$ Gpc, and $8.5$ kpc for the Milky Way's baryons. In the field, the measured acceleration lies between the
baryons' field and the horizon's; at every acceleration this is $g^2=g_{\rm bar}(g^n+a_0^n)^{1/n}$~\eqref{eq:dilation}. The dark-energy scale sits
the same way between the Planck and Hubble energies (\S50.4). \sO\ A sheaf or renormalization-group formulation of the coarse-graining, which
the proposal notes a rigorous treatment needs, is not supplied.

\subsection*{53.9\quad The dilation on the unbound web}
\sD\ \emph{The background cannot move.} A homogeneous universe has no gradient of the dilation field, $\nabla\ln S=0$, so $\mathcal S=1$: the
Friedmann equation, the sound horizon and the acoustic angle are those of \S25.11, and the CMB's $H_0=67.35\pm0.06$ stands. A tension between
that number and one measured inside structure is another matter, because the dilation acts inside structure. The distance ladder measures the
expansion of our own neighbourhood, $0.023<z<0.15$. Continuity of a spherical region of mean contrast $\delta$, growing at the rate
$f=d\ln\delta/d\ln a$, gives its edge the peculiar velocity $-\tfrac13fH\delta R$, so the expansion rate measured inside it is
\begin{equation}
\frac{H_{\rm loc}}{H}=1-\frac{f\,\delta}{3}.
\label{eq:local-H}
\end{equation}
The local distance network's $73.50\pm0.81$ \cite{H0DN2026} needs $f|\delta|=0.275\pm0.036$; SH0ES's $73.04\pm1.04$ \cite{Riess2022} needs
$0.254\pm0.047$. Undilated growth gives $f=0.528$ today, which needs a region $52\%$ underdense; whether the dilation can supply the rest is
settled by the data at the end of this section.

\sD\ \emph{The web is in the deep regime.} The rms peculiar field of a sphere of radius $R$ is $\tfrac12\Omega_mH_0^2\sigma(R)R$: between
$0.0010$ and $0.0024\,a_0$ for every radius from $12$ to $500$ Mpc, and $0.0079\,a_0$ at a point, summed over the linear modes. Its baryons'
share, $0.0012\,a_0$, the law dilates $28.9$-fold: $4.5$ times the cosmic dilation $1+\Omega_c/\Omega_b$ that the share already supplies, the most
the web's growth can take. The field cannot say when that boost begins or where it stops. It is $0.0019\,a_0$ at $8\,h^{-1}$ Mpc and
$0.0014\,a_0$ at $300$ Mpc, so a dilation set by it alone boosts the one nearly as much as the other: a boost $B$ of the growth equation on every
scale raises $S_8$ to $0.89$ for $B=3.6$ from $z=0.372$, $6.4\sigma$ above DES Y6 with both errors counted \cite{DESY6}, to $1.00$ for $B=8.1$ from
the same epoch and to $1.42$ for $B=5.6$ from $z=1$. That form is excluded.

\sD\ \emph{The dilation keeps a clock's causes.} A clock runs at $\Theta=N\sqrt{1-v^2/c^2}$ with $N^2=1-r_s/R-R^2/r_\Lambda^2$
(\S43B.2--43B.3): it is slowed by the well it sits in, by its motion, and by the horizon. In a bound system the dilation is the horizon's tilt
of the light path, set by what the system captures (\S53.4, \S53.7); on the unbound web it is the horizon's setting of the clocks, and each
cause fixes one part of it.

\emph{When.} The horizon sets the gradient of a region's clocks only beyond the region's balance surface, where its mean density is below
$2\rho_\Lambda$ (\S43B.3, \S53.2); inside it, the region's own mass does. A system inside its balance surface carries the dilation of \S53.4, set
by what it captures; the unbound web takes its dilation from the horizon, and only where the horizon sets its clocks. For the universe as a
whole that is $q=0$, $z=0.630$, the onset of acceleration; for a region $20\%$ underdense today, $z=0.713$; for one $80\%$ overdense today,
$z=0.378$. At recombination the matter density was $3\times10^8$ times $2\rho_\Lambda$, and the baryons moved with the photon fluid at $0.45c$:
off on every scale, and off through the growth of the first structures, which ran on the clocks of matter.

\emph{Where.} A region holds the medium where its well slows time more than the medium's motion does: bound when $v^2<2|\Phi|$, the capture
fraction $\Gamma$ of eq.~\eqref{eq:capture-switch}. The well is the potential, which carries the scale the field lacks: the rms $|\Phi|/c^2$ is
$0.9\times10^{-6}$ at $12$ Mpc, $8.6\times10^{-6}$ at $100$ Mpc and $1.7\times10^{-5}$ at $300$ Mpc; per mode it is flat for wavelengths beyond the
equality horizon, $1/k_{\rm eq}=96$ Mpc, and falls inside it, where the radiation era erased the wells. Only peculiar motion counts, since
comoving clocks keep cosmic time, and all of it counts: the flows the dilation itself drives, and the motion inside haloes, which hold $77\%$ of
the mass with a mass-weighted dispersion of $264\ \mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$ on the framework's own spectrum. The law acts on the field the matter
moves in, the sum of the modes, so with $\mu=1/28.9$ the dilation exceeds the share where $\Gamma(1-\mu)>\Omega_c/\Omega_m$, $\Gamma>0.873$.
Dispersion and cut close on themselves: the dilated flows give $417$ and the haloes $264\ \mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$, $\sigma_{1D}=494\ \mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$,
and the dilation acts above $68$ Mpc ($k<0.025\ \mathrm{Mpc^{-1}}$), with boosts $1.6$, $3.4$ and $4.2$ at $k=0.02$, $0.01$ and
$0.005\ \mathrm{Mpc^{-1}}$ and growth rates today $0.63$, $0.95$ and $1.06$ against $0.53$. No input remains. The previous form of this section
cut at $29$ to $80$ Mpc according to which motions it counted; the medium's motion is all of them.

\sD\ \emph{Light crosses the web undilated.} The horizon's part of the lapse, $R^2/r_\Lambda^2$, is de Sitter's, and de Sitter spacetime
is conformally flat: the horizon dilates clocks and rulers by one factor. A dilation it sets on the web is therefore a conformal factor of
spacetime, where the captured dilation of a bound system is the index of the optical metric (\S53.7). The index is the ratio of space to time,
so the common factor cancels in it:
\begin{equation}
\tilde g_{\mu\nu}=\Omega^2g_{\mu\nu}:\qquad \frac{\tilde g_{ij}}{|\tilde g_{00}|}=\frac{g_{ij}}{|g_{00}|},\qquad
\frac{\tilde\nu_{\rm o}}{\tilde\nu_{\rm e}}=\frac{\Omega_{\rm e}}{\Omega_{\rm o}}\,\frac{\nu_{\rm o}}{\nu_{\rm e}},\qquad
\ddot{\mathbf x}=-\nabla\Phi-c^2\nabla\ln\Omega .
\label{eq:web-conformal}
\end{equation}
Light's paths are unchanged, a photon picks up the factor only at its two ends, and a massive body, whose clock it slows, feels the dilated
pull, $c^2\nabla\ln\Omega=(B-1)\nabla\Phi$ on the held modes. Light sees only the matter the dilation has moved. Had it crossed the web dilated,
the late integrated Sachs--Wolfe signal would rise $4{,}200$-fold at $\ell=10$ and $3{,}000$-fold at $\ell=20$, its correlation with galaxies reversing
sign: the CMB excludes that outright. As derived, the large-scale potentials, fed by growth that now keeps pace with the expansion, decay less,
and the late signal falls to $0.28$, $0.41$, $0.54$ and $0.71$ of the background's at $\ell=2$, $4$, $10$ and $20$, taking $222$, $97$ and
$25\ \mu\mathrm{K}^2$ from the power at $\ell=2$, $4$ and $10$, in the sense of the low large-angle power the CMB shows \cite{Planck2018}. Its
correlation with galaxies keeps $0.4$--$1.0$ of the background's at $\ell=4$--$40$ for tracers near $z=1$, which carry most of the $4\sigma$
detection \cite{Planck2015ISW}; for tracers at $z=0.3$--$0.5$ it falls to $0$--$0.6$ at $\ell=10$--$20$ and reverses at $\ell=4$, and from $\ell=40$ it is unchanged.

\sM\ \emph{What the data say.} The added growth lives above $68$ Mpc and below $z=0.63$. $\sigma_8$ rises by $0.16\%$ today and by
$0.02$--$0.08\%$ at $z=0.2$--$0.4$, the epochs of clusters and lensing; cosmic shear changes by $0.3\%$ at $\ell=30$ and not at all from
$\ell=100$. DES Y6, $0.789\pm0.012$ \cite{DESY6}, therefore stays $2.8\sigma$ below, the difference between that survey and the CMB that the
dilation did not make, and KiDS-Legacy, $0.815^{+0.016}_{-0.021}$ \cite{KiDSLegacy}, $0.9\sigma$. CMB lensing rises by $3.5\%$ at $L=10$ and $0.8\%$
at $L=20$ and is unchanged from $L=40$: the amplitude GRUT predicts at its own neutrino mass, $S_8^{\rm CMBL}=\sigma_8(\Omega_m/0.3)^{1/4}=0.822$,
is the $0.825^{+0.015}_{-0.013}$ that ACT, SPT and Planck measure together \cite{Qu2026}. No lensing excess is left for a negative neutrino mass
to absorb \cite{GreenMeyers2024}: $\sum m_\nu=59.34$ meV lies inside ACT DR6's $82$ meV \cite{ACTDR6ext} and DESI DR2's $64$ meV with the CMB
\cite{DESInu2025} ($95\%$), and the floor's remaining pull rests on DESI's BAO matter density, $0.2975\pm0.0086$ against $0.316$ ($2.2\sigma$),
which the dilation does not touch. The flows are where the dilation shows. Cosmicflows-4 bulk flows of $387\pm28$ and
$419\pm36\ \mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$ within $150$ and $200\,h^{-1}$ Mpc, which $\Lambda$CDM expects at $139$ and $120$ and allows with probabilities
below $0.03\%$ and $0.003\%$ \cite{Watkins2023}, fall at the $78$th and $90$th percentiles of the dilated web's distribution (rms $321$ and
$288\ \mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$); the $628\pm82\ \mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$ dipole of the $120$--$160\,h^{-1}$ Mpc shell, $3.4\sigma$ in $\Lambda$CDM
\cite{NusserTully2026}, at its $98$th.

\sP\ \emph{The Hubble rate.} The framework has one expansion rate, the background's $67.35$, and the dilation leaves it, and the
sound horizon behind it, alone. Nor can the dilation make the ladder read more. A clock or a ruler is shifted by $|\Phi|/c^2\lesssim10^{-5}$,
so a $9\%$ excess is not a dilation of clocks; and a local void cannot carry it either. The K-band luminosity density is
$(3.09\pm0.19)\times10^8\,h_{70}L_\odot\,\mathrm{Mpc^{-3}}$ in the samples below $z=0.07$ and $5.40\pm0.27$ beyond $z=0.09$ \cite{Keenan2013}, a
ratio $1.75\pm0.14$; read as a matter deficit and inverted with the framework's own outflow, which lowers the apparent density by
$H_{\rm loc}/H$, it is a void $37\%$ deep today ($25\%$ when its clocks were set, at $z=0.79$) whose interior would expand at $74.2\pm0.6$
($74.6$ with the full-sky 2M++ sample alone, $72.9$ for the authors' summary ratio of $1.5$, $72.2$ undilated). The supernovae decide
it. The deficit ends by $z\approx0.09$, the ladder's supernovae run to $z=0.15$, and beyond the edge the rate would fall back by $8.3\%$;
the supernova Hubble diagram shows no change of $H_0$ across $0.023<z<0.15$ to $0.60\%$ \cite{Kenworthy2019}, which bounds the deficit
below $27\%$ at $5\sigma$ with undilated growth and near half that with the dilated. What the dilation does change is the scatter of the
local rate: its flows give a $300$ Mpc sphere an rms offset of $0.88\%$, against $0.41\%$ in $\Lambda$CDM. The prediction is therefore that
distance ladders calibrated alike converge on $67.3$ within that scatter. The 2026 network lies $6.1\sigma$ away ($7.6\sigma$ bare), SH0ES
$4.8\sigma$, the CCHP red-giant and carbon-star ladder \cite{CCHP2025} $1.5\sigma$, and the lens time delays, $71.6^{+3.9}_{-3.3}$
(TDCOSMO, as quoted in \cite{H0DN2026}), $1.3\sigma$. The ladder calibrates the supernovae against Cepheids and red giants and the CMB
against the sound horizon; JWST distances to the supernova hosts settled at the network's value at $5\sigma$ would falsify the
framework's background (register row 84).

\sH\ \emph{Status.} GRUT as built gates the excess with the capture of a store, which is zero in the unbound web, and the web grows
undilated. The proposal's reading---the dilation as the geometry rather than a store, the branch the lensing of isolated galaxies already
favours (\S53.4, register row 28)---lets the web take it from the horizon on a clock's terms: from the onset of acceleration, above $68$ Mpc at
the fixed point of its own flows, on clocks and not on light~\eqref{eq:web-conformal}. So read, it keeps $S_8$, cosmic shear and CMB lensing at
the background's values, lowers the late Sachs--Wolfe signal, and carries the Cosmicflows-4 bulk flows that $\Lambda$CDM does not; it leaves the
ladder's $H_0$ at the background's $67.35$, on which the framework predicts the ladders converge (register rows 84--85). Unaffected either way: the TM1 solar angle and $a_0(z)$.

\subsection*{53.10\quad What this changes}
\sD\ Derived:
\begin{itemize}\itemsep1pt
\item dark energy as spacetime unexcited and the cosmic share as spacetime excited, the spin's quanta in two states~\eqref{eq:dark-one}, with
no separate dark matter;
\item the equation of state $w=-1+\Theta$, the vacuum's own density as the threshold, and equality at $z=0.372$~\eqref{eq:wdark};
\item the dilation $\mathcal S=1/\cos_n\theta$, $\tan\theta=a_0/g$~\eqref{eq:dilation}, $1$ at the centre and $r/\ell$ toward the
edge~\eqref{eq:dilation-deep}, set by the horizon and varying with capture and relaxation, its excess carried by the excited geometry as the
phantom density;
\item the dilation field as the refractive index of the optical metric, whose gradient, measured against $1/R_{a_0}$, carries the
law~\eqref{eq:dilation-field};
\item the proposal's switch as the capture fraction~\eqref{eq:capture-switch};
\item the sky's slant~\eqref{eq:abel-dilation}, which keeps the total~\eqref{eq:proj-theorem};
\item the Bullet Cluster's offset from the collisionless excitation;
\item the background as its own consistency condition, the closure law, with the intermediate scales as geometric means of the outer
ones~\eqref{eq:geometric-means};
\item the background unmoved by the dilation, and in the proposal's reading the web dilated on a clock's terms---from the onset of
acceleration, above $68$ Mpc at the fixed point of its own flows, on clocks and not on light~\eqref{eq:web-conformal}; the exclusion by $S_8$ of
a dilation set on the web by the field alone, and by the integrated Sachs--Wolfe effect of one that light would see.
\end{itemize}
\sO\ How far the dilation grows is open (register row 28). Unchanged: the background expansion, which has no gradient and so no local dilation, and
with it the CMB's $H_0$, the TM1 solar angle and the acceleration scale at $z\simeq1$. Changed in the proposal's reading (\S53.9): the
growth of the web above $68$ Mpc after $z=0.63$, which carries the Cosmicflows-4 bulk flows and lowers the late Sachs--Wolfe signal, with $S_8$,
cosmic shear and CMB lensing at the background's values, so that the neutrino-mass floor's pull rests on the BAO matter density alone. Not
changed: the distance-ladder $H_0$, since the supernova Hubble diagram rules out a local void as the ladder's rate (register rows 84--85).
Corrected:
\begin{itemize}\itemsep1pt
\item the first form of this section, which held the tensions of the October 2026 confrontation unchanged: only the background is (\S53.9);
\item its second form, which read the ladder's $H_0$ as the rate inside a local void, $71.8\pm0.8$, against the supernova Hubble diagram, and
cut the web's dilation at $29$--$80$ Mpc according to the motions it counted;
\item the heights of the proposal's first table, and its single mean dilation $\langle\mathcal S\rangle=4$, which is Cauchy's ratio, not the
measured excess;
\item in the second form, the excess assigned to the value of the dilation field rather than its gradient;
\item a coupling that rises with density rather than with capture, and multiplies the baryons rather than the excess;
\item the enclosed-mass form of $\ln S$ inside a source.
\end{itemize}
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{3.4cm}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{11.6cm}@{}}
\toprule
Dark energy & spacetime, the geometry itself: unexcited, $T_{\mu\nu}=-\rho_\Lambda g_{\mu\nu}$, $w=-1$; excited and bound to baryonic structure,
$T_{\mu\nu}=\rho_\chi u_\mu u_\nu$, $w=0$ \\
